**2. Grammar: pointing back to the subject of the verb.** (возвратный)

3. *Myself* and *herself* are *reflexive* pronouns.
4. In he hurt himself, the verb takes a *reflexive* pronoun.

---

### reform-minded

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfɔːrmˌmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *rih-FORM-myn-did*
**Русский:** настроенный на реформы, реформаторский
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reform-minded*, *most reform-minded*
**Form:** compound — noun + *mind* + *-ed*, like *open-minded*

Wanting to change a system to make it work better.

1. A *reform-minded* mayor took office last year.
2. The new board is *more reform-minded* than the old one.
3. *Reform-minded* teachers pushed for a longer school day.

---

### reformed

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfɔːrmd/ &middot; *rih-FORMD*
**Русский:** исправившийся; реформированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *reform*

Reformed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. No longer doing the bad thing you used to do.** (исправившийся, завязавший)

1. He is a *reformed* smoker and now hates the smell.
2. She is a *reformed* shoplifter who counsels teenagers.

**2. Of a system or law: changed so that it works better.** (реформированный, преобразованный)

3. The *reformed* tax code takes effect in January.
4. Critics say the *reformed* system is no fairer than the old one.

---

### reformist

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfɔːrmɪst/ &middot; *rih-FOR-mist*
**Русский:** реформистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ist from *reform*
**Contrast:** The same word is also a noun: *a reformist* is a person who wants reform.

Supporting gradual change to a political or religious system.

1. The *reformist* wing of the party won the vote.
2. He ran on a *reformist* platform and lost badly.
3. *Reformist* candidates did well in the big cities.

---

### refreshed

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfrɛʃt/ &middot; *rih-FRESHT*
**Русский:** отдохнувший, посвежевший
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more refreshed*, *most refreshed*
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Contrast:** *refreshed* is how you feel; *refreshing* is what causes it: *a refreshing nap leaves you refreshed*.

Rested and full of energy again.

1. I woke up *refreshed* for the first time in weeks.
2. A twenty-minute nap left her feeling *refreshed*.
3. He came back from vacation *more refreshed* than anyone expected.

---

### refreshing

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfrɛʃɪŋ/ &middot; *rih-FRESH-ing*
**Русский:** освежающий; приятно необычный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more refreshing*, *most refreshing*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive, after *it*: *It is refreshing to hear that*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

Refreshing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cool and pleasant, making you feel less hot or tired.** (освежающий, бодрящий)

1. A glass of cold lemonade is *refreshing* on a day like this.
2. We took a *refreshing* swim in the lake before dinner.

**2. Pleasantly different from the usual, in a way you welcome.** (приятно необычный, отрадный)

3. It is *refreshing* to hear a politician admit a mistake.
4. Her honesty was *more refreshing* than anything else that week.

---

### refrigerated

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfrɪdʒəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *rih-FRIJ-uh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** охлаждённый, холодильный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *refrigerate*

Kept cold so that it does not spoil.

1. The vaccine has to travel in a *refrigerated* truck.
2. Keep the dough *refrigerated* until you are ready to bake.
3. The store added a whole *refrigerated* section for drinks.

---

### regal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈriːɡəl/ &middot; *REE-guhl*
**Русский:** царственный, величественный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regal*, *most regal*
**Form:** from Latin *rex* — king
**Contrast:** *regal* describes a manner that reminds you of a king (*a regal wave*); *royal* says the person or thing really belongs to a king's family (*the royal family*).

Grand and dignified, like a king or queen.

1. She has a *regal* bearing that people notice right away.
2. The old hotel looks *more regal* from the street than it does inside.
3. He gave a *regal* nod and walked past us.

---

### regimented

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛdʒɪmɛntɪd/ &middot; *REJ-ih-men-tid*
**Русский:** строго регламентированный, зарегулированный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regimented*, *most regimented*
**Form:** -ed from *regiment*, an army unit
**Opposite:** *relaxed*

Organized by strict rules and a strict schedule, often too strict.

1. Her day is highly *regimented*, down to the minute.
2. He grew up in a *regimented* household with a chore chart.
3. Training camp is *more regimented* than the regular season.

---

### regional

**Pronunciation:** /ˈriːdʒənəl/ &middot; *REE-juh-nuhl*
**Русский:** региональный, местный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *region*
**Opposite:** *national*

Belonging to one part of a country rather than the whole of it.

1. The chain started as a small *regional* grocery in Ohio.
2. She won the *regional* championship in March.
3. There are *regional* differences in how people say that word.

---

### registered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛdʒɪstərd/ &middot; *REJ-ih-sturd*
**Русский:** зарегистрированный, дипломированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *register*

Officially listed, licensed or recorded.

1. She is a *registered* nurse at the county hospital.
2. You have to be a *registered* voter to take part.
3. The name is a *registered* trademark.

---

### regressive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈɡrɛsɪv/ &middot; *rih-GRES-iv*
**Русский:** регрессивный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regressive*, *most regressive*
**Opposite:** *progressive*

Regressive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a tax or fee: taking a bigger share from people who earn less.** (регрессивный (о налоге))

1. A sales tax is *regressive* because it hits poor families hardest.
2. Critics call the flat fee deeply *regressive*.

**2. Going back to an earlier and worse state.** (регрессивный, отбрасывающий назад)

3. The policy is a *regressive* step for women's rights.
4. His behavior got *more regressive* the longer he stayed home.

---

### regretful

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈɡrɛtfəl/ &middot; *rih-GRET-fuhl*
**Русский:** сожалеющий, полный сожаления
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regretful*, *most regretful*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ful from *regret*
**Contrast:** *regretful* is how a person feels; *regrettable* is a thing that is a shame: *a regretful apology* for *a regrettable mistake*.

Feeling sorry about something you did or missed.

1. She sounded *regretful* about missing the wedding.
2. He gave a *regretful* shrug and closed the door.
3. The older he gets, the *more regretful* he seems about that year.

---

### regrettable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈɡrɛtəbəl/ &middot; *rih-GRET-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** прискорбный, досадный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regrettable*, *most regrettable*
**Takes:** *that*-clause, after *it*
**Form:** -able from *regret*

Bad enough that people are sorry it happened.

1. The whole incident was *regrettable* but nobody's fault.
2. He made a *regrettable* remark about her weight.
3. It is *regrettable* that no one spoke up sooner, and even *more regrettable* that no one has apologized.

---

### regular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛɡjələr/ &middot; *REG-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** регулярный; обычный; постоянный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regular*, *most regular*
**Opposite:** *irregular*

Regular has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Happening again and again at even intervals.** (регулярный, равномерный)

1. Take the medicine at *regular* intervals, about six hours apart.
2. Her heartbeat is strong and *regular* now.
3. His visits have become *more regular* since he retired.

**2. Normal or standard, not special and not large.** (обычный, обыкновенный)

4. I will just have a *regular* coffee, nothing fancy.
5. It is not a holiday — it is a *regular* workday.

**3. Coming to the same place or doing the same thing often.** (постоянный (о клиенте, посетителе))

6. He is one of our *regular* customers.
7. The bar has a few *regular* patrons who come every night.

**4. Grammar: following the normal pattern.** (правильный (о глаголе, форме))

8. *Walk* is a *regular* verb — its past is *walked*.
9. Most English plurals are *regular* and simply add an s.

---

### regulated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛɡjəleɪtɪd/ &middot; *REG-yuh-lay-tid*
**Русский:** регулируемый, контролируемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more regulated*, *most regulated*
**Form:** -ed from *regulate*
**Opposite:** *unregulated*

Controlled by official rules.

1. Banking is a heavily *regulated* industry.
2. The market is *more regulated* now than it was in 2005.
3. Water quality is closely *regulated* by the state.

---

### regulatory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛɡjələˌtɔːri/ &middot; *REG-yuh-luh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** регулирующий, нормативный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with official rules and the bodies that enforce them.

1. The merger still faces a *regulatory* review.
2. *Regulatory* agencies have been slow to act.
3. There are a few *regulatory* hurdles to clear first.

---

### reigning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈreɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *RAY-ning*
**Русский:** правящий; действующий (о чемпионе)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *reign*
**Contrast:** Spelled from *reign* (to rule), not *raining* or *reining*, though all three sound the same.

Holding the title or the throne right now.

1. She is the *reigning* world champion.
2. The *reigning* monarch opened parliament on Tuesday.
3. He is the *reigning* king of late-night television.

---

### reinforced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌriːɪnˈfɔːrst/ &middot; *ree-in-FORST*
**Русский:** укреплённый, армированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *reinforce*

Made stronger by adding extra material or support.

1. The bunker is built of *reinforced* concrete.
2. The backpack has *reinforced* straps at the shoulders.
3. The door was *reinforced* with steel plates.

---

### rejected

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈdʒɛktɪd/ &middot; *rih-JEK-tid*
**Русский:** отвергнутый, отклонённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *reject*
**Opposite:** *accepted*

Rejected has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Turned down or refused.** (отклонённый, отвергнутый)

1. He keeps a folder of *rejected* drafts.
2. *Rejected* applicants can appeal within thirty days.

**2. Feeling unwanted by other people.** (отвергнутый, никому не нужный)

3. She felt hurt and *rejected* after that phone call.
4. Kids who feel *rejected* by classmates often act out.

---

### relational

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈleɪʃənəl/ &middot; *rih-LAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** реляционный; касающийся отношений между людьми
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *relation*

Relational has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Computing: storing data in tables that are linked to each other.** (реляционный)

1. The app runs on a *relational* database.
2. SQL was built for *relational* data.

**2. Having to do with how people get along with each other.** (касающийся отношений между людьми)

3. The therapist focuses on *relational* problems rather than symptoms.
4. Teaching has a strong *relational* side that no test measures.

---

### relative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛlətɪv/ &middot; *REL-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** относительный, сравнительный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Contrast:** The same word is also a common noun: *a relative* is a member of your family.

Relative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Judged by comparison with something else, not on its own.** (относительный, сравнительный)

1. After the storm, the *relative* calm felt strange.
2. They live in *relative* comfort compared with their neighbors.
3. What counts as expensive is *relative* to how much you earn.

**2. Grammar: joining a clause to the noun it describes.** (относительный (о местоимении, придаточном))

4. *Who*, *which* and *that* introduce a *relative* clause.
5. *Whose* is a *relative* pronoun.

---

### relentless

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈlɛntləs/ &middot; *rih-LENT-luhs*
**Русский:** неослабный, безжалостный, неустанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more relentless*, *most relentless*
**Form:** -less from *relent* — to soften or give in

Relentless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Never stopping or easing off.** (непрекращающийся, неослабный)

1. The *relentless* heat lasted the whole of August.
2. The pressure on young athletes is *relentless*.

**2. Refusing to give up or to show any mercy.** (неумолимый, безжалостный)

3. She is *relentless* once she decides she wants something.
4. Their defense got *more relentless* in the fourth quarter.

---

### relevant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛləvənt/ &middot; *REL-uh-vuhnt*
**Русский:** относящийся к делу, уместный, актуальный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more relevant*, *most relevant*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [relevant to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#relevant-to)
**Opposite:** *irrelevant*

Connected with the subject at hand, and therefore worth considering.

1. Please bring any *relevant* documents to the meeting.
2. That question is not *relevant* to what we are discussing.
3. The book feels *more relevant* now than when it came out.

---

### reliable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈlaɪəbəl/ &middot; *rih-LY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** надёжный; достоверный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reliable*, *most reliable*
**Form:** -able from *rely*
**Opposite:** *unreliable*

Reliable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to be trusted to do what is needed, every time.** (надёжный)

1. She is the *most reliable* person on the team.
2. We need a *more reliable* car for that drive.

**2. Of information: likely to be correct.** (достоверный)

3. Is that a *reliable* source?
4. We have *reliable* figures for only three of the years.

---

### reliant

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈlaɪənt/ &middot; *rih-LY-uhnt*
**Русский:** зависящий, полагающийся (на кого-то)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — before a noun only inside compounds like *self-reliant*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reliant*, *most reliant*
**Takes:** *on* or *upon* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [reliant on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#reliant-on)
**Form:** -ant from *rely*

Needing someone or something in order to manage.

1. The town is heavily *reliant* on summer tourism.
2. He has become *more reliant* on his phone than he would like.
3. Many older residents are *reliant* on the one bus line.

---

### relieved

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈliːvd/ &middot; *rih-LEEVD*
**Русский:** испытывающий облегчение
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more relieved*, *most relieved*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive; *that*-clause; *about* or *at* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels

Glad because something you were worried about is over or did not happen.

1. I am so *relieved* that you are okay.
2. She gave a *relieved* laugh when the test came back clean.
3. He looked *more relieved* than happy when the verdict was read.

---

### religious

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈlɪdʒəs/ &middot; *rih-LIJ-uhs*
**Русский:** религиозный, верующий; церковный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more religious*, *most religious*
**Opposite:** *secular*

Religious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Believing in a religion and practicing it.** (религиозный, верующий)

1. Her family is very *religious* and goes to church twice a week.
2. He became *more religious* after his illness.

**2. Having to do with religion.** (религиозный, церковный)

3. The museum has a whole room of *religious* art.
4. *Religious* holidays are marked on the school calendar.

---

### remaining

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmeɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *rih-MAY-ning*
**Русский:** оставшийся
**Type:** participial; also quantity
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *remain*

Still there after the rest has gone or been used.

1. Divide the *remaining* money between the two of you.
2. The *remaining* guests stayed to help clean up.
3. We will deal with the *remaining* questions tomorrow.

---

### remarkable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/ &middot; *rih-MAR-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** замечательный, поразительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more remarkable*, *most remarkable*
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *that*-clause, after *it*
**Form:** -able from *remark* — worth remarking on

Unusual enough that people notice and talk about it.

1. She has made a *remarkable* recovery.
2. The house is *remarkable* for its stained-glass windows.
3. What is *most remarkable* is how calm he stayed.

---

### remedial

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmiːdiəl/ &middot; *rih-MEE-dee-uhl*
**Русский:** коррекционный, дополнительный; исправительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *remedy*

Remedial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a class: teaching the basics to students who are behind.** (коррекционный, дополнительный (о занятиях))

1. He had to take *remedial* math his freshman year.
2. The district pays for *remedial* reading classes.

**2. Meant to put right something that has gone wrong.** (исправительный, устраняющий недостатки)

3. The city took *remedial* action after the flood.
4. *Remedial* work on the bridge starts Monday.

---

### reminiscent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛməˈnɪsənt/ &middot; *rem-uh-NIS-uhnt*
**Русский:** напоминающий (о чём-то)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be* or *seem*, almost always with *of*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reminiscent*, *most reminiscent*
**Takes:** *of* + noun

Making you think of someone or something else.

1. The smell was *reminiscent* of my grandmother's kitchen.
2. Her singing is *reminiscent* of early Joni Mitchell.
3. The lobby is *more reminiscent* of a hotel than an office.

---

### remiss

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmɪs/ &middot; *rih-MIS*
**Русский:** небрежный, упустивший что-то сделать
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, often in *It would be remiss of me not to...*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + *-ing*; *of* + person

Formal: careless about a duty you should have carried out.

1. It would be *remiss* of me not to thank the volunteers.
2. The inspector was *remiss* in not checking the wiring.
3. I would be *remiss* if I did not mention her help.

---

### remorseful

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmɔːrsfəl/ &middot; *rih-MORS-fuhl*
**Русский:** раскаивающийся, полный раскаяния
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more remorseful*, *most remorseful*
**Takes:** *about* or *for* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ful from *remorse*
**Opposite:** *unrepentant*

Feeling deep guilt about something bad you did.

1. He seemed genuinely *remorseful* about what he had done.
2. The judge saw no sign of a *remorseful* defendant.
3. She grew *more remorseful* as the details came out in court.

---

### remorseless

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmɔːrsləs/ &middot; *rih-MORS-luhs*
**Русский:** безжалостный; неумолимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more remorseless*, *most remorseless*
**Form:** -less from *remorse*

Remorseless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Feeling no guilt at all about the harm you cause.** (безжалостный, без угрызений совести)

1. The killer was cold and *remorseless* on the stand.
2. He seemed *more remorseless* with every question the prosecutor asked.

**2. Never letting up.** (неумолимый, непрекращающийся)

3. The *remorseless* rain went on for five days.
4. There is a *remorseless* logic to her argument.

---

### remote

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmoʊt/ &middot; *rih-MOHT*
**Русский:** отдалённый, удалённый; ничтожный (о шансе); отчуждённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *remoter* or *more remote*, *remotest* or *most remote*
**Takes:** *from* + noun

Remote has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Far away from towns and from other people.** (отдалённый, глухой)

1. They live on a *remote* island off the coast of Maine.
2. The cabin was *more remote* than the photos suggested.

**2. Done from home or from another city instead of at the office.** (удалённый, дистанционный)

3. The job is fully *remote*, with two trips a year.
4. She has had a *remote* position since 2020.

**3. Of a chance or possibility: very small.** (ничтожный, крайне малый)

5. There is not the *remotest* chance he will agree.
6. The risk is *remote* but it is real.

**4. Distant and hard to get close to, in manner.** (отчуждённый, холодный)

7. He can seem *remote* until you know him well.
8. Her father was a *remote* figure throughout her childhood.

---

### remote-controlled

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˌmoʊtkənˈtroʊld/ &middot; *rih-moht-kuhn-TROHLD*
**Русский:** с дистанционным управлением
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle

Operated from a distance by a handheld device or a signal.

1. He got a *remote-controlled* car for his birthday.
2. The gate is *remote-controlled*, so stay in the truck.
3. They filmed the wedding with a *remote-controlled* drone.

---

### removable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmuːvəbəl/ &middot; *rih-MOO-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** съёмный, отсоединяемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *remove*
**Opposite:** *permanent*

Made so it can be taken off and put back.

1. The stroller has a *removable* seat cover you can wash.
2. The stickers are *removable* and will not damage the paint.
3. It comes with a *removable* liner for cold weather.

---

### removed

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈmuːvd/ &middot; *rih-MOOVD*
**Русский:** далёкий (от чего-то); (о родстве) через поколение
**Type:** participial
**Position:** after the noun — in *a first cousin once removed*; otherwise after a verb, in *far removed from*
**Comparison:** not gradable — graded with *far*: *far removed*
**Takes:** *from* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *remove*
**Contrast:** In *a cousin once removed*, *removed* means one generation apart — nobody was taken away.

Removed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very different or very distant from something.** (далёкий, не имеющий ничего общего)

1. His account of the evening is far *removed* from what actually happened.
2. Life in the capital is far *removed* from village life.

**2. Of a cousin: one or two generations apart from you.** ((о двоюродном родстве) через поколение)

3. She is my first cousin once *removed*.
4. He is a cousin twice *removed* on my mother's side.

---

### renegade

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛnɪɡeɪd/ &middot; *REN-ih-gayd*
**Русский:** мятежный, отступнический, самовольный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The same word is also a noun: *a renegade* is the person who breaks away.

Having left the group you belonged to and turned against it.

1. A *renegade* group broke away from the main party.
2. The movie is about a *renegade* cop who ignores every rule.
3. *Renegade* soldiers seized the radio station at dawn.

---

### renewable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈnuːəbəl/ &middot; *rih-NOO-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** возобновляемый; продлеваемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *renew*
**Opposite:** *nonrenewable*

Renewable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of energy or resources: never running out, because nature replaces it.** (возобновляемый)

1. The state gets a third of its power from *renewable* sources.
2. Wind and solar are *renewable*; coal is not.

**2. Able to be extended for another period of time.** (продлеваемый)

3. The contract is for one year, *renewable* for two more.
4. Your permit is *renewable* online in about ten minutes.

---

### renewed

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈnuːd/ &middot; *rih-NOOD*
**Русский:** возобновлённый, с новой силой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *renew*

Starting again, and often stronger than before.

1. There is *renewed* interest in the case after the documentary.
2. She came back from the break with *renewed* energy.
3. *Renewed* fighting broke out near the border.

---

### renowned

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈnaʊnd/ &middot; *rih-NOWND*
**Русский:** знаменитый, прославленный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more renowned*, *most renowned*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing*; *as* + noun

Famous and widely admired for something good.

1. He is one of the *most renowned* heart surgeons in the country.
2. The region is *renowned* for its wine.
3. She is *renowned* as a teacher as much as a performer.

---

### rent-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛntˈfriː/ &middot; *rent-FREE*
**Русский:** бесплатный (о жилье), без арендной платы
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-free*, like *sugar-free*, *duty-free*
**Contrast:** The same form works as an adverb after a verb — *They lived there rent-free* — and that use is not an adjective.

Costing nothing to live in or use.

1. They offered him a *rent-free* apartment above the shop.
2. The space is *rent-free* for the first six months.
3. The deal includes a *rent-free* period while the building is fixed up.

---

### rental

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛntəl/ &middot; *REN-tuhl*
**Русский:** арендованный, прокатный, съёмный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *rent*
**Contrast:** The same word is also a noun: *a rental* is the car or apartment you have rented.

Rented out to people for money, or having to do with renting.

1. We picked up the *rental* car at the airport.
2. *Rental* prices downtown have gone up again.
3. He owns three *rental* properties across the river.

---

### repeatable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpiːtəbəl/ &middot; *rih-PEE-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** воспроизводимый, повторяемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *repeat*

Repeatable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to be done again with the same result.** (воспроизводимый, повторяемый)

1. An experiment has to be *repeatable* before anyone believes it.
2. We want a *repeatable* process, not one lucky quarter.

**2. Polite enough to say out loud.** (такой, что можно повторить вслух)

3. His reaction was not *repeatable* in front of the kids.
4. She said something about the referee that is not *repeatable* here.

---

### repeated

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpiːtɪd/ &middot; *rih-PEE-tid*
**Русский:** неоднократный, повторный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *repeat*
**Contrast:** *repeated* just says it happened more than once; *repetitive* adds that it is boringly the same.

Happening more than once, again and again.

1. *Repeated* warnings had no effect on him.
2. She made *repeated* attempts to reach the office by phone.
3. He was fired after *repeated* absences.

---

### repellent

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpɛlənt/ &middot; *rih-PEL-uhnt*
**Русский:** отталкивающий, отвратительный; отталкивающий (о материале)
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repellent*, *most repellent*
**Takes:** *to* + person
**Form:** -ent from *repel*
**Contrast:** The same word is also a noun: *insect repellent* is the spray you put on your skin.

Repellent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So unpleasant that it makes you want to keep away.** (отталкивающий, отвратительный)

1. The whole idea is *repellent* to me.
2. He found the man's politics *more repellent* than his manners.

**2. Keeping a substance off, usually in a compound.** (отталкивающий (воду, грязь))

3. The jacket is made of a water-*repellent* fabric.
4. The couch is treated with a stain-*repellent* coating.

---

### repentant

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpɛntənt/ &middot; *rih-PEN-tuhnt*
**Русский:** раскаивающийся, кающийся
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repentant*, *most repentant*
**Form:** -ant from *repent*
**Opposite:** *unrepentant*

Sorry for what you did and wanting to make it right.

1. He did not sound the least bit *repentant*.
2. A *repentant* thief returned the money anonymously.
3. She seemed *more repentant* once she saw the damage.

---

### repetitive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpɛtətɪv/ &middot; *rih-PET-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** однообразный, повторяющийся
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repetitive*, *most repetitive*

Doing or saying the same thing over and over, usually in a boring way.

1. The work is *repetitive* but it pays well.
2. Typing all day can cause *repetitive* strain injuries.
3. The second album is *more repetitive* than the first.

---

### replaceable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpleɪsəbəl/ &middot; *rih-PLAY-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** заменимый, сменный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *replace*
**Opposite:** *irreplaceable*

Able to be swapped for a new one of the same kind.

1. The battery is *replaceable*, so do not throw the whole thing out.
2. Everyone here is *replaceable* except the founder.
3. The filters are cheap and easily *replaceable*.

---

### replete

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpliːt/ &middot; *rih-PLEET*
**Русский:** изобилующий, полный (чего-то)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, almost always with *with*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [replete with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#replete-with)
**Contrast:** Formal and written. In speech Americans say *full of*.

Filled with something, or having plenty of it.

1. The report is *replete* with errors.
2. His speech was *replete* with quotations from the Bible.
3. The museum is *replete* with treasures from the region.

---

### reported

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpɔːrtɪd/ &middot; *ri-POR-tid*
**Русский:** сообщаемый; заявленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun (*the reported cases*); after a verb it is the passive verb, not the adjective
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *report*

Said by the news or by officials to be true, though not proved.

1. The *reported* number of cases keeps going up.
2. The *reported* cause of the fire was a space heater.
3. *Reported* crime is down, but that isn't the same as crime being down.

---

### reprehensible

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛprɪˈhɛnsəbəl/ &middot; *rep-ri-HEN-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** предосудительный, заслуживающий осуждения
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reprehensible*, *most reprehensible*

So wrong that it deserves strong criticism.

1. His behavior at the meeting was *reprehensible*.
2. Lying to the families was a *reprehensible* thing to do.
3. Nothing is more *reprehensible* than blaming the victim.

---

### representational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛprɪzɛnˈteɪʃənəl/ &middot; *rep-ri-zen-TAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** изобразительный, фигуративный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *abstract*

In art, showing people and things as they really look.

1. He likes *representational* art better than abstract painting.
2. Her early work was strictly *representational*.
3. The museum has a whole floor of *representational* painting.

---

### representative

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛprɪˈzɛntətɪv/ &middot; *rep-ri-ZEN-tuh-tiv*
**Русский:** типичный, репрезентативный; представительный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more representative*, *most representative*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [representative of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#representative-of)
**Contrast:** Also a common noun: *a sales representative*, *our representative in Congress*.

Representative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Typical of a larger group, so that it shows what the whole group is like.** (типичный, репрезентативный)

1. The sample was not *representative* of the country as a whole.
2. Is this painting *representative* of his later work?
3. We need a more *representative* group of testers.

**2. Working through elected officials who act for the people.** (представительный (о демократии, власти))

4. The United States has a *representative* democracy.
5. *Representative* government was the whole point of the revolution.

---

### repressed

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈprɛst/ &middot; *ri-PREST*
**Русский:** подавленный; закомплексованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repressed*, *most repressed*
**Form:** -ed from *repress*

Repressed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pushed down and never expressed, of a feeling or a memory.** (подавленный, вытесненный)

1. Years of *repressed* anger came out at once.
2. He talked about his *repressed* memories of the accident.

**2. Unable to show feelings or enjoy things, of a person.** (зажатый, закомплексованный)

3. She describes her family as cold and *repressed*.
4. He was the most *repressed* person I have ever worked with.

---

### repressive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈprɛsɪv/ &middot; *ri-PRES-iv*
**Русский:** репрессивный, деспотичный
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repressive*, *most repressive*
**Form:** -ive from *repress*

Using force and strict control to keep people from being free.

1. They fled a *repressive* regime.
2. The new law is the most *repressive* in decades.
3. The government grew more *repressive* after the protests.

---

### reproducible

**Pronunciation:** /ˌriːprəˈduːsəbəl/ &middot; *ree-pruh-DOO-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** воспроизводимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** re- + *producible*, from *reproduce*

Able to be done again by someone else with the same result.

1. The results have to be *reproducible* by other labs.
2. Their findings turned out not to be *reproducible*.
3. Science depends on *reproducible* experiments.

---

### reproductive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌriːprəˈdʌktɪv/ &middot; *ree-pruh-DUHK-tiv*
**Русский:** репродуктивный, детородный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with producing babies or young.

1. The clinic focuses on *reproductive* health.
2. *Reproductive* rights were on the ballot this year.
3. Salmon return to the river in the *reproductive* season.

---

### reptilian

**Pronunciation:** /rɛpˈtɪliən/ &middot; *rep-TIL-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** рептильный, змеиный; холодный и неприятный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reptilian*, *most reptilian*

Reptilian has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to, or looking like, snakes, lizards and other reptiles.** (рептильный, змеиный)

1. The museum has a whole *reptilian* collection.
2. Its skin is dry and *reptilian* to the touch.

**2. Cold and unpleasant, in the way a snake seems to people.** (змеиный, холодный и скользкий)

3. His smile was *more reptilian* than friendly.
4. She played the lawyer with a *reptilian* calm.

---

### Republican

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpʌblɪkən/ &middot; *ri-PUHB-li-kuhn*
**Русский:** республиканский (о партии США); республиканский (о форме правления)
**Type:** classifying; also origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Republican* when it means 'typical of the party'
**Contrast:** Capital *Republican* is the American political party; small-letter *republican* is about a republic as a form of government. Capitalized, it is also a noun: *She's a Republican*.

Republican has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to or supporting the Republican Party of the United States.** (республиканский (о партии США))

1. She comes from a *Republican* family in Texas.
2. The state has been solidly *Republican* for decades.

**2. Having to do with a republic, a country run by elected leaders and not a king.** (республиканский (о форме правления))

3. France has a long *republican* tradition.
4. They fought for a *republican* form of government.

---

### repugnant

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpʌɡnənt/ &middot; *ri-PUHG-nuhnt*
**Русский:** отвратительный, противный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repugnant*, *most repugnant*
**Takes:** *to* + noun (*repugnant to me*)
**Preposition patterns:** [repugnant to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#repugnant-to)

So wrong or unpleasant that it disgusts you.

1. The very idea was *repugnant* to her.
2. He called the comments morally *repugnant*.
3. Nothing is more *repugnant* to most Americans than censorship.

---

### repulsive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈpʌlsɪv/ &middot; *ri-PUHL-siv*
**Русский:** отвратительный, отталкивающий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more repulsive*, *most repulsive*
**Opposite:** *attractive*

Extremely unpleasant to look at, smell or think about.

1. The smell in the fridge was *repulsive*.
2. I find his politics *repulsive*.
3. She plays a *repulsive* villain who somehow makes you laugh.

---

### reputable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛpjətəbəl/ &middot; *REP-yuh-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** уважаемый, с хорошей репутацией
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reputable*, *most reputable*
**Opposite:** *disreputable*
**Contrast:** The stress is on the first syllable, REP-yuh-tuh-buhl, even though *reputation* stresses the third.

Known to be honest and to do good work.

1. Buy the part from a *reputable* dealer.
2. The story came from a *reputable* source.
3. Theirs is the most *reputable* firm in the city.

---

### required

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈkwaɪərd/ &middot; *ri-KWY-urd*
**Русский:** требуемый, обязательный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *of* + person; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *require*
**Opposite:** *optional*

That you must do or have, because a rule says so.

1. *Required* courses take up most of the first year.
2. A signature is *required* on both forms.
3. All visitors are *required* to sign in at the desk.

---

### requisite

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛkwɪzɪt/ &middot; *REK-wi-zit*
**Русский:** необходимый, требуемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Formal. Everyday English says *required* or *necessary*. Do not confuse it with the noun *prerequisite*, the course or condition you need first.

Needed for a particular purpose.

1. She has the *requisite* experience for the job.
2. He filled out the *requisite* forms and waited.
3. The team lacks the *requisite* funding.

---

### resentful

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzɛntfəl/ &middot; *ri-ZENT-fuhl*
**Русский:** обиженный, полный негодования
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resentful*, *most resentful*
**Takes:** *of* or *about* + noun or *-ing*; *toward* + person; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ful from *resent*

Angry and bitter because you feel you have been treated unfairly.

1. He was *resentful* of his brother's success.
2. She grew *resentful* about doing all the housework.
3. Years later he is still *resentful* that nobody thanked him.

---

### reserved

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzɜːrvd/ &middot; *ri-ZURVD*
**Русский:** сдержанный, замкнутый; забронированный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reserved*, *most reserved*
**Takes:** *for* + noun, in the 'kept' sense
**Opposite:** *outgoing*

Reserved has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not saying much about your feelings; quiet with people you don't know.** (сдержанный, замкнутый)

1. Her father is a *reserved* man who rarely says much.
2. He seems cold at first, but he is just *reserved*.

**2. Kept for one particular person or purpose.** (забронированный, зарезервированный)

3. This table is *reserved*.
4. The front row is *reserved* for family.

---

### resident

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛzɪdənt/ &middot; *REZ-i-duhnt*
**Русский:** постоянно проживающий; штатный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly; formal writing also puts it after the noun: *a citizen resident abroad*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Much more common as a noun: *local residents*, *a hospital resident* (a young doctor in training).

Resident has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Living in a place, especially permanently.** (постоянно проживающий)

1. The *resident* population doubles every summer.
2. Students *resident* in the state pay less tuition.

**2. Working at one place all the time instead of visiting.** (штатный, работающий на месте)

3. She is our *resident* expert on tax law.
4. The hotel has a *resident* chef and a *resident* cat.

---

### residential

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛzɪˈdɛnʃəl/ &middot; *rez-i-DEN-shuhl*
**Русский:** жилой; с проживанием
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Residential has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made up of homes rather than stores, offices or factories.** (жилой)

1. They live in a quiet *residential* neighborhood.
2. The whole block is *residential*, so there are no shops.
3. *Residential* streets have a 25 mile-an-hour limit.

**2. Where you live on site while you take part in a program.** (с проживанием, стационарный)

4. He spent six weeks in a *residential* treatment program.
5. The college has a *residential* requirement for freshmen.

---

### residual

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzɪdʒuəl/ &middot; *ri-ZIJ-oo-uhl*
**Русский:** остаточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *residue*
**Contrast:** In film and television *residuals* is a noun: the payments an actor gets each time a show is aired again.

Left over after most of something is gone.

1. There is still some *residual* pain in the knee.
2. *Residual* chemicals stayed in the soil for years.
3. He has a *residual* fear of big dogs.

---

### resilient

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzɪliənt/ &middot; *ri-ZIL-ee-uhnt*
**Русский:** стойкий, жизнестойкий; упругий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resilient*, *most resilient*

Resilient has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to recover quickly after trouble or illness.** (стойкий, жизнестойкий)

1. Kids are more *resilient* than we give them credit for.
2. The local economy proved surprisingly *resilient*.

**2. Springing back into shape after being bent or pressed.** (упругий, эластичный)

3. The foam is light and *resilient*.
4. Pick a *resilient* flooring for the kitchen.

---

### resistant

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzɪstənt/ &middot; *ri-ZIS-tuhnt*
**Русский:** устойчивый, стойкий; сопротивляющийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resistant*, *most resistant*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** also the second half of compounds: *heat-resistant*, *water-resistant*

Resistant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not harmed or affected by something.** (устойчивый, стойкий)

1. These bacteria are *resistant* to most antibiotics.
2. Choose a stain-*resistant* fabric for the couch.

**2. Unwilling to accept something and quietly fighting it.** (сопротивляющийся, не принимающий)

3. Older staff were *resistant* to the change.
4. He is *resistant* to any kind of therapy.

---

### resolute

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛzəluːt/ &middot; *REZ-uh-loot*
**Русский:** решительный, твёрдый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resolute*, *most resolute*
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *hesitant*

Firm about what you have decided, and not changing your mind.

1. She was *resolute* in her decision to leave.
2. The mayor stayed *resolute* under a week of pressure.
3. He gave a *resolute* answer: no.

---

### resonant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛzənənt/ &middot; *REZ-uh-nuhnt*
**Русский:** звучный, глубокий; созвучный, находящий отклик
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resonant*, *most resonant*
**Takes:** *with* + noun; *for* or *with* + person, in the 'meaningful' sense

Resonant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Deep, clear and continuing, of a sound or a voice.** (звучный, глубокий)

1. He has a deep, *resonant* voice.
2. The old bell gave a *resonant* hum.

**2. Full of a meaning that stays with people.** (созвучный, находящий отклик)

3. The film is *resonant* with memories of childhood.
4. Her speech was especially *resonant* for young voters.

---

### resounding

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈzaʊndɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-ZOWN-ding*
**Русский:** полный, безоговорочный; оглушительный
**Type:** emphasizing; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Resounding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Complete and unmistakable, of a success, a defeat or an answer.** (полный, безоговорочный)

1. The show was a *resounding* success.
2. The proposal met with a *resounding* no.

**2. Loud and echoing.** (оглушительный, раскатистый)

3. There was a *resounding* crash from the kitchen.
4. The speech got a *resounding* cheer.

---

### resourceful

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈsɔːrsfəl/ &middot; *ri-SORS-fuhl*
**Русский:** находчивый, изобретательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resourceful*, *most resourceful*
**Form:** -ful from *resource*

Good at finding a way to solve problems with whatever you have.

1. She is the most *resourceful* person on the team.
2. *Resourceful* cooks can make dinner out of almost nothing.
3. You have to be *resourceful* when the budget is this small.

---

### respected

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈspɛktɪd/ &middot; *ri-SPEK-tid*
**Русский:** уважаемый
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more respected*, *most respected*
**Form:** -ed from *respect*
**Contrast:** Three words that learners mix up: *respected* — admired by other people; *respectful* — behaving politely toward someone; *respective* — belonging to each one separately.

Admired by other people because of what you do or know.

1. He is a widely *respected* surgeon.
2. The paper is *respected* on both sides of the aisle.
3. She is one of the most *respected* judges in the state.

---

### respective

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈspɛktɪv/ &middot; *ri-SPEK-tiv*
**Русский:** соответствующий (каждый свой)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Not *respectful* (polite) and not *respected* (admired). *Respective* always goes with a plural noun and means 'each one's own'.

Belonging to each of the people or things just named, separately.

1. After the meeting they went back to their *respective* offices.
2. The two companies kept their *respective* names.
3. Each writer spoke about their *respective* field.

---

### respiratory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛspərəˌtɔːri/ &middot; *RES-pur-uh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** дыхательный, респираторный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with breathing and the lungs.

1. He was treated for a *respiratory* infection.
2. Smoke can cause serious *respiratory* problems.
3. The virus attacks the *respiratory* system.

---

### resplendent

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈsplɛndənt/ &middot; *ri-SPLEN-duhnt*
**Русский:** блистательный, великолепный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — most often after a verb or right after the noun: *the general, resplendent in blue*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more resplendent*, *most resplendent*
**Takes:** *in* + clothing; *with* + noun
**Contrast:** Literary. You meet it in writing and in wedding announcements, not in everyday speech.

Looking splendid, bright and colorful.

1. She arrived *resplendent* in a red silk gown.
2. The hall was *resplendent* with flowers.
3. The general stood on the steps, *resplendent* in full uniform.

---

### responsive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈspɑːnsɪv/ &middot; *ri-SPAHN-siv*
**Русский:** отзывчивый, чутко реагирующий; поддающийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more responsive*, *most responsive*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Opposite:** *unresponsive*

Responsive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Reacting quickly and well to what people need or say.** (отзывчивый, чутко реагирующий)

1. The staff was friendly and *responsive*.
2. The mayor has to be more *responsive* to voters.

**2. Reacting well to treatment or to a control.** (поддающийся (лечению); чувствительный (об управлении))

3. The tumor was *responsive* to the new drug.
4. The steering is light and *responsive*.

---

### rested

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛstɪd/ &middot; *RES-tid*
**Русский:** отдохнувший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — before a noun only in the compound *well-rested*: *a well-rested team*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rested*, *most rested*
**Form:** -ed from *rest*; usually *well-rested*

Feeling fresh again because you have slept or relaxed.

1. I felt *rested* for the first time in weeks.
2. You'll play better *rested*.
3. A short nap left her *rested* and ready to work.

---

### restful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛstfəl/ &middot; *REST-fuhl*
**Русский:** спокойный, умиротворяющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restful*, *most restful*
**Form:** -ful from *rest*
**Contrast:** *Restful* describes the thing that gives you rest; *rested* describes the person who got it. *A restful weekend leaves you rested.*

Calm and quiet, so that it helps you relax.

1. We had a *restful* weekend at the lake.
2. The room is painted a *restful* green.
3. Nothing is more *restful* than a quiet morning.

---

### restive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛstɪv/ &middot; *RES-tiv*
**Русский:** беспокойный, нетерпеливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restive*, *most restive*
**Contrast:** Close to *restless*, but *restive* adds impatience with being held back or controlled: a *restive* crowd is about to stop obeying.

Unwilling to keep waiting or to be controlled, and beginning to show it.

1. The crowd grew *restive* as the delay went on.
2. *Restive* lawmakers began to question the plan.
3. The horses were *restive* before the storm.

---

### restless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛstləs/ &middot; *REST-luhs*
**Русский:** беспокойный, неугомонный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restless*, *most restless*
**Form:** -less from *rest*

Restless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unable to stay still or be patient, because you are bored or worried.** (беспокойный, неугомонный)

1. The kids get *restless* on long drives.
2. He was *restless* all evening before the interview.

**2. Broken and without real rest, of a night or of sleep.** (беспокойный (о сне, о ночи))

3. She had a *restless* night.
4. It was a *restless* sleep full of strange dreams.

---

### restorative

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈstɔːrətɪv/ &middot; *ri-STOR-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** восстанавливающий силы; восстановительный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restorative*, *most restorative*

Restorative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making you feel strong and fresh again.** (восстанавливающий силы, целительный)

1. A hot bath is wonderfully *restorative*.
2. She took a *restorative* week off in the mountains.

**2. Aimed at repairing harm rather than punishing.** (восстановительный (о правосудии, лечении))

3. The school uses *restorative* justice instead of suspensions.
4. He does *restorative* dental work.

---

### restrained

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈstreɪnd/ &middot; *ri-STRAYND*
**Русский:** сдержанный, умеренный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restrained*, *most restrained*
**Form:** -ed from *restrain*
**Opposite:** *unrestrained*

Calm and controlled; not extreme or showy.

1. His reaction was surprisingly *restrained*.
2. The design is elegant and *restrained*.
3. She gave a *restrained* smile and said nothing.

---

### restricted

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈstrɪktɪd/ &middot; *ri-STRIK-tid*
**Русский:** ограниченный; с ограниченным доступом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restricted*, *most restricted*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *restrict*

Restricted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Controlled, so that only certain people may enter or use it.** (с ограниченным доступом, закрытый)

1. This is a *restricted* area — you need a badge.
2. The documents are *restricted*.

**2. Limited in amount, range or choice.** (ограниченный)

3. He is on a *restricted* diet after the surgery.
4. The team works with a very *restricted* budget.

---

### restrictive

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈstrɪktɪv/ &middot; *ri-STRIK-tiv*
**Русский:** ограничительный, стесняющий
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more restrictive*, *most restrictive*
**Form:** -ive from *restrict*

Limiting what people can do, usually more than they think is fair.

1. The dress code is too *restrictive*.
2. These are the most *restrictive* voting laws in the state's history.
3. *Restrictive* rules drove the best players away.

---

### resurgent

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈsɜːrdʒənt/ &middot; *ri-SUR-juhnt*
**Русский:** возрождающийся, вновь набирающий силу
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ent — the adjective behind the noun *resurgence*

Becoming strong, popular or common again after a weak period.

1. A *resurgent* auto industry brought jobs back to the city.
2. The team faces a *resurgent* rival on Sunday.
3. Measles is *resurgent* in states with low vaccination rates.

---

### retaliatory

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtæliəˌtɔːri/ &middot; *ri-TAL-ee-uh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** ответный, в порядке возмездия
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ory from *retaliate*

Done to punish someone for something they did to you first.

1. The country launched a *retaliatory* strike.
2. He says the firing was *retaliatory*.
3. *Retaliatory* tariffs hit farmers hardest.

---

### reticent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛtɪsənt/ &middot; *RET-i-suhnt*
**Русский:** сдержанный, неразговорчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reticent*, *most reticent*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Contrast:** *Reticent* is about not speaking; *reluctant* is about not wanting to act. You are *reticent* about your past but *reluctant* to leave.

Not willing to talk about something or to say what you think.

1. He is *reticent* about his years in the army.
2. She was oddly *reticent* during the whole meeting.
3. Witnesses were more *reticent* than the police had expected.

---

### retired

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtaɪərd/ &middot; *ri-TY-urd*
**Русский:** вышедший на пенсию, отставной
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *retire*

No longer working, because you have reached the end of your working life.

1. My neighbor is a *retired* teacher.
2. Both of my parents are *retired* now.
3. He is a *retired* Navy officer who still gets up at five.

---

### retiring

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtaɪərɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-TY-ur-ing*
**Русский:** застенчивый, скромный; уходящий в отставку
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — the 'leaving office' sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more retiring*, *most retiring*

Retiring has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shy, and staying out of the center of attention.** (застенчивый, скромный)

1. He is a *retiring* man who hates parties.
2. She is shy and *retiring* in a big group.

**2. About to leave a job or an office for good.** (уходящий в отставку, уходящий на пенсию)

3. The *retiring* chairman gave a short speech.
4. They threw a party for the *retiring* principal.

---

### retractable

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtræktəbəl/ &middot; *ri-TRAK-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** убирающийся, выдвижной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *retract*

Able to be pulled back in or folded away.

1. The stadium has a *retractable* roof.
2. Cats have *retractable* claws.
3. The blade is *retractable*, which makes it safer to carry.

---

### retro

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛtroʊ/ &middot; *RET-roh*
**Русский:** ретро, в стиле прошлых десятилетий
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more retro*, *most retro*
**Form:** from the prefix *retro-* 'back'

Copying the style of an earlier time on purpose, usually the 1950s to the 1980s.

1. The diner has a *retro* look, right down to the jukebox.
2. Her whole outfit is very *retro*.
3. *Retro* video games are selling again.

---

### retroactive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛtroʊˈæktɪv/ &middot; *ret-roh-AK-tiv*
**Русский:** имеющий обратную силу
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + a date

Taking effect from a date in the past, not only from today.

1. The raise is *retroactive* to January 1.
2. Congress passed a *retroactive* tax change.
3. The new rule is not *retroactive*, so old cases stand.

---

### retrospective

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛtrəˈspɛktɪv/ &middot; *ret-ruh-SPEK-tiv*
**Русский:** ретроспективный, обращённый в прошлое
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a retrospective* is a show of an artist's work from across their whole career.

Looking back at things that happened or were made in the past.

1. The study is *retrospective*, based on old medical records.
2. He took a *retrospective* look at thirty years on the job.
3. The team holds a *retrospective* meeting after every project.

---

### returning

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtɜːrnɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-TUR-ning*
**Русский:** возвращающийся, вернувшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *return*

Coming back after being away, or taking part again after a break.

1. *Returning* students register on Monday.
2. The *returning* champion took the court first.
3. *Returning* soldiers were met at the airport by their families.

---

### reusable

**Pronunciation:** /riːˈjuːzəbəl/ &middot; *ree-YOO-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** многоразовый, пригодный для повторного использования
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** re- + *usable*
**Opposite:** *disposable*

Able to be used again instead of thrown away.

1. Bring a *reusable* bag to the store.
2. The containers are *reusable* and dishwasher safe.
3. We switched to *reusable* water bottles last year.

---

### revealing

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈviːlɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-VEE-ling*
**Русский:** откровенный (об одежде); показательный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more revealing*, *most revealing*
**Form:** -ing from *reveal*

Revealing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Showing a lot of the body, of clothes.** (откровенный, открытый (об одежде))

1. The dress was a little too *revealing* for the office.
2. She wore something less *revealing* to the interview.

**2. Telling you something important that was not obvious before.** (показательный, многое раскрывающий)

3. His answer was very *revealing*.
4. It is the most *revealing* interview she has ever given.

---

### revered

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈvɪrd/ &middot; *ri-VEERD*
**Русский:** почитаемый, глубоко уважаемый
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more revered*, *most revered*
**Takes:** *as* + noun; *by* + person
**Form:** -ed from *revere*

Deeply respected, almost in the way holy things are respected.

1. He is *revered* as the father of the sport.
2. She is a *revered* figure in American poetry.
3. The old cookbook is *revered* by chefs.

---

### reverent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɛvərənt/ &middot; *REV-ur-uhnt*
**Русский:** благоговейный, почтительный
**Type:** emotion; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more reverent*, *most reverent*
**Opposite:** *irreverent*

Showing deep respect, the way people behave in a holy place.

1. The crowd fell into a *reverent* silence.
2. He speaks about his old teacher in *reverent* tones.
3. The movie takes a *reverent* approach to its subject.

---

### reverse

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈvɜːrs/ &middot; *ri-VURS*
**Русский:** обратный, противоположный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*the reverse is true*) and a verb (*reverse the car*). As an adjective it goes only before a noun — for 'the opposite' after a verb, say *the opposite*.

Opposite in direction or order to the usual one.

1. Put the names in *reverse* order.
2. The truck has a *reverse* camera.
3. Critics called the policy *reverse* discrimination.

---

### reversible

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈvɜːrsəbəl/ &middot; *ri-VUR-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** обратимый; двусторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ible from *reverse*
**Opposite:** *irreversible*

Reversible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to be changed back to the way it was.** (обратимый)

1. The damage is *reversible* if you stop now.
2. Most of these side effects are *reversible*.

**2. Able to be worn with either side outward.** (двусторонний)

3. It is a *reversible* jacket — black on one side, red on the other.
4. *Reversible* belts save you buying two.

---

### revised

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈvaɪzd/ &middot; *ri-VYZD*
**Русский:** исправленный, переработанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *revise*
**Contrast:** In American English *revise* means to change a text or a figure. It does not mean 'study for an exam' — that British use is *review* or *study* here.

Changed and corrected from an earlier version.

1. Use the *revised* edition, not the old one.
2. The *revised* figures are much lower.
3. Here is the *revised* schedule for next week.

---

### revolting

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈvoʊltɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-VOHL-ting*
**Русский:** отвратительный, омерзительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more revolting*, *most revolting*
**Contrast:** In this everyday sense it has nothing to do with *revolt* 'to rebel', so *the revolting peasants* is an old English joke.

So unpleasant that it almost makes you feel sick.

1. The smell in the basement was *revolting*.
2. He told a *revolting* joke at dinner.
3. That is the most *revolting* thing I have ever tasted.

---

### revolutionary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɛvəˈluːʃəˌnɛri/ &middot; *rev-uh-LOO-shuh-nair-ee*
**Русский:** революционный, новаторский
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more revolutionary*, *most revolutionary*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a revolutionary* is a person who fights to overthrow a government.

Revolutionary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Completely new and changing the way things are done.** (революционный, новаторский)

1. The first iPhone was a *revolutionary* design.
2. Her way of teaching reading was *revolutionary* at the time.

**2. Having to do with a political revolution.** (революционный (связанный с революцией))

3. *Revolutionary* leaders took over the capital within a week.
4. The group had openly *revolutionary* aims.

---

### rewarding

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈwɔːrdɪŋ/ &middot; *ri-WOR-ding*
**Русский:** приносящий удовлетворение, благодарный (о деле)
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rewarding*, *most rewarding*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive (*rewarding to watch*)
**Form:** -ing from *reward*
**Contrast:** It is about satisfaction, not money: a job can be *rewarding* and still badly paid. For money say *well-paid*.

Giving you the good feeling that the effort was worth it.

1. Teaching is hard work, but it is *rewarding*.
2. It was the most *rewarding* year of my life.
3. Small children are exhausting and *rewarding* to raise.

---

### rhetorical

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈtɔːrɪkəl/ &middot; *ri-TOR-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** риторический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Rhetorical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Asked for effect, not because you want an answer — of a question.** (риторический (о вопросе))

1. Don't answer — it was a *rhetorical* question.
2. He ended with a *rhetorical* question about fairness.

**2. Having to do with the art of speaking and writing persuasively.** (риторический, ораторский)

3. It is a *rhetorical* device, not a real promise.
4. Her *rhetorical* skill won the debate.

---

### rheumatoid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈruːmətɔɪd/ &middot; *ROO-muh-toyd*
**Русский:** ревматоидный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In practice you meet it in one phrase, *rheumatoid arthritis*. The silent *rh-* is simply 'r'.

Of the disease in which the body attacks its own joints and they swell and hurt.

1. She was diagnosed with *rheumatoid* arthritis at thirty.
2. *Rheumatoid* arthritis is an autoimmune disease.
3. He takes a weekly shot for his *rheumatoid* arthritis.

---

### rhyming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈraɪmɪŋ/ &middot; *RY-ming*
**Русский:** рифмующийся, рифмованный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *rhyme*

Ending in the same sound as another word or another line.

1. The poem is written in *rhyming* couplets.
2. Little kids love *rhyming* books.
3. 'Cat' and 'hat' are a *rhyming* pair.

---

### rhythmic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪðmɪk/ &middot; *RITH-mik*
**Русский:** ритмичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rhythmic*, *most rhythmic*
**Form:** -ic from *rhythm*

Having a regular, repeated beat or movement.

1. The *rhythmic* sound of the waves put me to sleep.
2. Her breathing was slow and *rhythmic*.
3. It is a *rhythmic*, danceable song.

---

### rickety

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪkɪti/ &middot; *RIK-i-tee*
**Русский:** шаткий, ветхий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rickety*, *most rickety*

Old, weak and likely to break or fall over.

1. We climbed a *rickety* wooden staircase.
2. The table is *rickety*, so don't lean on it.
3. They crossed on a *rickety* little bridge.

---

### riddled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪdəld/ &middot; *RID-uhld*
**Русский:** изрешечённый; пронизанный, полный (плохого)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, or right after the noun: *a report riddled with errors*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *riddle* 'to make holes in'

Riddled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of holes, especially holes made by bullets.** (изрешечённый)

1. The road sign was *riddled* with bullet holes.
2. They found the old boat *riddled* with worm holes.

**2. Full of something bad, all the way through.** (пронизанный, кишащий)

3. The report was *riddled* with errors.
4. The whole system is *riddled* with corruption.

---

### ridiculous

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈdɪkjələs/ &middot; *ri-DIK-yuh-luhs*
**Русский:** смешной, нелепый, абсурдный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ridiculous*, *most ridiculous*
**Contrast:** *Ridiculous* means laughably bad or unreasonable. For something meant to make you laugh, say *funny*.

So silly or unreasonable that nobody can take it seriously.

1. Twenty dollars for a cup of coffee is *ridiculous*.
2. He looked *ridiculous* in that hat.
3. This is the most *ridiculous* rule in the whole handbook.

---

### rife

**Pronunciation:** /raɪf/ &middot; *RYF*
**Русский:** широко распространённый; изобилующий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — never before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** *Rife* is used only about bad things — rumors, corruption, mistakes — and never goes before a noun.

Very common and everywhere, of something bad; with *with*, full of it.

1. Rumors were *rife* in the days before the vote.
2. Corruption is *rife* in the construction business.
3. The essay is *rife* with spelling mistakes.

---

### rigged

**Pronunciation:** /rɪɡd/ &middot; *RIGD*
**Русский:** сфальсифицированный, подстроенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *rig* 'to fix the result dishonestly'

Dishonestly arranged in advance so that a particular side wins.

1. He claimed the election was *rigged*.
2. It is a *rigged* game — the house always wins.
3. The whole contest looked *rigged* from the start.

---

### right-hand

**Pronunciation:** /ˈraɪthænd/ &middot; *RYT-hand*
**Русский:** правый, находящийся справа; правая рука (о человеке)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *right* + *hand*
**Contrast:** *Right-hand* is about position; *right-handed* is about the hand a person writes with.

Right-hand has 2 distinct senses.

**1. On or toward the right side.** (правый, находящийся справа)

1. Take the *right-hand* lane at the light.
2. The switch is on the *right-hand* side of the door.

**2. In *right-hand man* and *right-hand woman*: the person you rely on most.** (правая рука (о помощнике))

3. She is the governor's *right-hand* woman.
4. He was the boss's *right-hand* man for twenty years.

---

### right-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌraɪtˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *ryt-MYN-did*
**Русский:** здравомыслящий, порядочный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + *-minded*, like *open-minded*, *like-minded*
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with the political right — that is *right-wing*.

Having the opinions and values the speaker thinks are decent and correct.

1. No *right-minded* person would defend that.
2. *Right-minded* citizens spoke up at the meeting.
3. Every *right-minded* voter should be worried by this.

---

### right-wing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌraɪtˈwɪŋ/ &middot; *ryt-WING*
**Русский:** правый, правого толка
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more right-wing*, *most right-wing*
**Form:** compound — *right* + *wing*
**Opposite:** *left-wing*

Belonging to the conservative side in politics.

1. He writes for a *right-wing* website.
2. Her views are pretty *right-wing* for this town.
3. A *right-wing* group organized the rally.

---

### righteous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈraɪtʃəs/ &middot; *RY-chuhs*
**Русский:** праведный; справедливый (о гневе)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more righteous*, *most righteous*
**Contrast:** The insult is *self-righteous*: certain of being right and showing it.

Righteous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Morally right and good, often with a religious feeling to it.** (праведный)

1. He saw himself as a *righteous* man.
2. The sermon was about *righteous* living.

**2. Of anger: felt to be completely justified.** (справедливый, праведный (о гневе))

3. There was *righteous* anger in her voice.
4. *Righteous* indignation filled the letters page for a week.

---

### rightful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈraɪtfəl/ &middot; *RYT-fuhl*
**Русский:** законный, полноправный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ful from *right* 'a legal claim'

Having the legal or moral right to something — of an owner, an heir or a place.

1. The painting was returned to its *rightful* owner.
2. She took her *rightful* place at the top of the rankings.
3. He is the *rightful* heir to the property.

---

### rigid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪdʒɪd/ &middot; *RIJ-id*
**Русский:** жёсткий, негнущийся; негибкий (о правилах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rigid*, *most rigid*
**Opposite:** *flexible*

Rigid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Stiff and not bending.** (жёсткий, негнущийся)

1. The case has a *rigid* plastic shell.
2. Keep your arm *rigid* while I wrap it.

**2. Very strict and not open to change.** (жёсткий, негибкий)

3. The school has *rigid* rules about phones.
4. He is too *rigid* to work well on a team.

---

### rigorous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪɡərəs/ &middot; *RIG-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** строгий, тщательный; суровый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more rigorous*, *most rigorous*

Rigorous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Careful, thorough and exact.** (строгий, тщательный)

1. The drug went through *rigorous* testing.
2. She is a *rigorous* thinker who checks every number.

**2. Demanding and hard to keep up with, of a course or a schedule.** (суровый, напряжённый)

3. It is a *rigorous* program — expect long hours.
4. They follow a *rigorous* training schedule all winter.

---

### rip-roaring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪpˌrɔːrɪŋ/ &middot; *RIP-ror-ing*
**Русский:** бурный, шумный; оглушительный (об успехе)
**Type:** compound; also emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *rip* + *roaring*
**Contrast:** Informal and cheerful. It goes with *success*, *time* and *adventure*, not with people.

Full of noisy energy, or very great — of a good time or a success.

1. They had a *rip-roaring* good time at the fair.
2. The show was a *rip-roaring* success.
3. It is a *rip-roaring* adventure movie with no quiet moments.

---

### ripe

**Pronunciation:** /raɪp/ &middot; *RYP*
**Русский:** спелый, зрелый; созревший (для чего-то)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *riper*, *ripest*
**Takes:** *for* + noun, in the 'ready' sense
**Opposite:** *unripe*

Ripe has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Fully grown and ready to eat, of fruit, vegetables or cheese.** (спелый, зрелый)

1. The peaches are finally *ripe*.
2. Pick the *ripest* tomato on the vine.

**2. Ready for something to happen.** (созревший, готовый (для чего-то))

3. The market is *ripe* for change.
4. Conditions were *ripe* for a strike.

---

### rising

**Pronunciation:** /ˈraɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *RY-zing*
**Русский:** растущий, повышающийся; восходящий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *rise*
**Contrast:** In American schools a *rising* senior is a student who will be a senior next fall.

Rising has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Going up in amount, level or price.** (растущий, повышающийся)

1. *Rising* prices are hurting families on a fixed income.
2. We watched the *rising* water from the porch.

**2. Becoming more successful or important.** (восходящий, набирающий известность)

3. She is a *rising* star in country music.
4. He is a *rising* senior at the high school.

---

### risk-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɪskˈfriː/ &middot; *risk-FREE*
**Русский:** безрисковый, без риска
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-free*, like *sugar-free*, *tax-free*

Carrying no chance of loss or harm.

1. There is no such thing as a *risk-free* investment.
2. The trial is *risk-free* — you can cancel any time.
3. No surgery is completely *risk-free*.

---

### risky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪski/ &middot; *RIS-kee*
**Русский:** рискованный, опасный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *riskier*, *riskiest*
**Form:** -y from *risk*
**Opposite:** *safe*
**Contrast:** *Risky* and *risqué* look alike but are different words: *risky* is about danger, *risqué* is about being slightly indecent.

Carrying a real chance that something will go wrong.

1. Driving in that storm was *risky*.
2. Stocks are *riskier* than bonds.
3. It is a *risky* plan, but it might work.

---

### risque

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈskeɪ/ &middot; *ri-SKAY*
**Русский:** фривольный, рискованный, непристойный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a risque joke*; *the show was pretty risque*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more risque*, *most risque*
**Form:** from French; usually printed with the accent, *risqué*
**Contrast:** A false friend for Russian speakers: *risque* is about sex and decency, not about danger. Risky business is *risky*.

Slightly shocking because it deals with sex.

1. He told a *risque* joke at the wedding and his wife glared.
2. The play was *risque* for 1955.
3. Her act got *more risque* as the night went on.

---

### risqué

**Pronunciation:** /rɪˈskeɪ/ &middot; *ri-SKAY*
**Русский:** фривольный, скабрёзный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more risqué*, *most risqué*
**Form:** from French; often written without the accent, *risque*, and the ending is said 'kay'
**Contrast:** Not the same word as *risky*. A *risqué* joke has nothing to do with danger.

Slightly rude or sexual, in a way that may shock some people.

1. He told a *risqué* joke at the wedding.
2. The costumes were a little *risqué* for a family show.
3. Her act is the most *risqué* on the whole tour.

---

### ritual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪtʃuəl/ &middot; *RICH-oo-uhl*
**Русский:** ритуальный, обрядовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Far more common as a noun (*a morning ritual*). The adjective goes only before a noun; after a verb use *ritualistic*.

Done as part of a ceremony, or always done in the same fixed way.

1. *Ritual* washing comes before the meal.
2. The dance had a *ritual* meaning for the village.
3. There was the *ritual* exchange of gifts at the door.

---

### ritualistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌrɪtʃuəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *rich-oo-uh-LIS-tik*
