**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a screwball* is an oddball, and in baseball a pitch that breaks the wrong way.

Crazy in a funny, unpredictable way, used above all of comedy.

1. We watched a *screwball* comedy from the 1930s.
2. The plot takes a *screwball* turn in the second act.
3. She has a *screwball* sense of humor that wins people over.

---

### scripted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskrɪptɪd/ &middot; *SKRIP-tid*
**Русский:** написанный по сценарию, заготовленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more scripted*, *most scripted*
**Form:** -ed from *script*
**Opposite:** *unscripted*

Written out in advance instead of said or done spontaneously.

1. Network television still runs mostly *scripted* dramas.
2. His apology sounded *scripted*.
3. The town hall felt *more scripted* than the debate did.

---

### scruffy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskrʌfi/ &middot; *SKRUF-ee*
**Русский:** потрёпанный, неряшливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *scruffier*, *scruffiest*
**Opposite:** *neat*

Untidy and worn-looking.

1. A *scruffy* dog followed us the whole way home.
2. He turned up *scruffy* and an hour late.
3. The neighborhood looks *scruffier* than it did ten years ago.

---

### scrumptious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskrʌmpʃəs/ &middot; *SKRUMP-shuhs*
**Русский:** очень вкусный, объедение
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more scrumptious*, *most scrumptious*
**Contrast:** Warm and a little old-fashioned; *delicious* is the neutral word.

Extremely good to eat.

1. Her grandmother makes a *scrumptious* peach pie.
2. Everything on the table looked *scrumptious*.
3. The bread was warm and absolutely *scrumptious*.

---

### scrupulous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskruːpjələs/ &middot; *SKROO-pyuh-luhs*
**Русский:** щепетильный, скрупулёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more scrupulous*, *most scrupulous*
**Takes:** *about* or *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *unscrupulous*

Careful to do everything correctly and honestly, down to small details.

1. She is *scrupulous* about paying people on time.
2. The report was based on *scrupulous* record keeping.
3. He is *more scrupulous* than anyone else in that office.

---

### sculpted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskʌlptɪd/ &middot; *SKULP-tid*
**Русский:** вылепленный, высеченный; рельефный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sculpted*, *most sculpted*
**Form:** -ed from the verb *sculpt*

Sculpted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shaped by carving or modeling.** (вылепленный, высеченный)

1. A *sculpted* stone lion sits at each end of the bridge.
2. The doors are covered in *sculpted* panels.

**2. Firm and clearly shaped, as if carved, said of a body or a face.** (рельефный, точёный)

3. He has the *sculpted* arms of someone who lives at the gym.
4. Her cheekbones look *sculpted*.

---

### scurrilous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɜːrələs/ &middot; *SKUR-uh-luhs*
**Русский:** клеветнический, злобно-оскорбительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more scurrilous*, *most scurrilous*

Insulting and almost certainly untrue, said or written to damage someone.

1. The campaign spread *scurrilous* rumors about his family.
2. She sued the site over a *scurrilous* article.
3. The attacks grew *more scurrilous* as the vote got closer.

---

### seafaring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːfɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *SEE-fair-ing*
**Русский:** мореходный, мореплавательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *sea* + *faring*, from *fare* 'to travel'

Traveling by sea, or living by work at sea.

1. The Portuguese were a great *seafaring* nation.
2. He came from a long line of *seafaring* men.
3. The museum tells the story of the town's *seafaring* past.

---

### sealed

**Pronunciation:** /siːld/ &middot; *SEELD*
**Русский:** запечатанный, герметичный; закрытый для доступа
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *seal*
**Opposite:** *open*

Sealed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Closed so tightly that nothing can get in or out.** (запечатанный, герметичный)

1. Bring the form back in a *sealed* envelope.
2. The meat comes in a *sealed* plastic tray.
3. Every window in the building is *sealed* shut.

**2. Closed by a court or an official so that the public cannot see it.** (закрытый для доступа, засекреченный)

4. His juvenile record is *sealed*.
5. The indictment stayed *sealed* until the arrest.

---

### seamless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːmləs/ &middot; *SEEM-luhs*
**Русский:** плавный, без сбоев; бесшовный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seamless*, *most seamless*
**Form:** -less from *seam*

Seamless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Moving from one part to the next with no gap, break or trouble.** (плавный, без сбоев)

1. The handoff between the two crews was *seamless*.
2. They promise a *seamless* transfer of your old files.
3. The new checkout is *more seamless* than the old one.

**2. Made in one piece, with no sewn or welded joins.** (бесшовный)

4. The pipe is drawn as a single *seamless* tube.
5. She wore a *seamless* knit dress.

---

### searching

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrtʃɪŋ/ &middot; *SUR-ching*
**Русский:** испытующий, пытливый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more searching*, *most searching*
**Form:** -ing from the verb *search*

Trying hard to find out the truth about someone or something.

1. She gave him a long, *searching* look.
2. The reporter asked a few *searching* questions about the money.
3. The book is a *searching* account of his own failures.

---

### seared

**Pronunciation:** /sɪrd/ &middot; *SEERD*
**Русский:** обжаренный снаружи, опалённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *sear*

Browned fast over high heat on the outside.

1. The special is *seared* tuna with a sesame crust.
2. Serve the steak *seared* on both sides but still pink inside.
3. A *seared* scallop should be dark gold, not gray.

---

### searing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪrɪŋ/ &middot; *SEER-ing*
**Русский:** жгучий, палящий; резкий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more searing*, *most searing*
**Form:** -ing from the verb *sear*

Searing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Burning hot, or painful like a burn.** (жгучий, палящий)

1. A *searing* pain shot down his leg.
2. They crossed the desert in *searing* heat.
3. The wind off the pavement was *searing*.

**2. Sharply critical or emotionally intense.** (резкий, пронзительный)

4. The film is a *searing* portrait of life on the border.
5. She wrote a *searing* letter to the board.

---

### seasick

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːsɪk/ &middot; *SEE-sik*
**Русский:** страдающий морской болезнью
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seasick*, *most seasick*
**Form:** compound — *sea* + *sick*

Sick to your stomach because of the movement of a boat.

1. Half the passengers were *seasick* within an hour.
2. Take a pill before you board if you get *seasick*.
3. He looked *seasick* the whole way across.

---

### seasonal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːzənəl/ &middot; *SEE-zuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** сезонный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *season*
**Contrast:** *Seasonal* means 'tied to a season'; *seasoned* means 'experienced' or 'flavored'.

Happening in, or changing with, a particular time of year.

1. The store hires *seasonal* workers every November.
2. *Seasonal* allergies hit him hard in the spring.
3. Demand for the product is highly *seasonal*.

---

### seasoned

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːzənd/ &middot; *SEE-zuhnd*
**Русский:** опытный, бывалый; приправленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'experienced' sense goes before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seasoned*, *most seasoned*
**Form:** -ed from the verb *season*

Seasoned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a lot of experience at something.** (опытный, бывалый)

1. She is a *seasoned* negotiator who has seen every trick.
2. Even *seasoned* climbers turn back in that weather.

**2. Given flavor with salt, pepper or herbs.** (приправленный)

3. The soup was perfectly *seasoned*.
4. Use lightly *seasoned* ground beef for the filling.

---

### seated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːtɪd/ &middot; *SEE-tid*
**Русский:** сидящий, сидячий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *seat*
**Opposite:** *standing*

Sitting down, or done while sitting.

1. Please remain *seated* until the plane reaches the gate.
2. The class ends with a *seated* stretch.
3. She was already *seated* when I got there.

---

### seaworthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːwɜːrði/ &middot; *SEE-wur-thee*
**Русский:** мореходный, годный к плаванию
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seaworthy*, *most seaworthy*
**Form:** compound — *sea* + *worthy*

In good enough condition to sail safely.

1. The inspector declared the boat *seaworthy*.
2. It took two years to make the old schooner *seaworthy* again.
3. Nobody would call that raft a *seaworthy* vessel.

---

### secluded

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈkluːdɪd/ &middot; *sih-KLOO-did*
**Русский:** уединённый, укромный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more secluded*, *most secluded*
**Form:** -ed from the verb *seclude*
**Opposite:** *exposed*

Quiet and private, far from other people.

1. They rented a *secluded* cabin in the mountains.
2. There is a *secluded* beach on the far side of the island.
3. The back patio is *more secluded* than the front porch.

---

### second

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛkənd/ &middot; *SEK-uhnd*
**Русский:** второй; ещё один
**Type:** classifying; also quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** ordinal of *two*
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a second* of time) and a verb (*to second a motion*). In *I finished second* it is the adverb.

Second has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Coming right after the first.** (второй)

1. Her office is the *second* door on the left.
2. He struck out in the *second* inning.
3. This is the *second* time this week.

**2. One more of the same kind.** (ещё один, второй)

4. Give him a *second* chance.
5. Would you like a *second* helping?

---

### second-best

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkəndˈbɛst/ &middot; *sek-uhnd-BEST*
**Русский:** второй по качеству, запасной
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *second* + the superlative *best*
**Contrast:** Also a noun in *settle for second best*, written without the hyphen.

Not quite as good as the best one.

1. He wore his *second-best* suit to the interview.
2. Their offer was the *second-best* on the table.
3. Taking the bus is *second-best*, but it works.

---

### second-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkəndˈklæs/ &middot; *sek-uhnd-KLAS*
**Русский:** второсортный; второго класса
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *second* + *class*

Second-class has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Treated as less important, with fewer rights than other people.** (второсортный, неполноправный)

1. They were treated like *second-class* citizens in their own country.
2. No student should get a *second-class* education because of a zip code.

**2. Of the cheaper grade of travel or mail.** (второго класса)

3. He rode in a *second-class* car all the way to Chicago.
4. The magazine went out at *second-class* postage rates.

---

### second-rate

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkəndˈreɪt/ &middot; *sek-uhnd-RAYT*
**Русский:** второсортный, посредственный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *second* + *rate*
**Opposite:** *first-rate*

Clearly not very good.

1. It was a *second-rate* hotel with a good view.
2. He spent years writing *second-rate* thrillers.
3. The food is fine; the service is *second-rate*.

---

### second-string

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkəndˈstrɪŋ/ &middot; *sek-uhnd-STRING*
**Русский:** запасной, второго состава
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *second* + *string*, from the lineup of players
**Opposite:** *first-string*

Not in the starting lineup, or not in the top group.

1. The *second-string* quarterback finished the game.
2. They sent a *second-string* delegation to the talks.
3. He spent two seasons as a *second-string* catcher.

---

### second-year

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkəndˈjɪr/ &middot; *sek-uhnd-YEER*
**Русский:** второго года, второкурсный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *second* + *year*

In the second year of a course, a job or a career.

1. She is a *second-year* law student.
2. The team is built around a *second-year* point guard.
3. *Second-year* residents take the night shifts.

---

### secondary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛkəndɛri/ &middot; *SEK-uhn-dair-ee*
**Русский:** второстепенный; средний (об образовании)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Opposite:** *primary*

Secondary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Less important than something else.** (второстепенный)

1. Cost is *secondary* to safety here.
2. The plot is *secondary*; you watch it for the acting.
3. Those are *secondary* concerns we can handle later.

**2. Of the school years between elementary school and college.** (средний (об образовании))

4. He teaches at the *secondary* level.
5. Fewer than half of them finished *secondary* school.

---

### secret

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːkrɪt/ &middot; *SEE-krit*
**Русский:** тайный, секретный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more secret*, *most secret*
**Opposite:** *public*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *keep a secret*. The person who holds things back is *secretive*, not *secret*.

Secret has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Known to only a few people and deliberately kept from everyone else.** (тайный, секретный)

1. They held a *secret* meeting in the back office.
2. He had a *secret* second phone.
3. The vote is *secret*, so nobody sees your ballot.

**2. Hidden so that other people cannot find it.** (потайной, скрытый)

4. There is a *secret* door behind the bookcase.
5. She kept the letters in a *secret* compartment.

---

### secretarial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛkrəˈtɛriəl/ &middot; *sek-ruh-TAIR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** секретарский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *secretary*

Having to do with the work of a secretary.

1. She took a *secretarial* course after high school.
2. The job is mostly *secretarial* duties and scheduling.
3. He was hired for *secretarial* work at a law firm.

---

### secretive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːkrətɪv/ &middot; *SEE-kruh-tiv*
**Русский:** скрытный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more secretive*, *most secretive*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ive from *secret*
**Opposite:** *open*
**Contrast:** *Secretive* describes a person or an organization that hides things; *secret* describes the thing that is hidden.

Keeping your plans and feelings to yourself.

1. He was *secretive* about where the money came from.
2. The company is famously *secretive* about new products.
3. She got *more secretive* as the divorce dragged on.

---

### sectarian

**Pronunciation:** /sɛkˈtɛriən/ &middot; *sek-TAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** межконфессиональный, сектантский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sectarian*, *most sectarian*
**Form:** -arian from *sect*
**Opposite:** *nonsectarian*

Coming from the split between religious or political groups, often violently.

1. *Sectarian* violence broke out again in the capital.
2. The country is still divided along *sectarian* lines.
3. He warned that the debate was becoming *more sectarian* than practical.

---

### secular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛkjələr/ &middot; *SEK-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** светский, мирской
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more secular*, *most secular*
**Opposite:** *religious*

Not connected with religion or the church.

1. Public schools here are *secular* by law.
2. He writes *secular* music for a church choir to sing.
3. The country has grown *more secular* over two generations.

---

### secure

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈkjʊr/ &middot; *sih-KYUUR*
**Русский:** безопасный, надёжный; прочно закреплённый; уверенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more secure*, *most secure*
**Takes:** *about* + noun; *in* + noun; *that*-clause
**Opposite:** *insecure*

Secure has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Safe from attack, theft or danger.** (безопасный, защищённый)

1. Keep the files on a *secure* server.
2. The building is *more secure* than it looks.
3. She finally found a *secure* job with benefits.

**2. Firmly fixed so that it will not move or come loose.** (прочно закреплённый)

4. Make sure the ladder is *secure* before you climb.
5. Check that the straps are *secure*.

**3. Feeling confident and not worried.** (уверенный, спокойный)

6. Kids need to feel *secure* at home.
7. He is *secure* in the knowledge that his family backs him.

---

### sedate

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈdeɪt/ &middot; *sih-DAYT*
**Русский:** степенный, неторопливый, чинный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sedate*, *most sedate*
**Opposite:** *lively*
**Contrast:** Also a verb: *to sedate a patient* is to give them a calming drug. The adjective does not mean 'drugged'.

Calm, slow and rather formal.

1. They went for a *sedate* walk around the pond.
2. The party was a *sedate* affair with classical music.
3. Life on the island is *more sedate* than in the city.

---

### sedative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛdətɪv/ &middot; *SED-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** успокаивающий, седативный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from the same root as *sedate*
**Contrast:** Much more common as a noun: *the doctor gave her a sedative*.

Having the effect of calming a person down or making them sleepy.

1. The drug has a mild *sedative* effect.
2. *Sedative* properties make it useful before surgery.
3. They warned him about the *sedative* side effects.

---

### sedentary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛdəntɛri/ &middot; *SED-uhn-tair-ee*
**Русский:** сидячий, малоподвижный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sedentary*, *most sedentary*
**Opposite:** *active*

Involving a lot of sitting and very little exercise.

1. Twelve hours at a desk makes for a *sedentary* job.
2. A *sedentary* lifestyle raises your risk of heart disease.
3. He has gotten *more sedentary* since he started working from home.

---

### sedimentary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛdəˈmɛntəri/ &middot; *sed-uh-MEN-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** осадочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ary from *sediment*

Formed from layers of sand, mud or shells pressed together over time.

1. Sandstone and limestone are *sedimentary* rocks.
2. The canyon walls show bands of *sedimentary* layers.
3. Oil is usually found in *sedimentary* basins.

---

### seditious

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈdɪʃəs/ &middot; *sih-DISH-uhs*
**Русский:** подстрекательский, мятежный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seditious*, *most seditious*
**Form:** -ous from *sedition*

Encouraging people to rebel against the government.

1. He was arrested for printing *seditious* pamphlets.
2. The speech was condemned as *seditious*.
3. The later letters are *more seditious* than anything he published.

---

### seductive

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈdʌktɪv/ &middot; *sih-DUK-tiv*
**Русский:** соблазнительный, обольстительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seductive*, *most seductive*
**Form:** -ive from the verb *seduce*

Seductive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sexually attractive in a deliberate way.** (соблазнительный, обольстительный)

1. She sang the whole song in a low, *seductive* voice.
2. He gave her a *seductive* smile across the room.

**2. Tempting, because it makes a bad idea look good.** (заманчивый, подкупающий)

3. There is something *seductive* about a simple answer.
4. The argument is *more seductive* than it is sound.

---

### see-through

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːθruː/ &middot; *SEE-throo*
**Русский:** прозрачный, просвечивающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — the verb *see* + *through*
**Opposite:** *opaque*
**Contrast:** Used of clothing and thin fabric; for glass and water the word is *clear* or *transparent*.

Thin enough that you can see what is behind or under it.

1. She wore a *see-through* blouse over a tank top.
2. The shower curtain is almost *see-through*.
3. They packed everything in *see-through* bags for security.

---

### seeded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːdɪd/ &middot; *SEE-did*
**Русский:** посеянный (в турнире); засеянный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *seed*

Seeded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Ranked among the top players or teams in a tournament.** (посеянный (в турнире))

1. The top-*seeded* team lost in the first round.
2. She is *seeded* third at the Open this year.

**2. Planted with seed.** (засеянный)

3. Keep off the newly *seeded* lawn.
4. A freshly *seeded* field needs water twice a day.

---

### seedless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːdləs/ &middot; *SEED-luhs*
**Русский:** без косточек, бессемянный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *seed*

Grown without seeds or pits.

1. Get the *seedless* grapes, not the ones with pits.
2. *Seedless* watermelon is easier to feed to kids.
3. The green ones are *seedless*.

---

### seedy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːdi/ &middot; *SEE-dee*
**Русский:** захудалый, сомнительный, обшарпанный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *seedier*, *seediest*
**Opposite:** *respectable*

Run-down and slightly dishonest or unsafe.

1. They stayed in a *seedy* motel off the highway.
2. The bar has a *seedy* reputation.
3. The block gets *seedier* the closer you get to the station.

---

### seeming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːmɪŋ/ &middot; *SEE-ming*
**Русский:** кажущийся, мнимый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from the verb *seem*
**Opposite:** *real*
**Contrast:** Never after a verb — you cannot say *the calm was seeming*. The adverb *seemingly* is far more common.

Appearing to be true, though it may not be.

1. Her *seeming* calm hid a lot of panic.
2. Despite his *seeming* indifference, he read every word.
3. The *seeming* contradiction disappears once you read the footnote.

---

### seething

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːðɪŋ/ &middot; *SEE-thing*
**Русский:** кипящий от злости; бурлящий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seething*, *most seething*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ing from the verb *seethe*

Seething has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely angry but holding it in.** (кипящий от злости)

1. He sat through the whole meeting *seething*.
2. She was *seething* with resentment by the end of the trip.

**2. Full of crowded, restless movement.** (бурлящий, кишащий)

3. The market was a *seething* mass of people.
4. The river below was *seething* and brown.

---

### segregated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛɡrəɡeɪtɪd/ &middot; *SEG-ruh-gay-tid*
**Русский:** сегрегированный, раздельный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more segregated*, *most segregated*
**Form:** -ed from the verb *segregate*
**Opposite:** *integrated*

Kept apart by race, sex or class, usually unfairly.

1. His parents grew up in a *segregated* school system.
2. Housing in the city is still deeply *segregated*.
3. Some districts are *more segregated* now than they were in 1970.

---

### seismic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaɪzmɪk/ &middot; *SYZ-mik*
**Русский:** сейсмический; колоссальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more seismic*, *most seismic*

Seismic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with earthquakes.** (сейсмический)

1. The bridge was built to the latest *seismic* standards.
2. *Seismic* activity picked up under the volcano.

**2. Enormous and sudden, said of change.** (колоссальный, судьбоносный)

3. The ruling was a *seismic* shift in the law.
4. Streaming had a *seismic* effect on the music business.

---

### select

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈlɛkt/ &middot; *sih-LEKT*
**Русский:** избранный, отборный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a common verb (*select a file*). As an adjective it goes only before a noun.

Chosen as the best, and open to only a few.

1. Only a *select* few were invited to the dinner.
2. The wine list includes some *select* California reds.
3. He plays on a *select* travel team.

---

### selected

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈlɛktɪd/ &middot; *sih-LEK-tid*
**Русский:** отобранный, избранный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *select*
**Contrast:** *Selected* means someone picked them out; *select* means they are the best. *Selected poems* is a sample, *a select group* is an elite.

Picked out from a larger group.

1. The volume prints his *selected* essays from twenty years.
2. The sale runs on *selected* items only.
3. *Selected* students get to sit in on the seminar.

---

### selective

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈlɛktɪv/ &middot; *sih-LEK-tiv*
**Русский:** разборчивый; избирательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more selective*, *most selective*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ive from the verb *select*

Selective has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Choosing carefully, and taking only a few.** (разборчивый, привередливый)

1. She is very *selective* about who she works with.
2. The college is *more selective* than it was a decade ago.

**2. Affecting or including some things but not others.** (избирательный)

3. He has a *selective* memory about that night.
4. The policy amounts to *selective* enforcement.

---

### self-absorbed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəbˈzɔːrbd/ &middot; *self-uhb-ZORBD*
**Русский:** поглощённый собой, эгоцентричный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-absorbed*, *most self-absorbed*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *absorbed*
**Opposite:** *considerate*

So interested in your own life that you barely notice other people.

1. He is too *self-absorbed* to ask how anyone else is doing.
2. The narrator is a *self-absorbed* twenty-year-old.
3. She gets *more self-absorbed* every time she is in a play.

---

### self-addressed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəˈdrɛst/ &middot; *self-uh-DREST*
**Русский:** с обратным адресом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *addressed*

Carrying your own name and address, so that it can be mailed back to you.

1. Include a *self-addressed* stamped envelope with your entry.
2. They mailed the results back in the *self-addressed* envelope.
3. The form asks for a *self-addressed* postcard.

---

### self-aggrandizing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəˈɡrændaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *self-uh-GRAN-dy-zing*
**Русский:** самовозвеличивающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-aggrandizing*, *most self-aggrandizing*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *aggrandizing*, 'making greater'

Working to make yourself look more important than you are.

1. The memoir is a long, *self-aggrandizing* account of one lucky deal.
2. He made a *self-aggrandizing* speech about his own generosity.
3. Each retelling gets *more self-aggrandizing*.

---

### self-appointed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəˈpɔɪntɪd/ &middot; *self-uh-POYN-tid*
**Русский:** самозваный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *appointed*

Having taken a job or a role without anyone asking you to.

1. He is the *self-appointed* guardian of the block's parking spots.
2. A *self-appointed* committee rewrote the bylaws.
3. She acts as the *self-appointed* spokesperson for the neighborhood.

---

### self-assured

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəˈʃʊrd/ &middot; *self-uh-SHUURD*
**Русский:** уверенный в себе
**Type:** compound; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-assured*, *most self-assured*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *assured*
**Opposite:** *insecure*
**Contrast:** *Self-assured* is a compliment; *self-satisfied* and *smug* are not.

Calmly confident in your own abilities.

1. She walked into the room *self-assured* and unhurried.
2. He gave a *self-assured* answer to a hostile question.
3. He is *more self-assured* on stage than he is in conversation.

---

### self-aware

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfəˈwɛr/ &middot; *self-uh-WAIR*
**Русский:** осознающий себя, самокритичный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-aware*, *most self-aware*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *aware*

Able to see your own faults and how you come across to others.

1. For a nineteen-year-old he is remarkably *self-aware*.
2. The show is *self-aware* about how silly its own premise is.
3. She got *more self-aware* after a year of therapy.

---

### self-centered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈsɛntərd/ &middot; *self-SEN-turd*
**Русский:** эгоцентричный, себялюбивый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-centered*, *most self-centered*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *centered*
**Opposite:** *selfless*
**Contrast:** American *self-centered*; British writing spells it *self-centred*.

Thinking only about yourself and what you want.

1. He is too *self-centered* to notice that she was upset.
2. It was a *self-centered* thing to say at a funeral.
3. Teenagers can be *more self-centered* than they mean to be.

---

### self-confessed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfkənˈfɛst/ &middot; *self-kuhn-FEST*
**Русский:** по собственному признанию
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *confessed*

Admitted by the person themselves, usually about a fault or a habit.

1. He is a *self-confessed* workaholic.
2. The book was written by a *self-confessed* amateur.
3. She is a *self-confessed* fan of bad reality TV.

---

### self-conscious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈkɑːnʃəs/ &middot; *self-KAHN-shuhs*
**Русский:** стеснительный, смущённый
**Type:** compound; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-conscious*, *most self-conscious*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *conscious*
**Contrast:** A false friend: it does not mean 'сознательный'. It means uncomfortable about how you look or sound.

Self-conscious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Uncomfortably aware of how other people are seeing you.** (стеснительный, смущённый)

1. She is *self-conscious* about her accent.
2. He gave a *self-conscious* laugh and looked at the floor.
3. I felt *more self-conscious* in that room than anywhere else.

**2. Deliberately aware of its own style, said of art and writing.** (нарочитый, подчёркнуто стилизованный)

4. The film is full of *self-conscious* references to old westerns.
5. The prose is *self-conscious* in a way that gets tiring.

---

### self-contained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfkənˈteɪnd/ &middot; *self-kuhn-TAYND*
**Русский:** автономный, самодостаточный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-contained*, *most self-contained*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *contained*

Self-contained has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Complete in itself, with everything it needs inside it.** (автономный, замкнутый в себе)

1. Each episode is a *self-contained* story.
2. The guest house is *self-contained*, with its own kitchen.

**2. Keeping to yourself and not needing other people's company.** (замкнутый, самодостаточный)

3. He is a quiet, *self-contained* man who reads all evening.
4. She seemed perfectly *self-contained* at the party.

---

### self-contradictory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfkɑːntrəˈdɪktəri/ &middot; *self-kahn-truh-DIK-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** противоречащий сам себе
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-contradictory*, *most self-contradictory*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *contradictory*

Containing two statements that cannot both be true.

1. His testimony was *self-contradictory* from the first page.
2. The rule is *self-contradictory*: it bans what the next line requires.
3. That is a *self-contradictory* way to describe a free market.

---

### self-defeating

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfdɪˈfiːtɪŋ/ &middot; *self-dih-FEE-ting*
**Русский:** контрпродуктивный, обречённый на провал
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-defeating*, *most self-defeating*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *defeating*

Working against the very thing you are trying to achieve.

1. Cutting the training budget to save money is *self-defeating*.
2. He is stuck in a *self-defeating* cycle of quitting and starting over.
3. Their strategy turned out to be *more self-defeating* than risky.

---

### self-deprecating

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈdɛprəkeɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *self-DEP-ruh-kay-ting*
**Русский:** самоироничный, самокритичный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-deprecating*, *most self-deprecating*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *deprecating*, 'putting down'
**Contrast:** Usually charming rather than sad: it is making fun of yourself before anyone else can.

Making light of your own abilities or achievements.

1. His speech was funny and *self-deprecating*.
2. She made a *self-deprecating* joke about her cooking.
3. He is *more self-deprecating* in interviews than he is in person.

---

### self-destructive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfdɪˈstrʌktɪv/ &middot; *self-dih-STRUK-tiv*
**Русский:** саморазрушительный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-destructive*, *most self-destructive*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *destructive*

Harming yourself by the way you behave.

1. Drinking before every shift was *self-destructive* and he knew it.
2. The band fell apart in a *self-destructive* spiral.
3. His choices got *more self-destructive* after the injury.

---

### self-directed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfdəˈrɛktɪd/ &middot; *self-duh-REK-tid*
**Русский:** самостоятельный, самоуправляемый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-directed*, *most self-directed*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *directed*

Organized and driven by the person doing it, without a teacher or a boss.

1. The course is *self-directed*, with deadlines you set yourself.
2. He opened a *self-directed* retirement account.
3. Graduate work is *more self-directed* than college.

---

### self-disciplined

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈdɪsəplɪnd/ &middot; *self-DIS-uh-plind*
**Русский:** дисциплинированный, волевой
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-disciplined*, *most self-disciplined*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *disciplined*

Able to make yourself do what needs doing without being pushed.

1. You have to be *self-disciplined* to work from home.
2. She is the *most self-disciplined* runner on the team.
3. He is a quiet, *self-disciplined* kid.

---

### self-educated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈɛdʒəkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *self-EJ-uh-kay-tid*
**Русский:** самообразованный, самоучка
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *educated*

Having learned by reading and studying alone rather than at a school.

1. He was a *self-educated* engineer who never finished high school.
2. She is largely *self-educated* in music theory.
3. The library was full of *self-educated* readers.

---

### self-effacing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪˈfeɪsɪŋ/ &middot; *self-ih-FAY-sing*
**Русский:** скромный, не выпячивающий себя
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-effacing*, *most self-effacing*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *effacing*, 'rubbing out'
**Opposite:** *self-important*

Not wanting attention or credit for what you have done.

1. He is so *self-effacing* that nobody knew he had funded the whole thing.
2. She gave a short, *self-effacing* answer and changed the subject.
3. The coach is *more self-effacing* than most people in the job.

---

### self-employed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪmˈplɔɪd/ &middot; *self-im-PLOYD*
**Русский:** работающий на себя, самозанятый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *employed*

Working for yourself rather than for a company.

1. She has been *self-employed* since 2015.
2. *Self-employed* people pay their taxes quarterly.
3. He left the firm to become a *self-employed* consultant.

---

### self-evident

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈɛvədənt/ &middot; *self-EV-uh-duhnt*
**Русский:** самоочевидный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-evident*, *most self-evident*
**Takes:** *that*-clause
**Form:** compound — *self* + *evident*

Obviously true without needing any proof.

1. We hold these truths to be *self-evident*.
2. It is *self-evident* that the two numbers cannot both be right.
3. The answer is not as *self-evident* as he thinks.

---

### self-explanatory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪkˈsplænətɔːri/ &middot; *self-ik-SPLAN-uh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** не требующий пояснений, понятный сам по себе
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-explanatory*, *most self-explanatory*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *explanatory*

Clear on its own, with no explanation needed.

1. The instructions are *self-explanatory*.
2. Most of the buttons are *self-explanatory* once you open the app.
3. The chart is *more self-explanatory* than the paragraph under it.

---

### self-governing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈɡʌvərnɪŋ/ &middot; *self-GUV-ur-ning*
**Русский:** самоуправляемый, автономный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *governing*

Running its own affairs without being controlled from outside.

1. The territory has been *self-governing* since 1979.
2. Each *self-governing* district sets its own school calendar.
3. The profession is largely *self-governing*.

---

### self-important

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪmˈpɔːrtənt/ &middot; *self-im-POR-tuhnt*
**Русский:** спесивый, важничающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-important*, *most self-important*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *important*
**Opposite:** *modest*

Acting as if you matter far more than you do.

1. A *self-important* little man checked our badges twice.
2. The email was long and *self-important*.
3. He gets *more self-important* with every promotion.

---

### self-imposed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪmˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *self-im-POHZD*
**Русский:** добровольно взятый на себя
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *imposed*

Chosen by yourself rather than forced on you by someone else.

1. He missed his own *self-imposed* deadline by a month.
2. She spent a year in *self-imposed* exile in Maine.
3. The company works under a *self-imposed* limit on overtime.

---

### self-induced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪnˈduːst/ &middot; *self-in-DOOST*
**Русский:** вызванный собственными действиями
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *induced*

Brought on by your own actions.

1. The whole crisis was *self-induced*.
2. He blamed his *self-induced* panic on too much coffee.
3. It was a *self-induced* injury from lifting too much too fast.

---

### self-indulgent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪnˈdʌldʒənt/ &middot; *self-in-DUL-juhnt*
**Русский:** потакающий своим слабостям; самолюбующийся
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-indulgent*, *most self-indulgent*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *indulgent*

Self-indulgent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Letting yourself have whatever you want, more than is good for you.** (потакающий своим слабостям)

1. A whole Saturday in bed felt gloriously *self-indulgent*.
2. He has a *self-indulgent* streak when it comes to gadgets.

**2. Too long or too pleased with itself, said of art and writing.** (самолюбующийся, затянутый)

3. The director's cut is *self-indulgent* at three and a half hours.
4. Critics called the album *more self-indulgent* than his last one.

---

### self-involved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfɪnˈvɑːlvd/ &middot; *self-in-VAHLVD*
**Русский:** поглощённый собой
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-involved*, *most self-involved*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *involved*
**Contrast:** A close synonym of *self-absorbed*; American writers use both.

Wrapped up in your own problems and feelings.

1. He was too *self-involved* that year to see what she was going through.
2. The characters are all young and *self-involved*.
3. Grief made her *more self-involved* than usual.

---

### self-made

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈmeɪd/ &middot; *self-MAYD*
**Русский:** всего добившийся сам
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *made*

Rich or successful through your own work, starting with nothing.

1. He likes to call himself a *self-made* millionaire.
2. She is a *self-made* businesswoman from a mill town.
3. The magazine ran a list of *self-made* founders under thirty.

---

### self-possessed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfpəˈzɛst/ &middot; *self-puh-ZEST*
**Русский:** хладнокровный, владеющий собой
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-possessed*, *most self-possessed*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *possessed*

Calm and in control of yourself under pressure.

1. She stayed *self-possessed* through two hours of questions.
2. He is a *self-possessed* witness who never raises his voice.
3. The younger sister is *more self-possessed* than either parent.

---

### self-proclaimed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfprəˈkleɪmd/ &middot; *self-pruh-KLAYMD*
**Русский:** самопровозглашённый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *proclaimed*
**Contrast:** Usually skeptical: the writer is signaling that only this person makes the claim.

Called that by the person themselves, with nobody else agreeing.

1. A *self-proclaimed* expert ran the whole workshop.
2. The *self-proclaimed* king of neighborhood barbecue lost the contest.
3. He is a *self-proclaimed* political outsider with thirty years in office.

---

### self-propelled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfprəˈpɛld/ &middot; *self-pruh-PELD*
**Русский:** самоходный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *propelled*

Carrying its own engine or motor instead of being pushed or pulled.

1. The army bought a battery of *self-propelled* guns.
2. He mows the hill with a *self-propelled* mower.
3. A *self-propelled* barge came up the channel.

---

### self-reliant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfrɪˈlaɪənt/ &middot; *self-rih-LY-uhnt*
**Русский:** самостоятельный, полагающийся на себя
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-reliant*, *most self-reliant*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *reliant*, from *rely*
**Opposite:** *dependent*

Able to manage without help from other people.

1. Living alone made him *self-reliant* fast.
2. She raised three *self-reliant* daughters.
3. The town is *more self-reliant* than it was before the mill closed.

---

### self-respecting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfrɪˈspɛktɪŋ/ &middot; *self-rih-SPEK-ting*
**Русский:** уважающий себя
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *respecting*
**Contrast:** Nine times out of ten it follows *no*: *no self-respecting cook would buy that*.

Having proper pride in yourself and your standards.

1. No *self-respecting* barbecue joint serves that sauce.
2. No *self-respecting* editor would let that headline through.
3. Any *self-respecting* fan knows the whole roster.

---

### self-righteous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈraɪtʃəs/ &middot; *self-RY-chuhs*
**Русский:** самодовольно-праведный, ханжеский
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-righteous*, *most self-righteous*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *righteous*

Sure that you are more moral than everyone else, and showing it.

1. He gave a *self-righteous* lecture about recycling.
2. The tone of the letter is *self-righteous* and a little cruel.
3. She gets *more self-righteous* every time the subject comes up.

---

### self-sacrificing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈsækrəfaɪsɪŋ/ &middot; *self-SAK-ruh-fy-sing*
**Русский:** самоотверженный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-sacrificing*, *most self-sacrificing*
**Form:** compound — *self* + the participle *sacrificing*
**Opposite:** *selfish*

Giving up your own comfort or wishes for other people.

1. Her mother was a *self-sacrificing* woman who worked two jobs.
2. It was a *self-sacrificing* decision that cost him the promotion.
3. Nurses on that ward are *more self-sacrificing* than anyone knows.

---

### self-satisfied

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈsætɪsfaɪd/ &middot; *self-SAT-iss-fyd*
**Русский:** самодовольный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-satisfied*, *most self-satisfied*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *satisfied*
**Opposite:** *humble*

Too pleased with yourself, in a way that annoys other people.

1. He gave a *self-satisfied* smile when the numbers came in.
2. She looked *self-satisfied* after winning the argument.
3. The whole article is smug and *self-satisfied*.

---

### self-service

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈsɜːrvɪs/ &middot; *self-SUR-viss*
**Русский:** с самообслуживанием
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Where customers serve themselves instead of being served.

1. We stopped at a *self-service* gas station outside Reno.
2. The cafeteria is *self-service*, so grab a tray.
3. *Self-service* checkout lanes move faster at this hour.

---

### self-serving

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈsɜːrvɪŋ/ &middot; *self-SUR-ving*
**Русский:** своекорыстный, корыстный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-serving*, *most self-serving*
**Form:** compound — *self* + *serving*
**Opposite:** *selfless*

Done to help yourself, while pretending to help others.

1. His apology sounded *self-serving*.
2. It was a *self-serving* decision dressed up as generosity.
3. Nothing is *more self-serving* than helping only when the cameras are on.

---

### self-styled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfˈstaɪld/ &middot; *self-STYLD*
**Русский:** самозваный, так называемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — never after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + *styled*
**Contrast:** Always doubtful: the speaker gives the title because the person claims it, not because it is earned.

Using a title or description that you gave yourself.

1. A *self-styled* expert took over the meeting.
2. He is a *self-styled* reformer with no record to show for it.
3. The *self-styled* king of late-night radio signed off for good.

---

### self-sufficient

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfsəˈfɪʃənt/ &middot; *self-suh-FISH-uhnt*
**Русский:** самодостаточный, самообеспечивающийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more self-sufficient*, *most self-sufficient*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Opposite:** *dependent*

Able to provide everything you need without help from outside.

1. The farm is almost *self-sufficient* in vegetables.
2. She learned to be *self-sufficient* at a young age.
3. The country is *more self-sufficient* in energy than it was ten years ago.

---

### self-sustaining

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛlfsəˈsteɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *self-suh-STAY-ning*
**Русский:** самоподдерживающийся, самоокупающийся
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *self* + *sustaining*

Able to keep itself going without outside help or money.

1. The project is *self-sustaining* now and needs no more grants.
2. A *self-sustaining* chain reaction starts in a fraction of a second.
3. Her garden is small but *self-sustaining*.

---

### selfish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛlfɪʃ/ &middot; *SEL-fish*
**Русский:** эгоистичный, себялюбивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more selfish*, *most selfish*
**Form:** -ish from *self*
**Opposite:** *generous*; *unselfish*

Caring only about yourself and not about other people.

1. Don't be *selfish* — share the cookies with your sister.
2. It was a *selfish* thing to do, and he knew it.
3. He gets *more selfish* the longer he lives alone.

---

### selfless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛlfləs/ &middot; *SELF-luhs*
**Русский:** самоотверженный, бескорыстный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more selfless*, *most selfless*
**Form:** -less from *self*
**Opposite:** *selfish*

Thinking about other people's needs before your own.

1. Her years of work with refugees were entirely *selfless*.
2. It was a *selfless* act of courage.
3. The nurses did the *most selfless* work of anyone that winter.

---

### semantic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈmæntɪk/ &middot; *sih-MAN-tik*
**Русский:** семантический, смысловой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Semantics* is the noun: *it's just semantics* means the argument is about words, not about facts.

Having to do with the meaning of words.

1. The difference is purely *semantic*.
2. They spent an hour on a *semantic* detail.
3. *Semantic* analysis of the contract turned up two readings.

---

### semi-formal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈfɔːrməl/ &middot; *sem-ee-FOR-muhl*
**Русский:** полуофициальный, полуформальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *formal*
**Contrast:** American dictionaries prefer *semiformal*, with no hyphen; the two spellings are the same word.

Less formal than a black-tie event, but dressier than everyday clothes.

1. The invitation says *semi-formal*, so wear a jacket.
2. It was a *semi-formal* dinner at the club.
3. We kept the tone of the meeting *semi-formal*.

---

### semiannual

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈænjuəl/ &middot; *sem-ee-AN-yoo-uhl*
**Русский:** полугодовой, происходящий раз в полгода
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *annual*
**Contrast:** *Semiannual* means twice a year, like *biannual*; *biennial* means every two years.

Happening twice a year.

1. The *semiannual* report comes out in June and December.
2. The store runs a *semiannual* sale on coats.
3. She goes in for a *semiannual* checkup.

---

### semiautomatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiɔːtəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *sem-ee-aw-tuh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** полуавтоматический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *automatic*

Partly automatic: one action by the operator, the rest by the machine.

1. A *semiautomatic* rifle fires one round per trigger pull.
2. The press is *semiautomatic*: you load it and it does the rest.
3. *Semiautomatic* transmissions were rare on American cars.

---

### semicircular

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈsɜːrkjələr/ &middot; *sem-ee-SUR-kyuh-lur*
**Русский:** полукруглый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *circular*

Shaped like half a circle.

1. The seats form a *semicircular* row around the stage.
2. A *semicircular* window sits above the front door.
3. The driveway in front of the house is *semicircular*.

---

### semiformal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈfɔːrməl/ &middot; *sem-ee-FOR-muhl*
**Русский:** полуофициальный, полуформальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *formal*
**Contrast:** The usual American spelling; *semi-formal* with a hyphen is the same word.

Dressier than everyday clothes, but short of black tie.

1. He wore a *semiformal* suit to the reception.
2. The wedding is *semiformal* — no jeans.
3. *Semiformal* attire means a dark suit or a cocktail dress.

---

### seminal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛmɪnəl/ &middot; *SEM-ih-nuhl*
**Русский:** основополагающий, фундаментальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Not a *semi-* word: *seminal* comes from *seed* and means first and influential, not half of anything.

Early and so important that later work grows out of it.

1. Her *seminal* paper changed the whole field.
2. That record is a *seminal* album of early punk.
3. The book is one of the *seminal* works of the period.

---

### semipermanent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈpɜːrmənənt/ &middot; *sem-ee-PUR-muh-nuhnt*
**Русский:** полупостоянный, долговременный (но не навсегда)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *permanent*

Lasting a long time but not forever.

1. She uses a *semipermanent* hair color that washes out in a month.
2. The trailers became *semipermanent* housing for the crew.
3. *Semipermanent* makeup lasts about a year.

---

### semiprecious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈprɛʃəs/ &middot; *sem-ee-PRESH-uhs*
**Русский:** полудрагоценный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *precious*

Of a stone: valuable, but less so than a diamond or a ruby.

1. The ring is set with *semiprecious* stones.
2. Amethyst is a *semiprecious* gem.
3. She keeps a tray of *semiprecious* beads for her jewelry.

---

### semisweet

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛmiˈswiːt/ &middot; *sem-ee-SWEET*
**Русский:** полусладкий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** semi- + *sweet*

Slightly sweet: less sugar than sweet chocolate or wine, more than bitter.

1. The recipe calls for a cup of *semisweet* chocolate chips.
2. We ordered a *semisweet* white wine.
3. This chocolate is *semisweet*, not bitter.

---

### Semitic

**Pronunciation:** /səˈmɪtɪk/ &middot; *suh-MIT-ik*
**Русский:** семитский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *Semitic* names a family of languages and peoples; *anti-Semitic* is used only of hostility toward Jews.

Belonging to the group of peoples and languages that includes Hebrew and Arabic.

1. Hebrew and Arabic are *Semitic* languages.
2. The inscription is written in a *Semitic* script.
3. The course covers the *Semitic* peoples of the ancient Near East.

---

### senatorial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛnəˈtɔːriəl/ &middot; *sen-uh-TOR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** сенаторский, сенатский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *senator*

Having to do with a senator or the Senate.

1. He announced his *senatorial* campaign in the spring.
2. *Senatorial* courtesy decides which judges get a hearing.
3. A *senatorial* district in this state covers everything.

---

### Senegalese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛnɪɡəˈliːz/ &middot; *sen-ih-guh-LEEZ*
**Русский:** сенегальский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The same word is the noun, and it does not change in the plural: *a Senegalese*, *the Senegalese*.

From Senegal, or having to do with Senegal.

1. A *Senegalese* restaurant opened on Main Street.
2. The *Senegalese* team won on penalties.
3. Her mother is *Senegalese*.

---

### senile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːnaɪl/ &middot; *SEE-nyl*
**Русский:** старческий, выживший из ума
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more senile*, *most senile*
**Contrast:** Offensive about a person today — doctors say *has dementia*. The word survives in the fixed term *senile dementia*.

Confused and forgetful because of old age.

1. He joked that he was going *senile*, but nobody laughed.
2. *Senile* dementia was the old name for the condition.
3. She seemed *more senile* each time we visited.

---

### senior

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːnjər/ &middot; *SEE-nyur*
**Русский:** старший; пожилой; выпускной
**Type:** classifying; also age
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more senior*, *most senior*
**Takes:** *to* + noun — *senior to everyone else on the team*
**Opposite:** *junior*
**Contrast:** After *senior* use *to*, never *than*: *She is senior to me*, not *senior than me*.

Senior has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Higher in rank, or longer in the job, than other people.** (старший (по должности))

1. She's a *senior* editor at the paper.
2. Two *more senior* officers reviewed the case.
3. He is *senior* to me by three years.

**2. Older; meant for older people.** (пожилой; для пожилых)

4. *Senior* citizens ride the city bus for free.
5. Ask about the *senior* discount before you pay.

**3. Of the last year of high school or college.** (выпускной (о последнем годе учёбы))

6. My *senior* year was the best one.
7. The *senior* class voted to go to Chicago.
8. *Senior* prom is the third Friday in May.

---

### sensational

**Pronunciation:** /sɛnˈseɪʃənəl/ &middot; *sen-SAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** потрясающий; сенсационный, скандальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sensational*, *most sensational*
**Contrast:** A *sensational* dinner is wonderful; *sensational* reporting is cheap and shocking. The noun it goes with tells you which.

Sensational has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely good; wonderful.** (потрясающий, великолепный)

1. The food at that place is *sensational*.
2. She gave a *sensational* performance on opening night.

**2. Written or presented to shock and excite people.** (сенсационный, скандальный)

3. The paper ran a *sensational* headline about the mayor.
4. The trial got *more sensational* with every witness.
5. *Sensational* coverage of the crash upset the families.

---

### sensationalistic

**Pronunciation:** /sɛnˌseɪʃənəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *sen-say-shuh-nuh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** падкий на сенсации, броский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sensationalistic*, *most sensationalistic*
**Form:** -istic from *sensationalism*

Using shocking stories and details to attract attention.

1. The coverage was *sensationalistic* and thin on facts.
2. He hosts a *sensationalistic* talk show.
3. Local news has gotten *more sensationalistic* since the ratings dropped.

---

### senseless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnsləs/ &middot; *SENS-luhs*
**Русский:** бессмысленный; без сознания
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more senseless*, *most senseless*
**Form:** -less from *sense*
**Contrast:** Not the opposite of *sensitive*. And the second meaning survives mostly in *knocked senseless* and *beaten senseless*.

Senseless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having no purpose or meaning; stupid.** (бессмысленный, безрассудный)

1. The *senseless* killing shocked the whole town.
2. It was a *senseless* waste of money.
3. Arguing about it now is *senseless*.

**2. Unconscious.** (без сознания)

4. He was knocked *senseless* by the fall.
5. The blow left her *senseless* on the floor.

---

### sensory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnsəri/ &middot; *SEN-suh-ree*
**Русский:** сенсорный, чувственный (связанный с органами чувств)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with the senses — sight, hearing, touch, taste and smell.

1. *Sensory* nerves carry signals to the brain.
2. The museum has a *sensory* room for small children.
3. *Sensory* overload hits me in big stores.

---

### sensual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnʃuəl/ &middot; *SEN-shoo-uhl*
**Русский:** чувственный, плотский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sensual*, *most sensual*
**Contrast:** *Sensual* usually points to physical, often sexual pleasure; *sensuous* to pleasure the senses take in art, music or food.

Connected with physical, often sexual pleasure.

1. Her voice on the record is low and *sensual*.
2. They danced a slow, *sensual* tango.
3. The film is *more sensual* than the novel it came from.

---

### sensuous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnʃuəs/ &middot; *SEN-shoo-uhs*
**Русский:** чувственный, приятный для органов чувств
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sensuous*, *most sensuous*
**Contrast:** Keep it apart from *sensual*: *sensuous* is about pleasure in colors, sounds and textures, with no sexual meaning attached.

Giving pleasure to the senses.

1. She loves the *sensuous* texture of velvet.
2. His poems are full of *sensuous* detail — smell, color, sound.
3. The soup was rich and *sensuous*.

---

### sentient

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnʃənt/ &middot; *SEN-shuhnt*
**Русский:** разумный, обладающий сознанием, чувствующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Able to feel and be aware of things.

1. Whales are *sentient* beings.
2. The movie asks whether the robot is *sentient*.
3. The law protects *sentient* animals from cruelty.

---

### sentimental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛntɪˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *sen-tih-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** сентиментальный; дорогой как память
**Type:** emotion; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sentimental*, *most sentimental*
**Takes:** *about* + noun

Sentimental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Showing tender feelings, often more than the situation deserves.** (сентиментальный)

1. He gets *sentimental* after two beers.
2. The ending is *sentimental*, but I cried anyway.
3. She's *more sentimental* about the old house than I am.

**2. Valued for the memories attached to it, not for the money.** (дорогой как память)

4. The ring has *sentimental* value.
5. I keep the letters for *sentimental* reasons.

---

### separate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛprət/ &middot; *SEP-ruht*
**Русский:** отдельный; различный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* + noun
**Contrast:** The adjective ends in a schwa — *SEP-ruht*; the verb is *SEP-uh-rayt*. Same spelling, two pronunciations.

Separate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not joined to, or not together with, something else.** (отдельный, раздельный)

1. The kids have *separate* rooms now.
2. Keep the raw chicken *separate* from the salad.
3. We arrived in two *separate* cars.

**2. Different; not the same one.** (другой, отличный)

4. That's a *separate* question, and we'll get to it.
5. Her work life and her home life are completely *separate*.

---

### separated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛpəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *SEP-uh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** живущий раздельно (о супругах); разлучённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* + noun
**Contrast:** *Separate* is a plain state — apart or different. *Separated* says something pulled them apart, and of a couple it means living apart but not yet divorced.

Separated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. No longer living together as a couple, but not divorced.** (живущий раздельно (о супругах))

1. They've been *separated* for about a year.
2. My parents are *separated*, not divorced.

**2. Kept apart from something or someone else.** (разлучённый, отделённый)

3. The twins were *separated* at birth.
4. The two wings of the school are *separated* by a courtyard.

---

### separatist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛprətɪst/ &middot; *SEP-ruh-tist*
**Русский:** сепаратистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Wanting a region to break away and become its own country.

1. A *separatist* movement grew in the north.
2. *Separatist* fighters took the airport.
3. He joined a small *separatist* party in his twenties.

---

### septic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛptɪk/ &middot; *SEP-tik*
**Русский:** септический, заражённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A *septic tank* uses an older sense — bacteria break the waste down. Nothing there is infected.

Infected with harmful bacteria.

1. The wound turned *septic* within two days.
2. She was rushed in with *septic* shock.
3. An untreated cut like that can go *septic*.

---

### sequential

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈkwɛnʃəl/ &middot; *sih-KWEN-shuhl*
**Русский:** последовательный, идущий по порядку
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *sequence*

Following one after another in a fixed order.

1. The files are stored in *sequential* order.
2. The tickets have *sequential* numbers.
3. The training is *sequential*: you can't skip a step.

---

### sequined

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːkwɪnd/ &middot; *SEE-kwind*
**Русский:** расшитый пайетками, с блёстками
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *sequin*

Covered with small shiny disks sewn onto the cloth.

1. She wore a *sequined* dress to the party.
2. A *sequined* jacket caught the stage lights.
3. The costumes were heavily *sequined*.

---

### Serbian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrbiən/ &middot; *SUR-bee-uhn*
**Русский:** сербский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

From Serbia, or having to do with Serbia and its language.

1. *Serbian* is written in two alphabets.
2. We went to a *Serbian* film festival downtown.
3. Her husband is *Serbian*.

---

### serendipitous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛrənˈdɪpɪtəs/ &middot; *ser-uhn-DIP-ih-tuhs*
**Русский:** счастливо случайный, удачный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more serendipitous*, *most serendipitous*

Happening by luck, and turning out well.

1. Our meeting was completely *serendipitous*.
2. It was a *serendipitous* discovery in the back of the archive.
3. Nothing is *more serendipitous* than finding the right book by accident.

---

### serene

**Pronunciation:** /səˈriːn/ &middot; *suh-REEN*
**Русский:** безмятежный, спокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more serene*, *most serene*
**Opposite:** *agitated*

Completely calm and peaceful.

1. She stayed *serene* through the whole hearing.
2. A *serene* lake at dawn is worth the early start.
3. The garden is *more serene* on a weekday morning.

---

### serial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪriəl/ &middot; *SEER-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** серийный; совершающий что-либо многократно
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Sounds exactly like *cereal*, the breakfast food — different word, same pronunciation.

Serial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Doing the same thing again and again.** (совершающий что-либо многократно)

1. Police were hunting a *serial* killer.
2. He's a *serial* entrepreneur — this is his fifth company.
3. She called him a *serial* liar to his face.

**2. Forming or belonging to a series.** (серийный, последовательный)

4. Write down the *serial* number on the back.
5. The novel first appeared in *serial* form.

---

### serrated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛreɪtɪd/ &middot; *SER-ay-tid*
**Русский:** зазубренный, зубчатый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having a row of small points along the edge, like a saw.

1. Use a *serrated* knife for bread.
2. The leaves have *serrated* edges.
3. The blade is *serrated* near the handle.

---

### serviceable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrvɪsəbəl/ &middot; *SUR-vih-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** годный, пригодный; сносный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more serviceable*, *most serviceable*

Serviceable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Good enough to do the job, though nothing special.** (сносный, приемлемый)

1. His Spanish is *serviceable* — he can order dinner and ask directions.
2. The hotel was plain but *serviceable*.

**2. Still in working condition; usable.** (годный, исправный)

3. Only two of the trucks are still *serviceable*.
4. The old boots are *serviceable* for another winter.

---

### servile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrvəl/ &middot; *SUR-vuhl*
**Русский:** раболепный, угодливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more servile*, *most servile*
**Opposite:** *defiant*

Too eager to obey and please, like a servant.

1. His *servile* manner around the boss made everyone uncomfortable.
2. She refused to be *servile* to anyone.
3. The letter struck a *servile* tone that embarrassed the whole department.

---

### set

**Pronunciation:** /sɛt/ &middot; *SET*
**Русский:** установленный, назначенный; готовый; упрямый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the *ready* meaning only after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — though *more set in his ways* is common
**Takes:** *on* + *-ing*; *against* + noun — *set on leaving*, *set against the idea*
**Contrast:** Mostly a verb and a noun. As an adjective it lives in a few patterns: *a set time*, *all set*, *set in his ways*, *set on going*.

Set has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Fixed or arranged in advance.** (установленный, назначенный)

1. We meet at a *set* time every Monday.
2. The restaurant offers a *set* menu for thirty dollars.
3. There's no *set* rule about how long it takes.

**2. Ready.** (готовый)

4. Are you all *set*?
5. We're *set* to leave at six.

**3. Determined about something, or unwilling to change.** (твёрдо решивший; упрямый)

6. He's *set* in his ways and won't try anything new.
7. She's *set* on going to law school.
8. They were dead *set* against the plan.

---

### settled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛtəld/ &middot; *SET-uhld*
**Русский:** устроенный, осевший; устоявшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *unsettled*

Settled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Living a regular life in one place.** (устроенный, осевший)

1. They finally feel *settled* in the new town.
2. Once the kids were *settled*, she went back to school.

**2. Calm and unlikely to change.** (устоявшийся, установившийся)

3. The weather looks *settled* for the weekend.
4. The market is *settled* after a rough month.

---

### severe

**Pronunciation:** /səˈvɪr/ &middot; *suh-VEER*
**Русский:** сильный, тяжёлый; суровый; строгий
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more severe*, *most severe*

Severe has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Very bad or serious.** (сильный, тяжёлый)

1. She's in *severe* pain.
2. The storm did *severe* damage to the pier.
3. This winter was *more severe* than last.

**2. Strict and harsh.** (суровый, строгий)

4. The judge handed down a *severe* sentence.
5. He gave me a *severe* look over his glasses.

**3. Very plain, with no decoration.** (строгий, простой (без украшений))

6. Her hair was pulled back in a *severe* bun.
7. The building's lines are *severe* and gray.

---

### severed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛvərd/ &middot; *SEV-urd*
**Русский:** отрезанный, отсечённый; разорванный (о связях)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sever*

Cut off, or cut completely through.

1. Surgeons reattached the *severed* finger.
2. A *severed* cable left the whole block dark.
3. He still talks about those *severed* friendships.

---

### sexist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛksɪst/ &middot; *SEK-sist*
**Русский:** сексистский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sexist*, *most sexist*
**Form:** -ist from *sex*

Treating one sex as less able or less important than the other.

1. That joke was *sexist* and everyone knew it.
2. The dress code is openly *sexist*.
3. Ads from the fifties look *more sexist* every year.

---

### sexual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛkʃuəl/ &middot; *SEK-shoo-uhl*
**Русский:** сексуальный, половой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A false friend: Russian *сексуальный* often means *sexy*. English *sexual* is the neutral, technical word — *a sexy dress*, never *a sexual dress*.

Sexual has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with sex or physical attraction.** (сексуальный, половой)

1. *Sexual* attraction is not the same thing as love.
2. The novel deals with *sexual* relationships openly.

**2. Having to do with the two sexes, or with reproduction.** (половой (о размножении, равенстве))

3. *Sexual* equality at work is still a fight.
4. Most plants use *sexual* reproduction.

---

### sexy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛksi/ &middot; *SEK-see*
**Русский:** сексуальный, привлекательный; заманчивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sexier*, *sexiest*

Sexy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sexually attractive.** (сексуальный, привлекательный)

1. He looked *sexy* in that jacket.
2. It's the *sexiest* song on the album.

**2. Informal: exciting and interesting to people.** (заманчивый, яркий (о теме, идее))

3. Budget reform is not a *sexy* topic.
4. They needed a *sexier* name for the product.

---

### shabby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃæbi/ &middot; *SHAB-ee*
**Русский:** потрёпанный, обшарпанный; подлый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shabbier*, *shabbiest*

Shabby has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worn out and untidy from long use.** (потрёпанный, обшарпанный)

1. He wore a *shabby* coat with a torn pocket.
2. The lobby looked *shabbier* than in the photos.

**2. Unfair and mean.** (подлый, некрасивый (о поступке))

3. That was *shabby* treatment after twenty years on the job.
4. It's a *shabby* way to treat a friend.

---

### shaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪdɪd/ &middot; *SHAY-did*
**Русский:** затенённый, в тени
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *shade*

Protected from direct sunlight.

1. We found a *shaded* spot for the picnic.
2. The path is *shaded* most of the way up.
3. The *shaded* seats at the ballpark sell out first.

---

### shadowy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃædoʊi/ &middot; *SHAD-oh-ee*
**Русский:** тёмный, полный теней; таинственный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shadowy*, *most shadowy*

Shadowy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of shadows and hard to see into.** (тёмный, полный теней)

1. A *shadowy* corridor led down to the basement.
2. The room stays *shadowy* even at noon.

**2. Mysterious and secretive.** (таинственный, теневой)

3. A *shadowy* figure was standing by the gate.
4. The money came from a *shadowy* offshore fund.

---

### shady

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪdi/ &middot; *SHAY-dee*
**Русский:** тенистый; сомнительный, тёмный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shadier*, *shadiest*

Shady has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sheltered from the sun; giving shade.** (тенистый)

1. We sat on a *shady* bench by the river.
2. The north side of the yard is *shadier*.

**2. Informal: probably dishonest.** (сомнительный, тёмный (о деле))

3. That deal sounds *shady* to me.
4. He made his money in some *shady* business out west.

---

### shaggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃæɡi/ &middot; *SHAG-ee*
**Русский:** лохматый, косматый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shaggier*, *shaggiest*

Covered with long, thick, untidy hair.

1. A *shaggy* dog followed us all the way home.
2. His hair was long and *shaggy*.
3. The pony's coat gets *shaggier* every winter.

---

### shaken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪkən/ &middot; *SHAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** потрясённый, выбитый из колеи
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shaken*, *most shaken*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Contrast:** *Shaken* is how a person feels after a shock; *shaky* describes hands, a voice or anything unsteady.

Upset and unsteady after a shock.

1. She was badly *shaken* by the accident.
2. He looked *shaken* when he came out of the office.
3. The driver was *more shaken* than hurt.

---

### shaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪki/ &middot; *SHAY-kee*
**Русский:** дрожащий; шаткий; ненадёжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shakier*, *shakiest*

Shaky has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Trembling, and hard to control.** (дрожащий)

1. My hands were *shaky* after the second coffee.
2. He answered in a *shaky* voice.

**2. Not firm or steady.** (шаткий, неустойчивый)

3. Don't put the vase on that *shaky* table.
4. The ladder felt *shakier* the higher I went.

**3. Weak and not to be relied on.** (ненадёжный, слабый)

5. The evidence is *shaky* at best.
6. The team got off to a *shaky* start.

---

### sham

**Pronunciation:** /ʃæm/ &middot; *SHAM*
**Русский:** фальшивый, поддельный, липовый
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Almost always before a noun. The same word is a noun: *the whole trial was a sham*.

Pretending to be real, and meant to fool people.

1. The election was a *sham* vote with one candidate.
2. They were arrested over a *sham* marriage.
3. *Sham* charities take the money and disappear.

---

### shamefaced

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪmfeɪst/ &middot; *SHAYM-fayst*
**Русский:** пристыженный, сконфуженный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shamefaced*, *most shamefaced*
**Form:** compound — *shame* + *faced*

Showing in your face that you are ashamed.

1. He gave a *shamefaced* apology and sat down.
2. She looked *shamefaced* when the teacher read her note out loud.
3. The *shamefaced* players walked off the field.

---

### shameful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪmfəl/ &middot; *SHAYM-fuhl*
**Русский:** позорный, постыдный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
