**Comparison:** more/most — *more shameful*, *most shameful*
**Form:** -ful from *shame*
**Contrast:** *Shameful* describes the act; *ashamed* describes the person: *a shameful lie*, but *he felt ashamed*.

So bad that the people responsible should be ashamed.

1. It was a *shameful* way to end a long career.
2. Conditions in the camp are *shameful*.
3. Nothing was *more shameful* than the cover-up that followed.

---

### shameless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪmləs/ &middot; *SHAYM-luhs*
**Русский:** бесстыдный, наглый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shameless*, *most shameless*
**Form:** -less from *shame*

Doing something bad or embarrassing without any shame at all.

1. That was a *shameless* plug for his own book.
2. *Shameless* lying got him through the interview.
3. She is *shameless* about asking for favors.

---

### shapeless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪpləs/ &middot; *SHAYP-luhs*
**Русский:** бесформенный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shapeless*, *most shapeless*
**Form:** -less from *shape*

Having no clear shape or form.

1. She wore a *shapeless* gray sweater.
2. The clay was still a *shapeless* lump.
3. His argument was *shapeless* and hard to follow.

---

### shapely

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃeɪpli/ &middot; *SHAYP-lee*
**Русский:** стройный, с красивыми формами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *shapelier*, *shapeliest*; *more shapely*, *most shapely*
**Form:** -ly adjective, from *shape*
**Contrast:** An adjective, though it ends in *-ly*: *shapely legs*, never *she walked shapely*.

Having an attractive shape.

1. The dancer had long, *shapely* legs.
2. A *shapely* vase stood on the mantel.
3. The hedge is *shapelier* since the gardener came.

---

### shared

**Pronunciation:** /ʃɛrd/ &middot; *SHAIRD*
**Русский:** общий, совместный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Used, owned or felt by two or more people.

1. We have a *shared* bathroom down the hall.
2. A *shared* interest in old movies brought them together.
3. Their *shared* history makes every argument worse.

---

### sharp

**Pronunciation:** /ʃɑːrp/ &middot; *SHARP*
**Русский:** острый; резкий; сообразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sharper*, *sharpest*
**Opposite:** *blunt*; *dull*
**Contrast:** *Sharp* is also an adverb in time phrases — *at seven o'clock sharp* — and in music a *sharp* note is slightly too high.

Sharp has 6 distinct senses.

**1. Having an edge or a point that cuts easily.** (острый (о ноже, крае))

1. Careful — that knife is *sharp*.
2. Use the *sharpest* scissors in the drawer.
3. He cut his hand on a *sharp* piece of metal.

**2. Sudden and large, of a change or a turn.** (резкий (о повороте, изменении))

4. There's a *sharp* turn at the bottom of the hill.
5. The city saw a *sharp* rise in rents.
6. Prices took a *sharp* drop in May.

**3. Quick to notice or understand things.** (сообразительный, острый (об уме))

7. She still has a *sharp* mind at ninety.
8. His *sharp* eyes caught the typo on page one.
9. He's *sharper* than anyone else in the room.

**4. Sudden and severe, of a pain.** (резкий, острый (о боли))

10. I felt a *sharp* pain in my side.
11. A *sharp* stab of cold air hit us at the door.

**5. Harsh and critical.** (резкий, колкий (о словах, тоне))

12. Her tone got *sharp* toward the end.
13. There was a *sharp* exchange at the school board meeting.

**6. Strong and slightly bitter, of a taste or smell.** (острый, резкий (о вкусе, запахе))

14. The recipe calls for *sharp* cheddar.
15. The wine has a *sharp* finish.

---

### sharp-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɑːrpˌaɪd/ &middot; *SHARP-yd*
**Русский:** зоркий, наблюдательный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sharp-eyed*, *most sharp-eyed*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Good at noticing small details.

1. A *sharp-eyed* reader spotted the error.
2. You have to be *sharp-eyed* to find the bird in that photo.
3. A *more sharp-eyed* editor would have caught it.

---

### shattered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃætərd/ &middot; *SHAT-urd*
**Русский:** разбитый вдребезги; раздавленный (морально)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Contrast:** In British English *shattered* also means exhausted. Americans say *wiped out* for that.

Shattered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Broken into many small pieces.** (разбитый вдребезги)

1. *Shattered* glass covered the sidewalk.
2. He swept up the *shattered* plate.

**2. Deeply upset, with your life or hopes broken.** (раздавленный, убитый горем)

3. She was *shattered* by the news.
4. The family is still *shattered* a year later.

---

### shattering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃætərɪŋ/ &middot; *SHAT-ur-ing*
**Русский:** сокрушительный, ошеломляющий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shattering*, *most shattering*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the shock

So shocking or destructive that it breaks something apart.

1. The verdict was a *shattering* blow to the family.
2. *Shattering* news came late that night.
3. There was a *shattering* crash from the kitchen.

---

### shatterproof

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃætərpruːf/ &middot; *SHAT-ur-proof*
**Русский:** небьющийся, ударопрочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *shatter* + *-proof*

Made so that it will not break into pieces.

1. The lenses in these glasses are *shatterproof*.
2. *Shatterproof* glass keeps the kids safe.
3. We bought *shatterproof* ornaments after the cat arrived.

---

### shaved

**Pronunciation:** /ʃeɪvd/ &middot; *SHAYVD*
**Русский:** бритый; тонко нарезанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Shaved has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With the hair cut off close to the skin.** (бритый)

1. He has a *shaved* head and a gray beard.
2. Her legs were freshly *shaved*.

**2. Cut into very thin slices.** (тонко нарезанный, строганый)

3. *Shaved* ice with syrup is summer around here.
4. The salad comes with *shaved* Parmesan on top.

---

### sheepish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃiːpɪʃ/ &middot; *SHEE-pish*
**Русский:** смущённый, сконфуженный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sheepish*, *most sheepish*
**Form:** -ish from *sheep*

Embarrassed because you have done something silly or wrong.

1. He gave me a *sheepish* grin and apologized.
2. She looked *sheepish* about forgetting my birthday.
3. The *sheepish* look on his face said everything.

---

### shelled

**Pronunciation:** /ʃɛld/ &middot; *SHELD*
**Русский:** очищенный от скорлупы (шелухи)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In *hard-shelled* and *soft-shelled* the word means the opposite — having a shell of that kind.

With the shell taken off.

1. The recipe calls for a cup of *shelled* walnuts.
2. Buy *shelled* pistachios if you're in a hurry.
3. *Shelled* peas cook in five minutes.

---

### sheltered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɛltərd/ &middot; *SHEL-turd*
**Русский:** защищённый, укрытый; тепличный (о воспитании)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sheltered*, *most sheltered*
**Takes:** *from* + noun

Sheltered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Protected from wind, rain or sun.** (защищённый, укрытый)

1. We ate lunch in a *sheltered* corner of the yard.
2. The bay is *sheltered* from the north wind.

**2. Kept away from anything difficult or unpleasant in life.** (тепличный, оберегаемый)

3. She had a *sheltered* childhood in a small town.
4. He's led a *more sheltered* life than he admits.

---

### shifting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪftɪŋ/ &middot; *SHIF-ting*
**Русский:** меняющийся, непостоянный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *shift*

Constantly moving or changing.

1. *Shifting* sands buried the road overnight.
2. The *shifting* politics of the region make planning hard.
3. Their *shifting* stories made the police suspicious.

---

### shiftless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪftləs/ &middot; *SHIFT-luhs*
**Русский:** ленивый, никчёмный, безынициативный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shiftless*, *most shiftless*
**Form:** -less from an old sense of *shift*, meaning effort

Lazy, with no ambition to do anything.

1. He called his nephew *shiftless* and lazy.
2. A *shiftless* crew showed up an hour late.
3. The main character is a *shiftless* dreamer who finishes nothing.

---

### shifty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪfti/ &middot; *SHIF-tee*
**Русский:** скользкий, подозрительный, бегающий (о взгляде)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shiftier*, *shiftiest*

Looking or sounding dishonest.

1. He had *shifty* eyes and a nervous laugh.
2. The salesman seemed *shifty* from the first minute.
3. The longer he talked, the *shiftier* he sounded.

---

### shimmery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪməri/ &middot; *SHIM-uh-ree*
**Русский:** мерцающий, переливающийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shimmery*, *most shimmery*
**Form:** -y from *shimmer*

Shining with a soft, moving light.

1. She wore a *shimmery* blue dress.
2. The lake looked *shimmery* in the heat.
3. This eyeshadow is *more shimmery* than I expected.

---

### shining

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃaɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *SHY-ning*
**Русский:** сияющий, блестящий; блестящий (об образце)
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both — the *excellent* meaning only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Shining* often carries approval — *a shining example*. *Shiny* only describes a surface: *shiny shoes*.

Shining has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Giving out or reflecting light.** (сияющий, блестящий)

1. Her eyes were *shining* with tears.
2. A *shining* moon lit the whole road.

**2. Excellent; standing out from everything around it.** (блестящий, выдающийся)

3. She's a *shining* example of how to do this job.
4. That was the one *shining* moment of the season.

---

### shiny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃaɪni/ &middot; *SHY-nee*
**Русский:** блестящий, глянцевый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shinier*, *shiniest*
**Opposite:** *dull*; *matte*

Smooth and bright, reflecting the light.

1. His shoes were black and *shiny*.
2. The new coin is *shinier* than the old one.
3. Kids grab whatever is *shiniest* on the shelf.

---

### shipboard

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪpbɔːrd/ &middot; *SHIP-bord*
**Русский:** судовой, корабельный (происходящий на борту)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *ship* + *board*

Happening or used on a ship.

1. They met during a *shipboard* romance.
2. *Shipboard* life takes some getting used to.
3. The crew ran *shipboard* drills every morning.

---

### shipshape

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪpʃeɪp/ &middot; *SHIP-shayp*
**Русский:** в полном порядке, аккуратный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *ship* + *shape*

Neat, clean and in good order.

1. The garage is finally *shipshape*.
2. She left the kitchen *shipshape*.
3. Everything on deck was *shipshape* before we sailed.

---

### shitty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪti/ &middot; *SHIT-ee*
**Русский:** паршивый, дерьмовый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shittier*, *shittiest*
**Contrast:** Vulgar. Fine among friends, never at work or in writing — use *lousy* or *crummy* there.

Very bad, or very unkind.

1. We had a *shitty* week.
2. That was a *shitty* thing to say to her.
3. The second hotel was even *shittier* than the first.

---

### shoddy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɑːdi/ &middot; *SHAH-dee*
**Русский:** халтурный, некачественный; подлый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shoddier*, *shoddiest*

Shoddy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Badly made or badly done.** (халтурный, некачественный)

1. They used *shoddy* materials and the roof leaked by spring.
2. The second contractor's work was even *shoddier*.

**2. Unfair and dishonest.** (подлый, недостойный)

3. That's *shoddy* treatment of a loyal customer.
4. He gave a *shoddy* excuse for breaking his promise.

---

### shoeless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃuːləs/ &middot; *SHOO-luhs*
**Русский:** босой, без обуви
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *shoe*

Wearing no shoes.

1. *Shoeless* kids ran across the hot sand.
2. He answered the door *shoeless* and half asleep.
3. Guests arrive *shoeless* and leave their boots by the door.

---

### shopworn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɑːpwɔːrn/ &middot; *SHAHP-worn*
**Русский:** залежалый, потёртый; избитый (о фразе)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shopworn*, *most shopworn*
**Form:** compound — *shop* + *worn*

Shopworn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dirty or damaged from sitting on display in a store.** (залежалый, потёртый)

1. The last box was *shopworn* and marked down.
2. They sell *shopworn* furniture at half price.

**2. No longer fresh or original.** (избитый, затасканный)

3. The speech was full of *shopworn* phrases.
4. That argument is *shopworn* by now.

---

### shorn

**Pronunciation:** /ʃɔːrn/ &middot; *SHORN*
**Русский:** остриженный; лишённый (чего-либо)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun — *shorn of power*
**Form:** past participle of *shear*

Shorn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cut very short, of hair or wool.** (остриженный)

1. A *shorn* sheep looks half its size.
2. His *shorn* head made him look ten years younger.

**2. Stripped of something you used to have.** (лишённый (чего-либо))

3. The king was left *shorn* of all real power.
4. The bill passed *shorn* of its best provisions.

---

### short-haired

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈhɛrd/ &middot; *short-HAIRD*
**Русский:** короткошёрстный; коротко стриженный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *long-haired*

Having short hair or short fur.

1. A *short-haired* terrier is easier to groom.
2. She's been *short-haired* since college.
3. *Short-haired* breeds shed less in the house.

---

### short-handed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈhændɪd/ &middot; *short-HAN-did*
**Русский:** испытывающий нехватку людей
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In hockey a team is *short-handed* while a player sits in the penalty box. At an office, *short-staffed* is the everyday word.

Without enough people to do the work.

1. We're *short-handed* tonight, so expect a wait.
2. The kitchen has been *short-handed* all month.
3. They played *short-handed* for two minutes.

---

### short-lived

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈlaɪvd/ &middot; *short-LYVD*
**Русский:** недолговечный, кратковременный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Two pronunciations are standard in the US: *short-LYVD* and *short-LIVD*.

Lasting only a short time.

1. Their happiness was *short-lived*.
2. It was a *short-lived* TV show from the nineties.
3. The truce proved *short-lived*.

---

### short-order

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈɔːrdər/ &middot; *short-OR-dur*
**Русский:** быстрого приготовления (о еде в закусочной)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of simple food cooked quickly to order in a diner.

1. He worked as a *short-order* cook for years.
2. The diner has a *short-order* menu — eggs, burgers, fries.
3. *Short-order* cooking is all speed and timing.

---

### short-range

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈreɪndʒ/ &middot; *short-RAYNJ*
**Русский:** ближнего действия; краткосрочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *long-range*

Reaching only a short distance, or covering only the near future.

1. *Short-range* missiles were moved to the border.
2. The forecast is only a *short-range* guess.
3. We made *short-range* plans and left the rest open.

---

### short-run

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈrʌn/ &middot; *short-RUN*
**Русский:** краткосрочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *long-run*
**Contrast:** As an adverb phrase it splits and drops the hyphen: *in the short run*.

Having to do with the near future only.

1. *Short-run* profits look good, but the debt keeps growing.
2. The tax cut is a *short-run* fix.
3. *Short-run* costs rose while output fell.

---

### short-staffed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈstæft/ &middot; *short-STAFT*
**Русский:** испытывающий нехватку сотрудников
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Without enough employees.

1. The clinic is badly *short-staffed*.
2. We've been *short-staffed* since two nurses quit.
3. A *short-staffed* ER means long waits.

---

### short-tempered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈtɛmpərd/ &middot; *short-TEM-purd*
**Русский:** вспыльчивый, несдержанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more short-tempered*, *most short-tempered*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *even-tempered*

Quick to get angry.

1. He's *short-tempered* before his first coffee.
2. A *short-tempered* boss keeps everyone quiet.
3. She gets *more short-tempered* when she's tired.

---

### shortsighted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃɔːrtˈsaɪtɪd/ &middot; *short-SY-tid*
**Русский:** недальновидный; близорукий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shortsighted*, *most shortsighted*
**Contrast:** For the eye condition Americans normally say *nearsighted*; in the US *shortsighted* usually means bad planning.

Shortsighted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not thinking about what will happen later.** (недальновидный)

1. Cutting the training budget is *shortsighted*.
2. It was a *shortsighted* decision that cost them the contract.
3. Nothing is *more shortsighted* than ignoring the repairs.

**2. Unable to see distant things clearly.** (близорукий)

4. He's *shortsighted* and can't read the board without glasses.
5. She's been *shortsighted* since grade school.

---

### showy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃoʊi/ &middot; *SHOH-ee*
**Русский:** броский, показной, вычурный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *showier*, *showiest*
**Form:** -y from *show*

Attracting attention, often too much of it.

1. The ring is a little *showy* for the office.
2. *Showy* red flowers fill the front bed.
3. His second car is even *showier* than the first.

---

### shredded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃrɛdɪd/ &middot; *SHRED-id*
**Русский:** измельчённый, натёртый; изорванный в клочья
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *shred*

Cut or torn into thin strips.

1. Add a cup of *shredded* cheese on top.
2. *Shredded* documents filled three garbage bags.
3. The lettuce comes *shredded* in a bag.

---

### shrewd

**Pronunciation:** /ʃruːd/ &middot; *SHROOD*
**Русский:** проницательный, расчётливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shrewder*, *shrewdest*

Good at judging people and situations, and at using that to your advantage.

1. She's a *shrewd* negotiator.
2. That was a *shrewd* guess.
3. He's *shrewder* about money than his brother.

---

### shrill

**Pronunciation:** /ʃrɪl/ &middot; *SHRIL*
**Русский:** пронзительный, резкий; крикливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *shriller*, *shrillest*

Shrill has 2 distinct senses.

**1. High and unpleasantly loud.** (пронзительный, резкий (о звуке))

1. A *shrill* whistle cut through the noise.
2. Her voice got *shriller* as she got angrier.

**2. Loud and angry in a way that stops people listening.** (крикливый, истеричный)

3. The debate turned *shrill* within ten minutes.
4. *Shrill* attacks from both sides drowned out the facts.

---

### shriveled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃrɪvəld/ &middot; *SHRIV-uhld*
**Русский:** сморщенный, высохший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shriveled*, *most shriveled*
**Form:** -ed from *shrivel*
**Contrast:** American spelling keeps one *l*: *shriveled*, *shriveling*. The British spelling *shrivelled* is common in books printed in the UK.

Dried out and covered in wrinkles.

1. I found two *shriveled* apples at the back of the drawer.
2. The flowers on the windowsill were brown and *shriveled*.
3. Without water the seedlings looked *more shriveled* every morning.

---

### shrunken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃrʌŋkən/ &middot; *SHRUNG-kuhn*
**Русский:** съёжившийся, усохший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more shrunken*, *most shrunken*
**Form:** an old past participle of *shrink*
**Contrast:** The verb's ordinary past participle is *shrunk* (*my sweater has shrunk*); *shrunken* is the adjective: *a shrunken sweater*.

Smaller than it used to be, often in a way that looks wrong.

1. He pulled a *shrunken* wool sweater out of the dryer.
2. Her grandfather seemed *shrunken* after the illness.
3. The company is trying to run on a *shrunken* budget.

---

### shut

**Pronunciation:** /ʃʌt/ &middot; *SHUT*
**Русский:** закрытый
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** predicative only — *the door is shut*; before a noun use *closed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *open*
**Contrast:** Don't say *a shut door* — before a noun Americans say *a closed door*. The verb is the same in all three forms: *shut*, *shut*, *shut*.

Shut has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Closed, not open.** (закрытый)

1. Make sure the windows are *shut* before you leave.
2. Her eyes were tightly *shut*.
3. The lid won't stay *shut*.

**2. Not open for business.** (закрытый (не работает))

4. The pharmacy is *shut* on Sundays.
5. By ten the whole town is *shut*.

---

### shuttered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃʌtərd/ &middot; *SHUT-urd*
**Русский:** закрытый ставнями; закрывшийся навсегда
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *shutter*

Shuttered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Closed down, out of business for good.** (закрывшийся, заколоченный)

1. The main street is lined with *shuttered* stores.
2. They bought a *shuttered* factory and turned it into lofts.
3. The restaurant has been *shuttered* since March.

**2. With the shutters closed over the windows.** (закрытый ставнями)

4. The house sat dark and *shuttered* all winter.
5. We walked past rows of *shuttered* windows.

---

### Siamese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaɪəˈmiːz/ &middot; *sy-uh-MEEZ*
**Русский:** сиамский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Siam* is the old name of Thailand, so the modern word for the country is *Thai*. *Siamese* survives mainly in *Siamese cat*; for *Siamese twins* doctors now say *conjoined twins*.

Of Siam, the old name of Thailand — now used mostly in fixed phrases.

1. They have two *Siamese* cats with bright blue eyes.
2. The museum has a room of *Siamese* art.
3. My grandmother still calls conjoined twins *Siamese* twins.

---

### Siberian

**Pronunciation:** /saɪˈbɪriən/ &middot; *sy-BEER-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** сибирский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Siberian* when it means 'bitterly cold'
**Contrast:** The person is the noun *a Siberian*. Americans often use it loosely for extreme cold: *Siberian temperatures* in Chicago.

Of or from Siberia.

1. A *Siberian* husky needs a lot of exercise.
2. The cold front brought *Siberian* air all the way to Texas.
3. Her family is *Siberian*, from a village near Irkutsk.

---

### Sicilian

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈsɪljən/ &middot; *sih-SIL-yuhn*
**Русский:** сицилийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The person is the noun *a Sicilian*. Sicily is part of Italy, so someone can be both *Italian* and *Sicilian*.

Of or from Sicily.

1. This is my great-aunt's *Sicilian* recipe for eggplant.
2. The pizza place does a thick *Sicilian* crust.
3. Both of his parents are *Sicilian*.

---

### sickening

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪkənɪŋ/ &middot; *SIK-uh-ning*
**Русский:** отвратительный; тошнотворный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sickening*, *most sickening*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

Sickening has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So bad or cruel that it shocks and disgusts you.** (отвратительный, возмутительный)

1. It was *sickening* to watch him blame the kids.
2. The details that came out in court were *sickening*.
3. There is something *sickening* about that much waste.

**2. Making you feel physically ill.** (тошнотворный)

4. A *sickening* smell of gas filled the hallway.
5. The car hit the guardrail with a *sickening* thud.

---

### sickly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪkli/ &middot; *SIK-lee*
**Русский:** болезненный; приторный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *sicklier*, *sickliest*; also *more sickly*, *most sickly*
**Form:** -ly from *sick*
**Opposite:** *healthy*
**Contrast:** Despite the *-ly*, *sickly* is an adjective, not an adverb. It is not the same as *sick*: a *sick* child is ill today, a *sickly* child is ill again and again.

Sickly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Often ill and not strong.** (болезненный, хилый)

1. He was a *sickly* kid who missed half of third grade.
2. The kitten was the *sickliest* of the litter.
3. She looked pale and *sickly* after the flight.

**2. Unpleasant in a weak, too-sweet or washed-out way.** (приторный; нездорового оттенка)

4. The waiting room was painted a *sickly* green.
5. The punch had a *sickly* sweet taste.

---

### sidesplitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaɪdˌsplɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *SYD-split-ing*
**Русский:** уморительный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sidesplitting*, *most sidesplitting*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle, from the idea of laughing until your sides hurt
**Contrast:** Also written *side-splitting*. It is strong praise, so save it for something truly funny.

Extremely funny.

1. His impression of the principal was *sidesplitting*.
2. We watched a *sidesplitting* comedy about a family road trip.
3. The second act is *more sidesplitting* than the first.

---

### sideways

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaɪdweɪz/ &middot; *SYD-wayz*
**Русский:** боковой, направленный вбок
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a sideways glance*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *sideways* is much more often an adverb (*he stepped sideways*, *turn it sideways*). As an adjective it goes only before a noun.

Pointing or facing toward the side.

1. She gave him a *sideways* look and said nothing.
2. The crab made a quick *sideways* move.
3. He answered with a *sideways* nod toward the door.

---

### Sierra Leonean

**Pronunciation:** /siˌɛrə liˈoʊniən/ &middot; *see-AIR-uh lee-OH-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** сьерра-леонский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Two words, both capitalized, and the person is the noun *a Sierra Leonean*. The country is *Sierra Leone*, with no *-an*.

Of or from Sierra Leone.

1. The *Sierra Leonean* team qualified for the first time.
2. She writes about *Sierra Leonean* music for a magazine.
3. Our new neighbors are *Sierra Leonean*.

---

### signed

**Pronunciation:** /saɪnd/ &middot; *SYND*
**Русский:** подписанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sign*
**Opposite:** *unsigned*
**Contrast:** The *g* is silent: SYND, one syllable.

With a signature on it.

1. Bring a *signed* copy of the lease to the office.
2. The contract isn't *signed* yet.
3. He keeps a *signed* baseball on his desk.

---

### significant

**Pronunciation:** /sɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/ &middot; *sig-NIF-ih-kuhnt*
**Русский:** значительный; значимый
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more significant*, *most significant*
**Form:** -ant from *signify*
**Opposite:** *insignificant*
**Contrast:** In research *statistically significant* means only that the result is unlikely to be chance — not that it is large.

Significant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large or important enough to matter.** (значительный, существенный)

1. There has been a *significant* drop in traffic deaths.
2. The repair will cost a *significant* amount of money.
3. This is the *most significant* change to the law in thirty years.

**2. Carrying a meaning that is not said out loud.** (многозначительный)

4. She gave her husband a *significant* look across the table.
5. It is *significant* that he never mentioned the money.

---

### Sikh

**Pronunciation:** /siːk/ &middot; *SEEK*
**Русский:** сикхский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Pronounced SEEK, like the verb *seek*. It is also the noun for a member of the religion (*a Sikh*); the religion itself is *Sikhism*.

Of the Sikh religion, which began in the Punjab, or of its followers.

1. There is a *Sikh* temple on the edge of town.
2. Many *Sikh* men wear a turban.
3. His family is *Sikh*.

---

### silent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaɪlənt/ &middot; *SY-luhnt*
**Русский:** молчаливый; беззвучный; немой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more silent*, *most silent*
**Takes:** *about* or *on* + noun, in the 'saying nothing' sense
**Opposite:** *noisy*

Silent has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Making no sound at all.** (беззвучный, тихий)

1. The house was *silent* except for the clock.
2. The new dishwasher is almost completely *silent*.
3. We stood in a *silent* line outside the door.

**2. Saying nothing, especially about something people expect you to speak about.** (молчаливый, умалчивающий)

4. The company has been *silent* about the layoffs.
5. He stayed *silent* for the whole meeting.

**3. Not pronounced, or made without recorded sound.** (немой (о букве, о фильме))

6. The *b* in 'climb' is *silent*.
7. She wrote her thesis on *silent* films.

---

### silky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪlki/ &middot; *SIL-kee*
**Русский:** шелковистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *silkier*, *silkiest*
**Form:** -y from *silk*

Silky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Soft and smooth to the touch, like silk.** (шелковистый)

1. The puppy has *silky* black ears.
2. This shampoo leaves your hair *silkier*.
3. She ran her hand over the *silky* fabric.

**2. Smooth and pleasant in sound or taste.** (бархатистый, мягкий)

4. He has a *silky* radio voice.
5. The sauce was rich and *silky*.

---

### silver-tongued

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪlvərˈtʌŋd/ &middot; *sil-vur-TUNGD*
**Русский:** красноречивый, сладкоречивый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more silver-tongued*, *most silver-tongued*
**Form:** compound — noun + noun + *-ed*
**Contrast:** It is half a compliment: a *silver-tongued* speaker is smooth, and often a little too smooth to trust.

Able to talk people into things with charming, persuasive speech.

1. A *silver-tongued* lawyer got the whole case thrown out.
2. He was the *silver-tongued* one; his brother just nodded.
3. Even the *most silver-tongued* salesman couldn't move that car.

---

### silvery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪlvəri/ &middot; *SIL-vuh-ree*
**Русский:** серебристый
**Type:** color; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more silvery*, *most silvery*
**Form:** -y from *silver*

Silvery has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shining pale gray, like silver.** (серебристый)

1. The lake looked *silvery* under the moon.
2. He has a full head of *silvery* hair.
3. The fish flashed *silvery* in the net.

**2. Clear and light in sound.** (серебристый (о звуке))

4. Her *silvery* laugh carried across the yard.
5. Somewhere below, a *silvery* bell rang twice.

---

### simple-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪmpəlˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *sim-puhl-MYN-did*
**Русский:** недалёкий; упрощённый
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more simple-minded*, *most simple-minded*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Contrast:** Used about a person it is an insult, and about someone with a disability it is offensive; say *has a learning disability* instead.

Simple-minded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too simple to deal with something complicated.** (упрощённый, примитивный)

1. That is a *simple-minded* answer to a hard question.
2. Their plan is *simple-minded*: build more parking and hope.

**2. Not clever, and easy to fool.** (недалёкий, простоватый)

3. The movie treats its audience as *simple-minded*.
4. He plays a sweet, *simple-minded* farmhand.

---

### simplified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪmpləfaɪd/ &middot; *SIM-pluh-fyd*
**Русский:** упрощённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more simplified*, *most simplified*
**Form:** -ed from *simplify*

Made easier to understand or to use, with the hard parts taken out.

1. The bank sent out a *simplified* version of the contract.
2. We read a *simplified* edition of the novel in class.
3. The new form is much *more simplified* than the old one.

---

### simplistic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪmˈplɪstɪk/ &middot; *sim-PLIS-tik*
**Русский:** упрощенческий, примитивный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more simplistic*, *most simplistic*
**Form:** -istic from *simple*
**Contrast:** Not a fancy word for *simple*. *Simple* is usually good (*a simple plan*); *simplistic* is always a criticism — it means too simple to be true.

Treating something complicated as if it were easy, and so getting it wrong.

1. That's a *simplistic* view of what caused the war.
2. His answer was *simplistic*, and the reporters said so.
3. Blaming one department is the *most simplistic* explanation there is.

---

### simulated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪmjəleɪtɪd/ &middot; *SIM-yuh-lay-tid*
**Русский:** имитированный; смоделированный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *simulate*
**Opposite:** *real*

Simulated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made by a computer or a machine to copy real conditions.** (смоделированный, имитирующий реальность)

1. Pilots train in a *simulated* landing before they ever fly.
2. The lab ran a *simulated* power outage.
3. Students take a *simulated* exam in the first week.

**2. Made to look like a more expensive material.** (искусственный, поддельный)

4. The seats are *simulated* leather.
5. She wore a ring with a *simulated* diamond.

---

### simultaneous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaɪməlˈteɪniəs/ &middot; *sy-muhl-TAY-nee-uhs*
**Русский:** одновременный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun; after a verb use *at the same time*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ous from Latin *simul*, 'at the same time'
**Contrast:** Americans say sy-muhl-TAY-nee-uhs; the British start it sim-. The adverb is *simultaneously*, and it is what you usually need after a verb.

Happening at exactly the same time as something else.

1. The band played *simultaneous* shows in three cities by video.
2. The conference offers *simultaneous* translation into four languages.
3. The launch was *simultaneous* with the announcement in Tokyo.

---

### sincere

**Pronunciation:** /sɪnˈsɪr/ &middot; *sin-SEER*
**Русский:** искренний
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sincere*, *most sincere*
**Takes:** *about* or *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *insincere*
**Contrast:** The fixed phrases keep an old superlative: *my sincerest apologies*, *sincerest thanks*. Letters end *Sincerely*, not *Sincere*.

Really meaning what you say, with nothing put on.

1. Her apology sounded *sincere*.
2. He is *sincere* about wanting to help.
3. Of all the speeches that night, hers was the *most sincere*.

---

### sinful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪnfəl/ &middot; *SIN-fuhl*
**Русский:** греховный; порочный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sinful*, *most sinful*
**Form:** -ful from *sin*

Sinful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Morally wrong, especially in a religious sense.** (греховный, грешный)

1. The sermon called gossip *sinful*.
2. He grew up in a house where dancing was *sinful*.
3. They treat waste as *more sinful* than anything else.

**2. So rich or enjoyable that it feels like cheating — used as a joke.** (греховно вкусный, недопустимо приятный)

4. That chocolate cake is downright *sinful*.
5. A nap at two in the afternoon feels *sinful*.

---

### Singaporean

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋəˈpɔːriən/ &middot; *sing-uh-POR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** сингапурский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The person is the noun *a Singaporean*. The country is *Singapore*, with no *-an*.

Of or from Singapore.

1. We had *Singaporean* noodles at a stand downtown.
2. The fund is run by a *Singaporean* bank.
3. Her husband is *Singaporean*.

---

### single

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪŋɡəl/ &middot; *SING-guhl*
**Русский:** один, единственный; холостой; одноместный
**Type:** quantity; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a single mistake*; but *she is single* in the 'not married' sense
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *a single ticket* in the US means one ticket; the British one-way ticket is a *one-way* in American English.

Single has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Only one, and no more — often for emphasis.** (единственный, один-единственный)

1. Not a *single* person showed up.
2. He answered every *single* question.
3. One *single* mistake cost them the game.

**2. Not married and not in a relationship.** (холостой, незамужняя)

4. She's been *single* since the divorce.
5. It's a hard schedule for a *single* parent.

**3. Meant for one person.** (одноместный, односпальный)

6. The room has two *single* beds.
7. I booked a *single* room for Thursday night.

---

### single-breasted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋɡəlˈbrɛstɪd/ &middot; *sing-guhl-BRES-tid*
**Русский:** однобортный
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *double-breasted*

Of a jacket or coat: closing with one row of buttons.

1. He wore a plain *single-breasted* navy suit.
2. The jacket is *single-breasted*, with two buttons.
3. A *single-breasted* coat is easier to wear open.

---

### single-celled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋɡəlˈsɛld/ &middot; *sing-guhl-SELD*
**Русский:** одноклеточный
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Contrast:** The scientific word is *unicellular*; *single-celled* is what a textbook or a teacher says.

Made of only one cell.

1. Bacteria are *single-celled* organisms.
2. Life on Earth was *single-celled* for billions of years.
3. We looked at a *single-celled* alga under the microscope.

---

### single-handed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋɡəlˈhændɪd/ &middot; *sing-guhl-HAN-did*
**Русский:** в одиночку совершённый, единоличный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a single-handed effort*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Contrast:** After a verb use the adverb: *she did it single-handedly*, not *she did it single-handed*.

Done by one person with no help.

1. His *single-handed* rescue of the crew made the news.
2. It was a *single-handed* sail across the Atlantic.
3. The report credits her with a *single-handed* turnaround.

---

### single-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋɡəlˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *sing-guhl-MYN-did*
**Русский:** целеустремлённый, сосредоточенный на одном
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more single-minded*, *most single-minded*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *in* + *-ing*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Giving all your attention to one goal and nothing else.

1. She is *single-minded* about getting into medical school.
2. It took *single-minded* work to finish the bridge on time.
3. He is the *most single-minded* person on the team.

---

### single-spaced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪŋɡəlˈspeɪst/ &middot; *sing-guhl-SPAYST*
**Русский:** напечатанный через один интервал
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *double-spaced*

Typed with no blank line between the lines of text.

1. He handed in four *single-spaced* pages.
2. The résumé should be *single-spaced*.
3. A *single-spaced* letter is hard to mark up.

---

### singular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪŋɡjələr/ &middot; *SING-gyuh-lur*
**Русский:** единственного числа; исключительный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *plural*
**Contrast:** The grammar sense is the everyday one. The 'remarkable' sense is formal and goes before a noun: *a singular talent*.

Singular has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In grammar: naming one, not more than one.** (единственного числа)

1. 'Child' is *singular* and 'children' is plural.
2. The verb has to agree with a *singular* subject.
3. *Singular* 'they' is now standard in American writing.

**2. Unusual and remarkable — formal.** (исключительный, необыкновенный)

4. She has a *singular* gift for calming a room.
5. The building is a *singular* piece of American design.

---

### sinister

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪnəstər/ &middot; *SIN-uh-stur*
**Русский:** зловещий
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sinister*, *most sinister*
**Opposite:** *harmless*

Giving the feeling that something evil or harmful is going on.

1. There was something *sinister* about the empty parking garage.
2. He plays a quiet, *sinister* landlord.
3. The second letter was *more sinister* than the first.

---

### sisterly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪstərli/ &middot; *SIS-tur-lee*
**Русский:** сестринский
**Type:** classifying; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sisterly*, *most sisterly*
**Form:** -ly from *sister*
**Contrast:** Despite the *-ly*, this is an adjective, not an adverb. The male pair is *brotherly*.

Of or like a sister — warm, protective and close.

1. She gave him a *sisterly* pat on the shoulder.
2. There is a *sisterly* feeling between the two teams.
3. That was the *most sisterly* thing anyone did for me all year.

---

### Sisyphean

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪsəˈfiːən/ &middot; *sis-uh-FEE-uhn*
**Русский:** сизифов, бесконечный и бесплодный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *Sisyphus*, the Greek king made to roll a rock uphill forever
**Contrast:** Capitalized, because it comes from a name. Russian has the same image: *сизифов труд*.

Endless and hopeless, because the work undoes itself.

1. Keeping the kitchen clean with three kids is a *Sisyphean* task.
2. Answering that inbox is *Sisyphean* labor.
3. He described the cleanup as a *Sisyphean* effort with no end date.

---

### sitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *SIT-ing*
**Русский:** действующий (о должностном лице); сидячий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *the sitting president*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *The president is sitting* is the verb, not this adjective. Note the idiom *a sitting duck* — an easy target. *Sitting room* for *living room* is chiefly British.

Sitting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Holding the office right now.** (действующий, нынешний)

1. No *sitting* senator has lost that seat in forty years.
2. The *sitting* judge recused herself.
3. It is rare for a *sitting* mayor to be challenged in the primary.

**2. Done in a seated position.** (сидячий)

4. Measure the chair from the floor in a *sitting* position.
5. The stretch can be done from a *sitting* pose.

---

### situated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪtʃueɪtɪd/ &middot; *SICH-oo-ay-tid*
**Русский:** расположенный; находящийся в положении
**Type:** participial
**Position:** after the noun — *a house situated on the lake*; also after *be*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** a place phrase: *on*, *in*, *near* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *situate*
**Contrast:** Don't put it before a noun — not *a situated house*. In everyday speech *located* or plain *is* sounds less formal: *The hotel is on the beach*.

Situated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Standing in a particular place — formal, common in ads and reports.** (расположенный)

1. The cabin is *situated* on twelve acres of woods.
2. We stayed at a hotel *situated* right on the beach.
3. The plant is *situated* between two rivers.

**2. In a particular position, especially about money — with *well* or *badly*.** (находящийся в (хорошем, плохом) положении)

4. After the sale they were comfortably *situated*.
5. Small farms are badly *situated* to survive a dry year.

---

### sixtieth

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪkstiəθ/ &middot; *SIKS-tee-uhth*
**Русский:** шестидесятый
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — *her sixtieth birthday*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -eth from *sixty*
**Contrast:** *sixty* counts, *sixtieth* puts in order: *sixty years* but *the sixtieth year*. It is also the noun for the fraction 1/60: *a sixtieth of a second*.

Coming after fifty-nine others in a series.

1. We threw a party for my mother's *sixtieth* birthday.
2. This is the museum's *sixtieth* year.
3. She was the *sixtieth* caller and won the tickets.

---

### sizable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaɪzəbəl/ &middot; *SY-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** значительный, немалый
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sizable*, *most sizable*
**Form:** -able from *size*
**Contrast:** American writing drops the *e*: *sizable*. The spelling *sizeable* is usual in Britain.

Fairly large.

1. They left a *sizable* tip.
2. A *sizable* crowd waited outside the courthouse.
3. The grant covers a *sizable* part of the cost.

---

### sizzling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪzlɪŋ/ &middot; *SIZ-ling*
**Русский:** шипящий (на сковороде); раскалённый; захватывающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sizzling*, *most sizzling*
**Form:** -ing from *sizzle*

Sizzling has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Cooking with the hissing sound of hot fat.** (шипящий, скворчащий)

1. The waiter brought out a *sizzling* plate of fajitas.
2. Bacon was *sizzling* in the pan when I came downstairs.

**2. Extremely hot.** (раскалённый, обжигающий)

3. We crossed the *sizzling* parking lot in flip-flops.
4. The forecast calls for another *sizzling* weekend.

**3. Exciting, full of energy or passion.** (захватывающий, жаркий)

5. The two leads have *sizzling* chemistry.
6. The band opened with a *sizzling* guitar solo.

---

### skeletal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɛlətəl/ &middot; *SKEL-uh-tuhl*
**Русский:** скелетный; истощённый; схематичный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more skeletal*, *most skeletal*
**Form:** -al from *skeleton*

Skeletal has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of the bones of the body.** (скелетный, костный)

1. The X-ray showed no *skeletal* damage.
2. The class covers the *skeletal* system.

**2. So thin that the bones show.** (истощённый, худой как скелет)

3. The dog they rescued was *skeletal*.
4. He came back from the hospital looking *skeletal*.

**3. Cut back to the bare minimum.** (минимальный, урезанный до основы)

5. The office runs on a *skeletal* staff over the holidays.
6. All I have so far is a *skeletal* outline.

---

### skeptical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɛptɪkəl/ &middot; *SKEP-tih-kuhl*
**Русский:** скептический, недоверчивый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more skeptical*, *most skeptical*
**Takes:** *about* or *of* + noun or *-ing*; *that*-clause
**Opposite:** *convinced*
**Contrast:** American spelling starts with *sk-*; the British write *sceptical*. It means doubting, not *pessimistic*.

Doubting that something is true, honest or going to work.

1. The neighbors were *skeptical* about the new parking plan.
2. He gave me a *skeptical* look.
3. Voters are *more skeptical* than they were a year ago.

---

### sketchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɛtʃi/ &middot; *SKECH-ee*
**Русский:** отрывочный; сомнительный, подозрительный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sketchier*, *sketchiest*
**Form:** -y from *sketch*
**Contrast:** The 'suspicious' sense is everyday American slang and is what you will hear most: *a sketchy guy*, *a sketchy neighborhood*.

Sketchy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not to be trusted; a little dangerous or dishonest — informal.** (подозрительный, сомнительный)

1. That website looks *sketchy* — don't put your card in.
2. We walked home through a *sketchy* part of town.
3. The deal got *sketchier* the more he explained it.

**2. Giving only a rough idea, without the details.** (отрывочный, неполный)

4. Details of the crash are still *sketchy*.
5. My memory of that summer is pretty *sketchy*.

---

### skewed

**Pronunciation:** /skjuːd/ &middot; *SKYOOD*
**Русский:** искажённый, смещённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more skewed*, *most skewed*
**Takes:** *toward* or *in favor of* + noun; *by* + noun for the cause
**Form:** -ed from *skew*

Skewed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Twisted away from a fair or true picture.** (искажённый, необъективный)

1. The results were *skewed* by two huge donations.
2. The sample is *skewed* toward older voters.
3. Their coverage is *more skewed* than they admit.

**2. Sitting at a crooked angle.** (перекошенный, скошенный)

4. The picture over the couch was *skewed*.
5. One *skewed* shelf threw off the whole wall.

---

### skillful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɪlfəl/ &middot; *SKIL-fuhl*
**Русский:** умелый, искусный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more skillful*, *most skillful*
**Takes:** *at* or *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ful from *skill*
**Opposite:** *clumsy*
**Contrast:** American spelling has two *l*'s: *skillful*. The British write *skilful*.

Doing something well, because of training and practice.

1. She is a *skillful* negotiator.
2. He is *skillful* at getting kids to talk.
3. That was the *most skillful* piece of driving I've ever seen.

---

### skim

**Pronunciation:** /skɪm/ &middot; *SKIM*
**Русский:** обезжиренный (о молоке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *skim milk*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In the US it is *skim milk*; the British say *skimmed milk*. Don't confuse it with the verb *skim* — to read something quickly.

Of milk: with the cream taken off.

1. I take my coffee with *skim* milk.
2. The recipe calls for a cup of *skim* milk.
3. Does this latte have *skim* milk in it?

---

### skimpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɪmpi/ &middot; *SKIM-pee*
**Русский:** скудный; слишком открытый (об одежде)
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *skimpier*, *skimpiest*

Skimpy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of clothes: very small, covering very little.** (открытый, куцый (об одежде))

1. She said the costume was too *skimpy* for a school play.
2. He showed up in *skimpy* running shorts.

**2. Not as much as you need or expect.** (скудный, недостаточный)

3. The portions were *skimpy* for the price.
4. This year's benefits package is *skimpier* than last year's.

---

### skin-deep

**Pronunciation:** /ˌskɪnˈdiːp/ &middot; *skin-DEEP*
**Русский:** поверхностный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** predicative only — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Contrast:** It comes from the saying *Beauty is only skin-deep*, and it is still used mostly in that shape: *only skin-deep*.

Only on the surface, not real or lasting.

1. Beauty is only *skin-deep*.
2. His politeness is *skin-deep* — watch how he treats waiters.
3. The changes at the company turned out to be *skin-deep*.

---

### skin-tight

**Pronunciation:** /ˌskɪnˈtaɪt/ &middot; *skin-TYT*
**Русский:** облегающий, в обтяжку
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Opposite:** *loose*
**Contrast:** Usually written as one word in American English: *skintight*. Both spellings are correct.

Fitting as closely as skin.

1. The cyclists wore *skin-tight* suits.
2. Her dress was black and *skin-tight*.
3. He can't stand *skin-tight* sleeves.

---

### skinny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɪni/ &middot; *SKIN-ee*
**Русский:** тощий; узкий, облегающий
**Type:** size; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *skinnier*, *skinniest*
**Opposite:** *chubby*
**Contrast:** *skinny* is blunt and can sting; *slim* and *slender* are compliments, *thin* is neutral.

Skinny has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Very thin, often too thin.** (тощий, худющий)

1. He was a *skinny* kid with big glasses.
2. The cat got *skinnier* every week until we took her to the vet.

**2. Of clothes: cut very narrow.** (узкий, зауженный)

3. He wears *skinny* jeans and high-tops.
4. A *skinny* tie looks odd with that suit.

**3. Of a coffee drink: made with nonfat milk — informal.** (на обезжиренном молоке)

5. She ordered a *skinny* vanilla latte.
6. Can you make that one *skinny*?

---

### skintight

**Pronunciation:** /ˌskɪnˈtaɪt/ &middot; *skin-TYT*
**Русский:** облегающий, в обтяжку
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective, written closed
**Contrast:** The same word as *skin-tight*; American writing usually closes it up.

Fitting as closely as skin.

1. The dancers wore *skintight* black leggings.
2. That shirt is *skintight* on him now.
3. She bought a *skintight* swimsuit for the meet.

---

### skittish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskɪtɪʃ/ &middot; *SKIT-ish*
**Русский:** пугливый; нервный, настороженный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more skittish*, *most skittish*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *calm*

Skittish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an animal: nervous and easily frightened.** (пугливый, норовистый)

1. The horse is *skittish* around loud trucks.
2. Our rescue dog was *skittish* for the first month.

**2. Of people or markets: nervous and quick to back out.** (нервный, осторожный)

3. Investors are *skittish* about the new tariffs.
4. Buyers got *more skittish* as the rates went up.

---

### sky-blue

**Pronunciation:** /ˌskaɪˈbluː/ &middot; *sky-BLOO*
**Русский:** лазурный, небесно-голубой
**Type:** color; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + color word

Of the light blue of a clear sky.

1. She painted the nursery *sky-blue*.
2. He wore a *sky-blue* shirt with no tie.
3. The team's away jerseys are *sky-blue*.

---

### slack

**Pronunciation:** /slæk/ &middot; *SLAK*
**Русский:** ослабленный, провисший; вялый; небрежный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slacker*, *slackest*
**Opposite:** *taut*
**Contrast:** The noun is in *cut me some slack* (go easy on me), and *slacks* means dress pants.

Slack has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Loose, not pulled tight.** (провисший, ненатянутый)

1. The rope went *slack* and the boat drifted.
2. Keep the line a little *slacker* or it will snap.

**2. With little business or activity.** (вялый, застойный)

3. January is the *slack* season at the store.
4. Demand has been *slack* since the spring.

**3. Not careful enough about doing a job properly.** (небрежный, нерадивый)

5. Security at the gate has gotten *slack*.
6. He was fired for *slack* record-keeping.

---

### slain

**Pronunciation:** /sleɪn/ &middot; *SLAYN*
**Русский:** убитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *the slain officer*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** the past participle of *slay*
**Contrast:** Everyday English says *killed*; *slain* survives in news writing, in the Bible and in fantasy. After a verb say *was killed*, not *was slain*.

Killed, especially violently — formal and journalistic.

1. The city named a park after the *slain* officer.
2. Families of the *slain* students spoke at the hearing.
3. The report lists the names of the *slain* soldiers.

---

### slanderous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslændərəs/ &middot; *SLAN-dur-uhs*
**Русский:** клеветнический
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slanderous*, *most slanderous*
**Form:** -ous from *slander*
**Contrast:** In American law *slander* is spoken and *libel* is written, so a magazine article is *libelous*, not *slanderous*.

Saying untrue things that damage someone's good name.

1. He called the accusation *slanderous* and hired a lawyer.
2. The radio host made *slanderous* remarks about the sheriff.
3. Her second statement was *more slanderous* than the first.

---

### slanted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslæntɪd/ &middot; *SLAN-tid*
**Русский:** наклонный; тенденциозный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slanted*, *most slanted*
**Takes:** *toward* or *against* + noun, in the 'biased' sense
**Form:** -ed from *slant*

Slanted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sloping, not straight up and down or flat.** (наклонный, скошенный)

1. The attic has a *slanted* ceiling.
2. Her handwriting is small and *slanted*.

**2. Presented in an unfair way that favors one side.** (тенденциозный, предвзятый)

3. The coverage was badly *slanted* toward the developer.
4. Both sides say the questions were *slanted*.

---

### slapdash

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslæpdæʃ/ &middot; *SLAP-dash*
**Русский:** небрежный, сделанный кое-как
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slapdash*, *most slapdash*
**Form:** compound — *slap* + *dash*, the sound of working too fast
**Opposite:** *careful*

Done in a hurry and without care.

1. The paint job was *slapdash* — you can see the tape lines.
2. He turned in a *slapdash* report an hour before the deadline.
3. This repair is even *more slapdash* than the last one.

---

### slapstick

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslæpˌstɪk/ &middot; *SLAP-stik*
**Русский:** фарсовый, буффонадный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *slapstick comedy*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *slap* + *stick*, the paddle clowns hit each other with
**Contrast:** *Slapstick* is much more often a noun: *the movie is pure slapstick*. As an adjective it goes before words like *comedy*, *humor* and *routine*.

Funny in a physical way — falling down, pies in the face, running into doors.

1. The kids love the *slapstick* humor in those old shorts.
2. It's a *slapstick* comedy with three chase scenes.
3. He does a *slapstick* routine with a ladder and a bucket.

---

### slashed

**Pronunciation:** /slæʃt/ &middot; *SLASHT*
**Русский:** разрезанный; резко сниженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *slash*

Slashed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cut with long, violent cuts.** (изрезанный, порезанный)

1. He came out to four *slashed* tires.
2. The painting was found *slashed* in the basement.

**2. Cut sharply, about prices or budgets.** (резко сниженный, урезанный)

3. The store advertised *slashed* prices on winter coats.
4. Libraries are working with *slashed* budgets.

---

### slaveholding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsleɪvˌhoʊldɪŋ/ &middot; *SLAYV-hohl-ding*
**Русский:** рабовладельческий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle
**Contrast:** A word from American history, used about the South before the Civil War. The noun for the person is *a slaveholder*; writers today often prefer *enslaver*.

Owning enslaved people, or allowing people to be owned.

1. Most of the early presidents came from *slaveholding* families.
2. The compromise kept the number of *slaveholding* states even.
3. The museum sits on a former *slaveholding* plantation.

---

### Slavic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɑːvɪk/ &middot; *SLAH-vik*
**Русский:** славянский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Slavic* covers a language family and the peoples who speak it, so a Russian, a Pole and a Serb are all *Slavic*. Don't confuse it with *slavish*, which means copying without thought.

Of the Slavic peoples or their languages.

1. Russian and Polish are both *Slavic* languages.
2. She teaches *Slavic* literature at a state university.
3. The neighborhood is old and heavily *Slavic*.

---

### slavish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsleɪvɪʃ/ &middot; *SLAY-vish*
**Русский:** рабский, слепо подражательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slavish*, *most slavish*
**Form:** -ish from *slave*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *Slavic* (of the Slavs), and the two are pronounced differently: SLAY-vish and SLAH-vik.

Slavish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Copying something exactly, with no ideas of your own.** (слепо подражательный)

1. The building is a *slavish* copy of a Paris train station.
2. His writing is a *slavish* imitation of Hemingway.

**2. Obeying or admiring someone far too much.** (рабский, безоговорочный)

3. The party demands *slavish* loyalty.
4. She warned against *slavish* devotion to the rankings.

---

### sleazy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsliːzi/ &middot; *SLEE-zee*
**Русский:** гнусный, аморальный; убогий и сомнительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sleazier*, *sleaziest*

Sleazy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dishonest and morally dirty.** (гнусный, нечистоплотный)

1. It was a *sleazy* move to read her texts.
2. He got rich running a *sleazy* loan business.

**2. Cheap, dirty and a little dangerous.** (убогий, сомнительный (о месте))

3. They stayed at a *sleazy* motel off the highway.
4. The bar got *sleazier* after the new owners took over.

---

### sleek

**Pronunciation:** /sliːk/ &middot; *SLEEK*
**Русский:** гладкий, лоснящийся; изящный, обтекаемый
**Type:** quality; also shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sleeker*, *sleekest*

Sleek has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Smooth, shiny and healthy-looking.** (гладкий, лоснящийся)

1. The horse had a *sleek* brown coat.
2. She pulled her hair back into a *sleek* ponytail.

**2. Elegant, with clean lines and no clutter.** (изящный, обтекаемый)

3. They drove up in a *sleek* black sedan.
4. The new phone is even *sleeker* than the last one.

---

### sleeping

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsliːpɪŋ/ &middot; *SLEE-ping*
**Русский:** спящий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a sleeping baby*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *waking*
**Contrast:** After a verb, *the baby is sleeping* is the verb; the adjective there is *asleep*: *the baby is asleep*. In *sleeping bag* and *sleeping pill* the word says what it is for, not that it sleeps.

Asleep right now.

1. Don't wake the *sleeping* baby.
2. He stepped over two *sleeping* dogs to get to the door.
3. Careful — there are *sleeping* kids in the next room.

---

### sleepless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsliːpləs/ &middot; *SLEEP-luhs*
**Русский:** бессонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *sleep*

Spent without sleep, or unable to sleep.

1. I spent a *sleepless* night waiting for the call.
2. Three *sleepless* weeks with a newborn left them both foggy.
3. She lay *sleepless* until the sky turned gray.

---

### sleepy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsliːpi/ &middot; *SLEE-pee*
**Русский:** сонный; сонный, тихий (о месте)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sleepier*, *sleepiest*
**Form:** -y from *sleep*
**Opposite:** *wide-awake*

Sleepy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Wanting to go to sleep.** (сонный)

1. The medicine makes me *sleepy*.
2. He gets *sleepier* right after lunch.

**2. Quiet, with very little going on.** (сонный, тихий)

3. They retired to a *sleepy* town on the coast.
4. It was a *sleepy* Sunday afternoon.

---

### sleeveless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsliːvləs/ &middot; *SLEEV-luhs*
**Русский:** без рукавов
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *sleeve*

Made with no sleeves.

1. She wore a *sleeveless* summer dress.
2. The dress code says no *sleeveless* shirts.
3. His jersey is *sleeveless*.

---

### slender

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɛndər/ &middot; *SLEN-dur*
**Русский:** стройный; незначительный
**Type:** size; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *slenderer*, *slenderest*; more often *more slender*, *most slender*
**Contrast:** *slender* is a compliment, like *slim*; *skinny* is blunt and *thin* is neutral.

Slender has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Thin in a graceful, attractive way.** (стройный, тонкий)

1. She has long, *slender* fingers.
2. The dancer was tall and *slender*.
3. The second vase is *more slender* than the first.

**2. Small in amount, and barely enough.** (незначительный, скудный)

4. The bill passed by a *slender* majority.
5. They are living on a *slender* income.

---

### sliced

**Pronunciation:** /slaɪst/ &middot; *SLYST*
**Русский:** нарезанный ломтиками
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *slice*
**Contrast:** It is in the American joke *the best thing since sliced bread* — the greatest invention there is.

Cut into thin flat pieces.

1. Buy a pound of *sliced* turkey at the deli.
2. Top it with *sliced* almonds.
3. The bread comes *sliced*.

---

### slick

**Pronunciation:** /slɪk/ &middot; *SLIK*
**Русский:** скользкий; ловкий, гладкий
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slicker*, *slickest*

Slick has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Smooth and slippery, usually because it is wet.** (скользкий)

1. The roads were *slick* with freezing rain.
2. The deck gets *slicker* than it looks after a shower.

**2. Done smoothly and well.** (ловкий, отточенный)

3. That was a *slick* piece of editing.
4. The company put out a *slick* thirty-second ad.

**3. Smooth in a way that makes you suspicious.** (скользкий, слишком гладкий)

5. A *slick* salesman talked them into the extended warranty.
6. His answers were too *slick* to believe.

---

### sliding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslaɪdɪŋ/ &middot; *SLY-ding*
**Русский:** раздвижной; скользящий
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a sliding door*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *slide*

Sliding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. That opens by sliding instead of swinging.** (раздвижной, выдвижной)

1. The kitchen has a *sliding* glass door to the deck.
2. Push the *sliding* panel to the left.
3. The van has a *sliding* side door.

**2. Changing with the circumstances, about a rate or a scale.** (скользящий (о шкале, ставке))

4. The clinic charges on a *sliding* scale based on income.
5. Daycare fees follow a *sliding* scale.

---

### slight

**Pronunciation:** /slaɪt/ &middot; *SLYT*
**Русский:** незначительный, лёгкий; хрупкий
**Type:** size; also quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a slight delay*; the 'thin' sense also goes after a verb
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slighter*, *slightest*
**Contrast:** The superlative lives in two fixed phrases: *not in the slightest* (not at all) and *the slightest idea*. There is also a noun and a verb *slight*, meaning an insult.

Slight has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very small in degree.** (незначительный, лёгкий)

1. There's a *slight* delay on the eastbound train.
2. She has a *slight* accent.
3. I don't have the *slightest* idea where he went.

**2. Thin and delicate in build.** (хрупкий, щуплый)

4. He is a *slight* man with quick movements.
5. She looked too *slight* to carry the pack.

---

### slimy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslaɪmi/ &middot; *SLY-mee*
**Русский:** склизкий; склизкий, мерзкий (о человеке)
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slimier*, *slimiest*
**Form:** -y from *slime*

Slimy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with something wet, thick and unpleasant.** (склизкий, покрытый слизью)

1. The rocks by the creek are *slimy*.
2. The lettuce at the bottom of the bag had gone *slimy*.

**2. Friendly in a false, unpleasant way.** (скользкий, мерзкий)

3. There's something *slimy* about that landlord.
4. He is the *slimiest* guy in the office.

---

### slippery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɪpəri/ &middot; *SLIP-uh-ree*
**Русский:** скользкий; изворотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *slipperier*, *slipperiest*; also *more slippery*, *most slippery*
**Form:** -y from *slip*
**Contrast:** *a slippery slope* means one small step that leads to something much worse.

Slippery has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to stand on or hold, because it is smooth or wet.** (скользкий)

1. The steps get *slippery* when it rains.
2. Wet leaves make the sidewalk *slipperier* than ice.

**2. Not to be trusted, and hard to pin down.** (изворотливый, ненадёжный)

3. He is a *slippery* witness who changes his story.
4. 'Fair' is a *slippery* word in this contract.

---

### slipshod

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɪpʃɑːd/ &middot; *SLIP-shahd*
**Русский:** небрежный, халтурный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slipshod*, *most slipshod*
**Opposite:** *meticulous*

Done carelessly, without attention to the details.

1. The inspector called the wiring *slipshod* and dangerous.
2. *Slipshod* record-keeping cost the clinic its license.
3. This draft is *more slipshod* than anything he's turned in before.

---

### sloping

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsloʊpɪŋ/ &middot; *SLOH-ping*
**Русский:** покатый, наклонный
**Type:** participial; also shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *gently sloping*, *steeply sloping*
**Form:** -ing from *slope*
**Opposite:** *level*

Going up or down at an angle instead of lying flat.

1. The house sits on a gently *sloping* lawn.
2. Watch your head under the *sloping* ceiling.
3. The field is *sloping*, so the water runs to the road.

---

### sloppy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɑːpi/ &middot; *SLAH-pee*
**Русский:** неряшливый, небрежный
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sloppier*, *sloppiest*
**Opposite:** *neat*

Sloppy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done carelessly and full of mistakes.** (небрежный, халтурный)

1. The report was full of *sloppy* math.
2. Their defense got *sloppier* in the fourth quarter.

**2. Messy or loose-looking.** (неряшливый, мешковатый)

3. He came to dinner in a *sloppy* old sweatshirt.
4. Her handwriting is *sloppy* but readable.

---

### sloshed

**Pronunciation:** /slɑːʃt/ &middot; *SLAHSHT*
**Русский:** пьяный, набравшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — *he got sloshed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *slosh*, the sound of liquid moving around
**Opposite:** *sober*
**Contrast:** Slang, and slightly old-fashioned but still understood. It goes after a verb only — never *a sloshed man*.

Drunk — informal.

1. He got completely *sloshed* at the reception.
2. Half the wedding party was *sloshed* by nine.
3. You don't want to be *sloshed* when your boss shows up.

---

### slothful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslɔːθfəl/ &middot; *SLAWTH-fuhl*
**Русский:** ленивый, праздный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slothful*, *most slothful*
**Form:** -ful from *sloth*
**Opposite:** *industrious*
**Contrast:** Formal and a little old-fashioned — everyday English says *lazy*. *Sloth* is also one of the seven deadly sins, and the name of the animal.

Lazy, unwilling to work or move.

1. He wrote about a *slothful* nephew who never left the couch.
2. Two weeks of vacation left me *slothful*.
3. The sermon called idleness the *most slothful* kind of sin.

---

### Slovakian

**Pronunciation:** /sloʊˈvɑːkiən/ &middot; *sloh-VAH-kee-uhn*
**Русский:** словацкий
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Slovak* is the shorter adjective and the name of the language and the people; *Slovakian* usually means 'of the country'. Slovakia is not Slovenia.

Of or from Slovakia.

1. He plays for a *Slovakian* hockey club.
2. We tried a *Slovakian* dumpling dish at the festival.
3. Her mother is *Slovakian*.

---

### Slovenian

**Pronunciation:** /sloʊˈviːniən/ &middot; *sloh-VEE-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** словенский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Slovenia borders Italy and Austria; Slovakia is farther east. The short form *Slovene* is also used for the language.

Of or from Slovenia.

1. The *Slovenian* Alps are full of small ski towns.
2. She translates *Slovenian* poetry.
3. Both coaches are *Slovenian*.

---

### slovenly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslʌvənli/ &middot; *SLUV-uhn-lee*
**Русский:** неряшливый, неопрятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slovenly*, *most slovenly*
**Opposite:** *tidy*
**Contrast:** Despite the *-ly*, this is an adjective, not an adverb. It has nothing to do with *Slovenian*.

Messy and careless about how you look or work.

1. The sergeant called his uniform *slovenly*.
2. She has *slovenly* habits in the kitchen.
3. His desk is the *most slovenly* one on the floor.

---

### slow

**Pronunciation:** /sloʊ/ &middot; *SLOH*
**Русский:** медленный; вялый; отстающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slower*, *slowest*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive — *slow to answer*
**Preposition patterns:** [slow at](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#slow-at)
**Opposite:** *fast*
**Contrast:** The adverb is *slowly*: *drive slowly*. *Drive slow* and *go slow* are heard in speech and on signs, but write *slowly*.

Slow has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Not moving quickly.** (медленный)

1. Traffic was *slow* the whole way home.
2. She's the *slowest* runner on the team, and she still finished.

**2. Taking a long time to happen or to get done.** (долгий, затяжной)

3. Recovery after the surgery was *slow*.
4. He was *slow* to answer my email.

**3. With little business or activity.** (вялый, неоживлённый)

5. Tuesday nights are *slow* at the restaurant.
6. It's been a *slow* month for sales.

**4. Of a clock or watch: showing a time earlier than the real one.** (отстающий (о часах))

7. The kitchen clock is five minutes *slow*.
8. My watch runs *slow* in the cold.

---

### slow-moving

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsloʊˈmuːvɪŋ/ &middot; *sloh-MOO-ving*
**Русский:** медленно движущийся; вяло идущий
**Type:** compound; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slow-moving*, *most slow-moving*
**Form:** compound — adverb + *-ing* participle

Slow-moving has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Moving slowly.** (медленно движущийся)

1. A *slow-moving* storm is sitting over the valley.
2. We were stuck behind a *slow-moving* tractor.

**2. Going forward or selling slowly.** (вяло идущий, залежавшийся)

3. The store marked down its *slow-moving* inventory.
4. It is a *slow-moving* case that has been in court for years.

---

### slow-witted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsloʊˈwɪtɪd/ &middot; *sloh-WIT-id*
**Русский:** тупой, несообразительный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more slow-witted*, *most slow-witted*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *quick-witted*
**Contrast:** An insult — use it about a character in a book, not about a person you know.

Slow to understand things.

1. The play makes the sheriff look *slow-witted*.
2. I felt *slow-witted* after three hours of jet lag.
3. He writes the villain as cruel and the henchman as *slow-witted*.

---

### sluggish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslʌɡɪʃ/ &middot; *SLUG-ish*
**Русский:** вялый, медлительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sluggish*, *most sluggish*
**Form:** -ish from *slug*, the slow-moving animal
**Opposite:** *brisk*

Sluggish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Moving or working more slowly than it should.** (вялый, заторможенный)

1. The engine is *sluggish* on cold mornings.
2. I feel *sluggish* if I skip breakfast.

**2. With little growth or activity, about business.** (вялый (о рынке, экономике))

3. Home sales have been *sluggish* all spring.
4. The economy looks *more sluggish* than it did in January.

---

### slurred

**Pronunciation:** /slɜːrd/ &middot; *SLURD*
**Русский:** невнятный, смазанный (о речи)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *slur*

Of speech: with the words running together and hard to make out.

1. His speech was *slurred* and the nurse called the doctor.
2. She left a *slurred* message at two in the morning.
3. *Slurred* words can be a sign of a stroke.

---

### slushy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈslʌʃi/ &middot; *SLUSH-ee*
**Русский:** покрытый талым снегом, слякотный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *slushier*, *slushiest*
**Form:** -y from *slush*

Covered with or made of half-melted snow.

1. The sidewalks were gray and *slushy*.
2. Wear boots — the driveway is *slushy*.
3. The roads get *slushier* once the plows come through.

---

### small

**Pronunciation:** /smɔːl/ &middot; *SMAWL*
**Русский:** маленький; небольшой, незначительный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smaller*, *smallest*
**Opposite:** *large*
**Contrast:** *small* is a plain measurement; *little* often adds feeling (*a little cabin* sounds fond). Use *small* after *how*: *How small is it?*

Small has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not big in size or amount.** (маленький, небольшой)

1. They live in a *small* apartment near the park.
2. Do you have this shirt in a *smaller* size?

**2. Young.** (маленький (о ребёнке))

3. She has two *small* children at home.
4. I remember that song from when I was *small*.
