**3. Not important.** (незначительный, мелкий)

5. It's a *small* problem — we can fix it today.
6. That is a *small* price to pay for a quiet street.

---

### small-cap

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsmɔːlˈkæp/ &middot; *smawl-KAP*
**Русский:** малой капитализации
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** attributive only — *small-cap stocks*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *small* + short for *capitalization*
**Opposite:** *large-cap*
**Contrast:** A finance word: it describes the size of the company, not the price of the share.

Of a company whose total stock value is small.

1. He put part of his savings in a *small-cap* fund.
2. *Small-cap* stocks swing more than the big names.
3. The index tracks about two thousand *small-cap* companies.

---

### small-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsmɔːlˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *smawl-MYN-did*
**Русский:** мелочный, ограниченный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more small-minded*, *most small-minded*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *broad-minded*

Interested only in small things, and unwilling to accept other people's ideas.

1. It was a *small-minded* rule about who could park where.
2. He left town to get away from *small-minded* gossip.
3. The longer the argument went on, the *more small-minded* both sides sounded.

---

### small-scale

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsmɔːlˈskeɪl/ &middot; *smawl-SKAYL*
**Русский:** небольшой, мелкомасштабный
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Opposite:** *large-scale*

Done at a limited size, not across a whole country or industry.

1. They started with a *small-scale* pilot in two schools.
2. *Small-scale* farmers can't compete on price.
3. For now the project is deliberately *small-scale*.

---

### small-time

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsmɔːlˈtaɪm/ &middot; *smawl-TYM*
**Русский:** мелкий, третьесортный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *a small-time crook*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Opposite:** *big-time*

Working on a small, unimportant level — usually said with a shrug.

1. He was a *small-time* dealer who got caught in a big case.
2. They started as a *small-time* operation out of a garage.
3. She is tired of *small-time* clients and the fees that come with them.

---

### smallish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmɔːlɪʃ/ &middot; *SMAW-lish*
**Русский:** маловатый, небольшой
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ish from *small*
**Contrast:** The *-ish* softens the word: *smallish* means fairly small, and avoids sounding too definite.

Fairly small.

1. It's a *smallish* town, maybe nine thousand people.
2. He ordered a *smallish* coffee and sat by the window.
3. The yard is *smallish* but the house is bright.

---

### smarmy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmɑːrmi/ &middot; *SMAHR-mee*
**Русский:** льстивый, приторно-угодливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smarmier*, *smarmiest*
**Opposite:** *sincere*

Polite and flattering in a way that feels false and unpleasant.

1. The host has a *smarmy* way of complimenting people.
2. He sent a *smarmy* thank-you note to the whole board.
3. That ad is the *smarmiest* thing on television.

---

### smart

**Pronunciation:** /smɑːrt/ &middot; *SMAHRT*
**Русский:** умный; нарядный; умный (о технике)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smarter*, *smartest*
**Opposite:** *dumb*
**Contrast:** In American English the first meaning is *intelligent*; in Britain *smart* usually means well-dressed. The technology sense is not gradable — a thermostat is *smart* or it isn't.

Smart has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Intelligent, quick to understand.** (умный, сообразительный)

1. She's the *smartest* person in the room and she knows it.
2. That was a *smart* decision.

**2. Of a machine: controlled by a computer and able to react on its own.** (умный (об устройстве))

3. We put in a *smart* thermostat last winter.
4. The building runs on *smart* lighting that shuts itself off.

**3. Neat and stylish in appearance.** (элегантный, нарядный)

5. He looked *smart* in a navy blazer.
6. The restaurant asks for *smart* casual dress.

---

### smashed

**Pronunciation:** /smæʃt/ &middot; *SMASHT*
**Русский:** разбитый вдребезги; в стельку пьяный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *smash*

Smashed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Broken into pieces by a blow or a fall.** (разбитый вдребезги)

1. A *smashed* headlight lay in the middle of the road.
2. The package arrived with everything inside *smashed*.
3. He swept up the *smashed* glass before the kids came in.

**2. Very drunk (informal).** (в стельку пьяный)

4. They got totally *smashed* at the wedding.
5. Half the crew was *smashed* by ten o'clock.

---

### smashing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmæʃɪŋ/ &middot; *SMASH-ing*
**Русский:** потрясающий, великолепный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more smashing*, *most smashing*
**Contrast:** In American English *smashing* survives mostly in the fixed phrase *a smashing success*; used on its own for "great", it still sounds British.

Wonderful; very successful.

1. The fundraiser was a *smashing* success.
2. Her first show got *smashing* reviews.
3. They threw a *smashing* party for his retirement.

---

### smiling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmaɪlɪŋ/ &middot; *SMY-ling*
**Русский:** улыбающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *smile*
**Contrast:** Before a noun it is an adjective (*a smiling face*); after *be* it is normally the verb: *She was smiling at me*.

With a smile on the face.

1. A *smiling* clerk waved us to the front of the line.
2. The wall was covered with *smiling* faces.
3. He gave me a *smiling* nod and kept walking.

---

### smitten

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmɪtən/ &middot; *SMIT-uhn*
**Русский:** влюблённый, очарованный
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* or *by* + noun
**Form:** old past participle of *smite*

Suddenly and completely in love with someone, or charmed by something.

1. He was completely *smitten* with his new neighbor.
2. She's *smitten* — she talks about him all day.
3. They were *smitten* by the little house the moment they walked in.

---

### smoggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmɑːɡi/ &middot; *SMAH-gee*
**Русский:** затянутый смогом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smoggier*, *smoggiest*
**Form:** -y from *smog*

Full of smog — dirty, hazy city air.

1. It was a hot, *smoggy* afternoon in the valley.
2. The air downtown is *smoggier* than out by the coast.
3. We couldn't see the mountains through the *smoggy* haze.

---

### smoke-filled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmoʊk ˌfɪld/ &middot; *SMOHK-fild*
**Русский:** заполненный дымом, прокуренный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle

Full of smoke.

1. They argued for hours in a *smoke-filled* back room.
2. Firefighters crawled down the *smoke-filled* hallway.
3. The deal was made in *smoke-filled* rooms long before the vote.

---

### smoke-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsmoʊk ˈfriː/ &middot; *smohk-FREE*
**Русский:** свободный от курения, для некурящих
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-free*, "with no smoking allowed"

Where smoking is not allowed.

1. The whole campus has been *smoke-free* since 2010.
2. We booked a *smoke-free* room on the third floor.
3. City buses and train platforms are *smoke-free*.

---

### smoked

**Pronunciation:** /smoʊkt/ &middot; *SMOHKT*
**Русский:** копчёный; затемнённый (о стекле)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *smoke*

Smoked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cured and flavored by hanging in smoke.** (копчёный)

1. We had bagels with *smoked* salmon.
2. He ordered the *smoked* turkey sandwich.
3. The store sells *smoked* ham by the pound.

**2. Made dark, so that you cannot see through it clearly.** (затемнённый, дымчатый)

4. The car came with *smoked* windows.
5. She wore glasses with *smoked* lenses.

---

### smokeless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmoʊkləs/ &middot; *SMOHK-luhs*
**Русский:** бездымный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *smoke*

Producing no smoke.

1. The stove burns *smokeless* fuel.
2. *Smokeless* tobacco is banned in the stadium.
3. They switched to a *smokeless* fire pit.

---

### smoking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmoʊkɪŋ/ &middot; *SMOH-king*
**Русский:** дымящийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *smoke*
**Contrast:** In *a smoking area* or *the smoking section*, *smoking* is a noun (the activity), not this adjective.

Giving off smoke.

1. Firefighters stood in front of the *smoking* ruins.
2. A *smoking* pan filled the kitchen with a burnt smell.
3. The email was the *smoking* gun the lawyers needed.

---

### smoky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmoʊki/ &middot; *SMOH-kee*
**Русский:** дымный; с дымком (о вкусе)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smokier*, *smokiest*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *smoky*, without the *e*.

Smoky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of smoke, or clouded by it.** (дымный, задымлённый)

1. The bar was dark and *smoky*.
2. A *smoky* haze hung over the whole valley.

**2. Tasting or smelling of smoke.** (с дымком, копчёный на вкус)

3. The cheese has a rich, *smoky* flavor.
4. This whiskey is *smokier* than the one we tried last time.

---

### smoldering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmoʊldərɪŋ/ &middot; *SMOHL-dur-ing*
**Русский:** тлеющий; затаённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *smolder*
**Contrast:** American spelling *smoldering*; British *smouldering*.

Smoldering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Burning slowly, with smoke but no flame.** (тлеющий)

1. Firefighters watered down the *smoldering* debris.
2. A *smoldering* cigarette had started the whole thing.

**2. Strong but held in, of a feeling.** (затаённый, тлеющий (о чувстве))

3. There was a *smoldering* resentment between the two brothers.
4. She gave him a *smoldering* look across the table.

---

### smooth

**Pronunciation:** /smuːð/ &middot; *SMOOTH*
**Русский:** гладкий; плавный; вкрадчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smoother*, *smoothest*
**Opposite:** *rough*

Smooth has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Flat and even to the touch, with no bumps or lumps.** (гладкий, ровный)

1. The stones by the river are worn *smooth*.
2. Beat the sauce until it's completely *smooth*.
3. She ran her hand over the *smooth* tabletop.

**2. Going along without jolts or problems.** (плавный, без сбоев)

4. We had a *smooth* flight to Denver.
5. The move to the new system was *smoother* than anyone expected.

**3. Charming and confident in a way you do not quite trust, of a person.** (вкрадчивый, слащавый)

6. He's a *smooth* talker — don't sign anything today.
7. The salesman was a little too *smooth* for my taste.

---

### smothering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmʌðərɪŋ/ &middot; *SMUHTH-ur-ing*
**Русский:** удушающий; душащий заботой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more smothering*, *most smothering*
**Form:** -ing from *smother*

So heavy or so constant that you feel you cannot breathe.

1. The *smothering* heat made it hard to sleep.
2. She moved out to get away from her mother's *smothering* attention.
3. A *smothering* blanket of smoke settled over the town.

---

### smug

**Pronunciation:** /smʌɡ/ &middot; *SMUHG*
**Русский:** самодовольный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *smugger*, *smuggest*
**Opposite:** *modest*

Too pleased with yourself, in a way that annoys other people.

1. He had a *smug* look on his face after the test.
2. Nobody likes a *smug* winner.
3. She got *smugger* with every point she scored.

---

### smuggled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsmʌɡəld/ &middot; *SMUHG-uhld*
**Русский:** контрабандный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *smuggle*

Brought into or out of a country secretly and illegally.

1. Police found *smuggled* cigarettes in the back of the truck.
2. The museum returned several *smuggled* artifacts.
3. *Smuggled* goods are seized at the border every day.

---

### snappy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnæpi/ &middot; *SNAP-ee*
**Русский:** бойкий, хлёсткий; щегольской
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snappier*, *snappiest*
**Contrast:** *Make it snappy* means "hurry up".

Snappy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Quick, short and lively.** (бойкий, хлёсткий)

1. The movie has *snappy* dialogue and a short runtime.
2. Give me a *snappier* headline for the front page.

**2. Stylish and sharp-looking (informal).** (щегольской, нарядный)

3. He showed up in a *snappy* blue suit.
4. That's a *snappy* pair of shoes.

---

### snazzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnæzi/ &middot; *SNAZ-ee*
**Русский:** шикарный, стильный (разг.)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snazzier*, *snazziest*

Stylish and eye-catching, often a little flashy (informal).

1. He drove up in a *snazzy* red convertible.
2. That's a *snazzy* jacket — is it new?
3. Their new website looks *snazzier* than ours.

---

### sneak

**Pronunciation:** /sniːk/ &middot; *SNEEK*
**Русский:** внезапный, тайный (о нападении, показе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *sneak* is also a noun and a verb; as an adjective it lives only in fixed pairs — *a sneak attack*, *a sneak preview*, *a sneak peek*.

Done without warning, before anyone can react.

1. It was a *sneak* attack on a sleeping base.
2. The studio held a *sneak* preview on Thursday night.
3. She gave us a *sneak* peek at the new design.

---

### sneaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsniːkɪŋ/ &middot; *SNEE-king*
**Русский:** тайный, подспудный (о подозрении)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Almost always with *suspicion*, *feeling* or *admiration*: a quiet thought you do not say out loud.

Felt quietly and kept to yourself.

1. I have a *sneaking* suspicion that he already knows.
2. She had a *sneaking* admiration for her rival.
3. There was a *sneaking* feeling that the deal was too good.

---

### sneaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsniːki/ &middot; *SNEE-kee*
**Русский:** хитрый, пронырливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sneakier*, *sneakiest*
**Opposite:** *straightforward*

Acting in secret to get what you want, in a way people find dishonest.

1. That was a *sneaky* way to get out of paying.
2. He's *sneakier* than his brother ever was.
3. Don't be *sneaky* about it — just ask her.

---

### snide

**Pronunciation:** /snaɪd/ &middot; *SNYD*
**Русский:** язвительный, ехидный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more snide*, *most snide*

Insulting in an indirect, sly way.

1. He made a *snide* remark about her accent.
2. The review was full of *snide* comments.
3. Her tone was *snide* rather than funny.

---

### snobbish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnɑːbɪʃ/ &middot; *SNAH-bish*
**Русский:** снобистский, высокомерный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more snobbish*, *most snobbish*
**Form:** -ish from *snob*

Looking down on people you think have less money, taste or education.

1. The staff was polite but a little *snobbish*.
2. He has a *snobbish* attitude about wine.
3. Don't be *snobbish* about where other people shop.

---

### snobby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnɑːbi/ &middot; *SNAH-bee*
**Русский:** снобистский, заносчивый (разг.)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snobbier*, *snobbiest*
**Contrast:** *snobby* is the casual spoken word; *snobbish* is the one you write.

Acting superior to other people (informal).

1. The restaurant felt *snobby* the minute we walked in.
2. Her new friends are *snobbier* than she is.
3. He's not *snobby* about music at all.

---

### snoopy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnuːpi/ &middot; *SNOO-pee*
**Русский:** любопытный, сующий нос в чужие дела
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snoopier*, *snoopiest*

Too interested in other people's private business (informal).

1. Our *snoopy* neighbor watches every car on the street.
2. Don't be *snoopy* about other people's mail.
3. She got *snoopier* after she retired.

---

### snooty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnuːti/ &middot; *SNOO-tee*
**Русский:** надменный, чванливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snootier*, *snootiest*

Treating other people as beneath you (informal).

1. The hostess gave us a *snooty* look.
2. It's a *snooty* little shop on the corner.
3. The service got *snootier* the longer we waited.

---

### snot-nosed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnɑːt ˌnoʊzd/ &middot; *SNAHT-nohzd*
**Русский:** сопливый, наглый (о молодом человеке)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle
**Contrast:** Rude and dismissive — fine in speech between friends, never in writing about a real person.

Young and annoyingly sure of himself (informal, insulting).

1. Some *snot-nosed* kid told me I was parked wrong.
2. He was a *snot-nosed* rookie back then.
3. I'm not taking orders from a *snot-nosed* intern.

---

### snotty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnɑːti/ &middot; *SNAH-tee*
**Русский:** наглый, заносчивый; сопливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snottier*, *snottiest*

Snotty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Rude and superior (informal).** (наглый, заносчивый)

1. Don't get *snotty* with me.
2. She sent a *snotty* email to the whole team.
3. His tone got *snottier* with every question.

**2. Covered with mucus from the nose.** (сопливый)

4. Wipe that *snotty* nose before we go in.
5. The toddler handed me a *snotty* tissue.

---

### snow-capped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnoʊ ˌkæpt/ &middot; *SNOH-kapt*
**Русский:** со снежными вершинами
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle

With snow on top, of a mountain.

1. *Snow-capped* peaks rose behind the town.
2. We could see the *snow-capped* mountains from the hotel.
3. The postcard showed a *snow-capped* volcano.

---

### snow-covered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnoʊ ˌkʌvərd/ &middot; *SNOH-kuv-urd*
**Русский:** заснеженный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle

With snow lying all over it.

1. We drove slowly down the *snow-covered* road.
2. The *snow-covered* fields went on for miles.
3. By morning every car on the block was *snow-covered*.

---

### snow-white

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsnoʊ ˈwaɪt/ &middot; *snoh-WYT*
**Русский:** белоснежный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective, "as white as snow"

Pure white.

1. He had a *snow-white* beard.
2. The tablecloth was *snow-white*.
3. She wore a *snow-white* dress to the ceremony.

---

### snowbound

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnoʊbaʊnd/ &middot; *SNOH-bownd*
**Русский:** занесённый снегом, отрезанный снегом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *snow* + *-bound*, "held in place by"

Unable to travel or get out because of heavy snow.

1. We were *snowbound* at the cabin for three days.
2. A *snowbound* village waited all week for the plows.
3. The storm left hundreds of travelers *snowbound* at the airport.

---

### snowy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsnoʊi/ &middot; *SNOH-ee*
**Русский:** снежный; белоснежный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snowier*, *snowiest*

Snowy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With a lot of snow.** (снежный, со снегом)

1. It was a cold, *snowy* January.
2. This is the *snowiest* winter in twenty years.

**2. White like snow.** (белоснежный)

3. He stroked his *snowy* beard.
4. *Snowy* sheets hung on the line behind the house.

---

### snub-nosed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsnʌb ˈnoʊzd/ &middot; *snuhb-NOHZD*
**Русский:** курносый; короткоствольный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Snub-nosed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With a short nose that turns up at the end.** (курносый)

1. A *snub-nosed* boy answered the door.
2. She has her father's *snub-nosed* face.

**2. With a very short barrel, of a gun.** (короткоствольный)

3. He carried a *snub-nosed* revolver.
4. The detective's *snub-nosed* .38 lay on the desk.

---

### snug

**Pronunciation:** /snʌɡ/ &middot; *SNUHG*
**Русский:** уютный, тёплый; плотно сидящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *snugger*, *snuggest*

Snug has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Warm, small and comfortable.** (уютный, тёплый)

1. The cabin was tiny but *snug*.
2. The kids were *snug* under two blankets.

**2. Fitting closely, sometimes a little too closely.** (плотно сидящий, тесноватый)

3. The jacket is a little *snug* across the shoulders.
4. These boots feel *snugger* than the last pair.

---

### so-so

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊ soʊ/ &middot; *SOH-soh*
**Русский:** так себе, средний
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — often after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also used as an adverb (*He plays so-so*), and always written with a hyphen.

Neither good nor bad; average (informal).

1. The food was *so-so*, but the view was great.
2. He got *so-so* grades his first semester.
3. How was the movie? *So-so*.

---

### soapy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊpi/ &middot; *SOH-pee*
**Русский:** мыльный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *soapier*, *soapiest*
**Form:** -y from *soap*

Full of soap, or covered with it.

1. Wash the pan in hot *soapy* water.
2. The floor was still *soapy* and slippery.
3. This water is *soapier* than it needs to be.

---

### soaring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɔːrɪŋ/ &middot; *SOR-ing*
**Русский:** стремительно растущий; взмывающий ввысь
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *soar*

Soaring has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Rising fast, of prices or numbers.** (стремительно растущий)

1. *Soaring* rents have pushed families out of the city.
2. The airline blamed *soaring* fuel costs.

**2. Rising very high into the air.** (взмывающий ввысь, уходящий вверх)

3. The church has a *soaring* ceiling.
4. She held the *soaring* high note at the end of the song.

---

### sober

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊbər/ &middot; *SOH-bur*
**Русский:** трезвый; серьёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sober*, *most sober*
**Opposite:** *drunk*

Sober has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not drunk.** (трезвый)

1. He's been *sober* for six years.
2. Someone has to stay *sober* and drive.
3. She was perfectly *sober* when she said it.

**2. Serious and unexcited.** (серьёзный, сдержанный)

4. It was a *sober* assessment of the damage.
5. The mood in the room was *sober*.

---

### sobering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊbərɪŋ/ &middot; *SOH-bur-ing*
**Русский:** отрезвляющий, заставляющий задуматься
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sobering*, *most sobering*
**Form:** -ing — what makes you serious
**Contrast:** *sobering* is the thing that makes you think; a person who is not drunk is *sober*.

Making you suddenly serious about how bad something is.

1. The accident report makes for *sobering* reading.
2. It was a *sobering* reminder of how fast things change.
3. The numbers in the study are *sobering*.

---

### sociable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊʃəbəl/ &middot; *SOH-shuh-buhl*
**Русский:** общительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sociable*, *most sociable*
**Opposite:** *unsociable*
**Contrast:** *sociable* describes a person who enjoys company; *social* describes society or gatherings (*a social event*). You cannot say "a sociable problem".

Enjoying the company of other people.

1. She's a *sociable* kid who makes friends anywhere.
2. He's not very *sociable* before his first coffee.
3. The dogs are *sociable* with strangers.

---

### social

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊʃəl/ &middot; *SOH-shuhl*
**Русский:** общественный, социальный; светский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — except the informal *He's very social* (= sociable)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *social* is about society or about gatherings; a friendly person is *sociable*.

Social has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with society and how people live together.** (общественный, социальный)

1. The city is facing serious *social* problems.
2. She studies *social* change in small towns.

**2. Having to do with spending time with other people.** (светский, связанный с общением)

3. We met at a *social* event at the club.
4. He has a busy *social* life.

---

### socialist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊʃəlɪst/ &middot; *SOH-shuh-list*
**Русский:** социалистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *She is a socialist*.

Based on socialism — the idea that the state or the workers should own the main industries.

1. The party ran on a *socialist* platform.
2. He studies *socialist* movements in postwar Europe.
3. *Socialist* governments came to power across the region.

---

### socialized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊʃəlaɪzd/ &middot; *SOH-shuh-lyzd*
**Русский:** обобществлённый (о медицине); социализированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more socialized*, *most socialized*
**Contrast:** American spelling *-ized*; British *-ised*.

Socialized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Run and paid for by the government instead of private companies.** (обобществлённый, государственный)

1. Critics call the plan *socialized* medicine.
2. He argued against *socialized* health care for an hour.

**2. Trained to behave well with others.** (социализированный, приученный к обществу)

3. The puppy is well *socialized* around children.
4. Kids who are poorly *socialized* struggle in big groups.

---

### societal

**Pronunciation:** /səˈsaɪətəl/ &middot; *suh-SY-uh-tuhl*
**Русский:** общественный, социальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *society*
**Contrast:** *societal* is formal and means "of society as a whole"; everyday English says *social*.

Belonging to society as a whole.

1. The report looks at the *societal* costs of long commutes.
2. *Societal* attitudes toward the issue have changed fast.
3. These are *societal* problems, not personal failings.

---

### sociocultural

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsoʊsioʊˈkʌltʃərəl/ &middot; *soh-see-oh-KUHL-chur-uhl*
**Русский:** социокультурный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** socio- + *cultural*

Having to do with both society and culture.

1. The study takes a *sociocultural* approach to language learning.
2. *Sociocultural* factors shape what people eat.
3. They mapped the *sociocultural* history of the neighborhood.

---

### socioeconomic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsoʊsioʊˌɛkəˈnɑːmɪk/ &middot; *soh-see-oh-ek-uh-NAH-mik*
**Русский:** социально-экономический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** socio- + *economic*

Having to do with both money and social position.

1. Test scores track *socioeconomic* status closely.
2. The two neighborhoods differ in *socioeconomic* background.
3. The researchers controlled for *socioeconomic* factors.

---

### sociological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsoʊsiəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *soh-see-uh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** социологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *sociology*

Having to do with the study of society.

1. She published a *sociological* study of small-town churches.
2. From a *sociological* point of view, the change is enormous.
3. He teaches *sociological* theory to first-year students.

---

### sociopathic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsoʊsioʊˈpæθɪk/ &middot; *soh-see-oh-PATH-ik*
**Русский:** социопатический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *sociopath*

Behaving with no conscience and no feeling for other people.

1. The novel's villain is charming and *sociopathic*.
2. Experts described the behavior as *sociopathic*.
3. He shows *sociopathic* traits under pressure.

---

### Socratic

**Pronunciation:** /səˈkrætɪk/ &middot; *suh-KRAT-ik*
**Русский:** сократический, сократовский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from the name *Socrates*

Teaching by asking question after question, in the manner of Socrates.

1. Law schools still teach by the *Socratic* method.
2. The class was run as a *Socratic* dialogue.
3. He answered every question with another one, in true *Socratic* style.

---

### sodden

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːdən/ &middot; *SAH-duhn*
**Русский:** промокший насквозь, раскисший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sodden*, *most sodden*

Soaked through with water, and heavy because of it.

1. We walked the last mile in *sodden* shoes.
2. The field was *sodden* after three days of rain.
3. He pulled off his *sodden* coat and hung it by the door.

---

### soft

**Pronunciation:** /sɔːft/ &middot; *SAWFT*
**Русский:** мягкий; тихий; нежный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *softer*, *softest*
**Takes:** *on* + noun in the sense "not strict": *soft on crime*
**Opposite:** *hard*; *loud*

Soft has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Easy to press or bend; not hard.** (мягкий)

1. The mattress is too *soft* for my back.
2. Leave the butter out until it's *soft*.

**2. Smooth and pleasant to touch.** (нежный, мягкий на ощупь)

3. That blanket is the *softest* thing she owns.
4. The baby has *soft* pink skin.

**3. Quiet; not loud.** (тихий, негромкий)

5. She answered in a *soft* voice.
6. The music was *soft* enough to talk over.

**4. Gentle; not strict enough.** (мягкий, нестрогий)

7. The judge is *soft* on first offenders.
8. Don't be too *soft* with them or nothing gets done.

---

### soft-boiled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɔːft ˈbɔɪld/ &middot; *sawft-BOYLD*
**Русский:** сваренный всмятку
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + past participle
**Opposite:** *hard-boiled*

Boiled only a few minutes, so the yolk is still runny.

1. He had a *soft-boiled* egg and toast.
2. I like my eggs *soft-boiled*.
3. The diner serves *soft-boiled* eggs all day.

---

### soft-spoken

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɔːft ˈspoʊkən/ &middot; *sawft-SPOH-kuhn*
**Русский:** говорящий тихо, мягкий в общении
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more soft-spoken*, *most soft-spoken*
**Form:** compound — adjective + past participle

Speaking in a quiet, gentle voice.

1. He's a *soft-spoken* man who never interrupts.
2. She was *soft-spoken* but completely firm.
3. Our *soft-spoken* coach has never yelled at anyone.

---

### soggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːɡi/ &middot; *SAH-gee*
**Русский:** раскисший, размокший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *soggier*, *soggiest*

Wet and limp, in a way that spoils food or ground.

1. Nobody wants *soggy* fries.
2. The cereal went *soggy* in the milk.
3. The bottom of the pie was even *soggier* the next day.

---

### soiled

**Pronunciation:** /sɔɪld/ &middot; *SOYLD*
**Русский:** испачканный, грязный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *soil*
**Contrast:** *soiled* is the polite, slightly formal word for dirty — hospitals and hotels say *soiled linens*, not "dirty sheets".

Made dirty by use.

1. Put the *soiled* towels in the blue bin.
2. The nurse changed the *soiled* sheets.
3. His shirt was *soiled* at the collar.

---

### solar

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊlər/ &middot; *SOH-lur*
**Русский:** солнечный; работающий на солнечной энергии
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *solar* means "of the sun" as a scientific term; a bright day is *sunny*, never "solar".

Solar has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of or from the sun.** (солнечный, относящийся к Солнцу)

1. A *solar* eclipse will be visible from the coast.
2. There are eight planets in the *solar* system.

**2. Using the sun's energy.** (на солнечной энергии)

3. They put *solar* panels on the roof last spring.
4. The whole house runs on *solar* power.

---

### sold-out

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsoʊld ˈaʊt/ &middot; *sohld-OWT*
**Русский:** распроданный, аншлаговый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Hyphenated before a noun (*a sold-out show*), two separate words after the verb (*The show sold out in an hour*).

With every ticket already sold.

1. They played to a *sold-out* arena.
2. The concert has been *sold-out* for weeks.
3. It's a *sold-out* crowd tonight.

---

### solemn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːləm/ &middot; *SAH-luhm*
**Русский:** торжественный; серьёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more solemn*, *most solemn*

Solemn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very serious, with no smiling.** (серьёзный, мрачно-торжественный)

1. The room went *solemn* when he stood up to speak.
2. She had a *solemn* look on her face all morning.

**2. Formal and binding.** (торжественный (об обещании, клятве))

3. He made a *solemn* promise to pay it back.
4. The new officers took a *solemn* oath.

---

### solicitous

**Pronunciation:** /səˈlɪsɪtəs/ &middot; *suh-LIS-i-tuhs*
**Русский:** заботливый, внимательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more solicitous*, *most solicitous*
**Takes:** *about* or *of* + noun

Showing careful, almost anxious concern for someone.

1. The host was *solicitous* about our comfort all evening.
2. She is *solicitous* of her elderly father.
3. A *solicitous* waiter refilled every glass before it was empty.

---

### solid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːlɪd/ &middot; *SAH-lid*
**Русский:** твёрдый; прочный; цельный; надёжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more solid*, *most solid*
**Contrast:** *three solid hours* means "three hours without a break" — a fourth, time meaning.

Solid has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Hard and keeping its shape; not liquid or gas.** (твёрдый)

1. The lake was frozen *solid* by January.
2. Water becomes *solid* at 32 degrees.

**2. Strongly built and not likely to break.** (прочный, крепкий)

3. It's an old table, but it's *solid*.
4. They built a *solid* stone wall around the yard.

**3. Made of one material all the way through.** (цельный, сплошной)

5. The ring is *solid* gold.
6. The door is *solid* wood, not veneer.

**4. Dependable and good, without being brilliant.** (надёжный, добротный)

7. He gave a *solid* performance in the second half.
8. The case rests on *solid* evidence.

---

### solid-colored

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɑːlɪd ˈkʌlərd/ &middot; *sah-lid-KUHL-urd*
**Русский:** однотонный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American spelling *-colored*; British *-coloured*.

All one color, with no pattern.

1. Wear a *solid-colored* shirt for the photo.
2. She prefers *solid-colored* dresses to prints.
3. The uniform has to be *solid-colored*.

---

### solid-state

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɑːlɪd ˈsteɪt/ &middot; *sah-lid-STAYT*
**Русский:** твердотельный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Built from solid electronic parts, with nothing moving inside.

1. The laptop has a *solid-state* drive.
2. *Solid-state* amplifiers replaced the old tube ones.
3. It's a *solid-state* sensor with no moving parts.

---

### solitary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːləˌtɛri/ &middot; *SAH-luh-tair-ee*
**Русский:** одинокий, уединённый; единственный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the "only one" sense goes before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more solitary*, *most solitary*
**Contrast:** *solitary* means alone by habit or by choice; *lonely* means sad because you are alone. Russian одинокий covers both.

Solitary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Alone, or done alone.** (одинокий, уединённый)

1. He took a *solitary* walk along the beach.
2. Tigers are *solitary* animals.

**2. Only one, and no more.** (единственный)

3. Not a *solitary* word was said about the money.
4. A *solitary* streetlight lit the whole block.

---

### solo

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊloʊ/ &middot; *SOH-loh*
**Русский:** сольный; одиночный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also an adverb (*She flew solo*) and a noun (*a piano solo*).

Done by one person alone.

1. She released her first *solo* album in March.
2. He made a *solo* flight across the Atlantic.
3. The climb was *solo*, with no support team.

---

### soluble

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːljəbəl/ &middot; *SAH-lyuh-buhl*
**Русский:** растворимый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Opposite:** *insoluble*

Able to dissolve in a liquid.

1. Salt is *soluble* in water.
2. The tablets come in a *soluble* form.
3. Vitamin D is fat-*soluble*, so take it with a meal.

---

### solvable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːlvəbəl/ &middot; *SAHL-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** разрешимый, решаемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *solve*
**Opposite:** *unsolvable*

Able to be solved.

1. The puzzle is *solvable* in under a minute.
2. These are *solvable* problems if we start now.
3. He treats every crisis as *solvable*.

---

### solvent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːlvənt/ &middot; *SAHL-vuhnt*
**Русский:** платёжеспособный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *insolvent*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a solvent* is a liquid that dissolves other things.

Having enough money to pay what you owe.

1. The company is barely *solvent*.
2. Two good quarters kept the firm *solvent*.
3. A bank has to stay *solvent* to keep its license.

---

### Somali

**Pronunciation:** /səˈmɑːli/ &middot; *suh-MAH-lee*
**Русский:** сомалийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — *a Somali* (a person) and *Somali* (the language). The country is *Somalia*.

From Somalia, or having to do with Somalia and its people.

1. She grew up in a *Somali* neighborhood in Minneapolis.
2. The restaurant serves *Somali* food.
3. Many of the students in the class are *Somali*.

---

### somber

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːmbər/ &middot; *SAHM-bur*
**Русский:** мрачный, угрюмый; тёмный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more somber*, *most somber*
**Contrast:** American spelling *somber*; British *sombre*.

Somber has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sad and serious in mood.** (мрачный, угрюмый)

1. The mood at the meeting was *somber*.
2. She read the news in a *somber* voice.

**2. Dark and dull in color.** (тёмный, тусклый)

3. He wore a *somber* gray suit to the service.
4. The room is painted in *somber* browns.

---

### sonic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːnɪk/ &middot; *SAH-nik*
**Русский:** звуковой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with sound or the speed of sound.

1. The jet made a *sonic* boom over the coast.
2. The band is known for its *sonic* experiments.
3. Engineers measured the *sonic* range of the hall.

---

### sonorous

**Pronunciation:** /səˈnɔːrəs/ &middot; *suh-NOR-uhs*
**Русский:** звучный, раскатистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sonorous*, *most sonorous*

Deep, full and pleasant in sound.

1. He has a deep, *sonorous* voice.
2. The bells were *sonorous* in the empty square.
3. She read the poem in *sonorous* tones.

---

### soothing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːðɪŋ/ &middot; *SOO-thing*
**Русский:** успокаивающий, смягчающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more soothing*, *most soothing*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

Making pain or worry easier to bear.

1. Her voice on the phone was *soothing*.
2. He put on some *soothing* music and lay down.
3. The cream is *soothing* on a sunburn.

---

### sooty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʊti/ &middot; *SUUT-ee*
**Русский:** закопчённый, покрытый сажей
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sootier*, *sootiest*
**Form:** -y from *soot*

Covered or blackened with soot.

1. He wiped his *sooty* hands on a rag.
2. The wall above the stove was *sooty*.
3. The chimney got *sootier* every winter.

---

### sophomore

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːfəmɔːr/ &middot; *SAH-fuh-mor*
**Русский:** второкурсный; второй (об альбоме, сезоне)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — *a sophomore* is a second-year student. As an adjective it means "second in a series": *a sophomore album*.

Of the second year of high school or college, or second in a series.

1. She's taking chemistry her *sophomore* year.
2. The band's *sophomore* album flopped.
3. He had a *sophomore* slump after a great rookie season.

---

### soporific

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɑːpəˈrɪfɪk/ &middot; *sah-puh-RIF-ik*
**Русский:** снотворный, усыпляющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more soporific*, *most soporific*

Making you sleepy.

1. The lecture had a *soporific* effect on the whole class.
2. He read the list in a *soporific* monotone.
3. The heat in that room is *soporific*.

---

### sordid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɔːrdɪd/ &middot; *SOR-did*
**Русский:** грязный, низкий, мерзкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sordid*, *most sordid*

Morally dirty — involving lies, greed or shabby behavior.

1. The paper printed every *sordid* detail.
2. It's a *sordid* story of money and lies.
3. The whole business was more *sordid* than anyone expected.

---

### sore

**Pronunciation:** /sɔːr/ &middot; *SOR*
**Русский:** больной, воспалённый; обиженный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sorer*, *sorest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun in the "upset" sense
**Contrast:** *a sore point* is a subject that upsets someone. Do not confuse *sore* (hurting) with *sorry*.

Sore has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Painful, especially from use or infection.** (больной, воспалённый)

1. I woke up with a *sore* throat.
2. My legs were *sorer* the day after the hike.
3. Her shoulder is still *sore* from the fall.

**2. Annoyed and hurt about something (informal).** (обиженный, задетый)

4. He's still *sore* about losing the bet.
5. Don't be a *sore* loser.

---

### sorrowful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɔːrəfəl/ &middot; *SOR-uh-fuhl*
**Русский:** печальный, скорбный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sorrowful*, *most sorrowful*
**Form:** -ful from *sorrow*
**Contrast:** *sorrowful* is literary; everyday American English says *sad*.

Full of deep sadness, or showing it.

1. She gave me a *sorrowful* look and said nothing.
2. The song is slow and *sorrowful*.
3. His face was *sorrowful* but calm.

---

### sought-after

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɔːt ˌæftər/ &middot; *SAWT-af-tur*
**Русский:** востребованный, желанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — often after *much* or *most*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sought-after*, *most sought-after*
**Form:** compound — past participle of *seek* + *after*

Wanted by many people, and therefore hard to get.

1. He's the *most sought-after* photographer in town.
2. These tickets are highly *sought-after*.
3. It's a *sought-after* address on the north side.

---

### soulful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊlfəl/ &middot; *SOHL-fuhl*
**Русский:** проникновенный, душевный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more soulful*, *most soulful*
**Form:** -ful from *soul*

Full of deep feeling that you can hear or see.

1. She sang a *soulful* version of the old hymn.
2. The dog gave him a *soulful* look.
3. His playing is *soulful* and unhurried.

---

### soulless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊlləs/ &middot; *SOHL-luhs*
**Русский:** бездушный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more soulless*, *most soulless*
**Form:** -less from *soul*
**Opposite:** *soulful*

With no warmth or feeling in it.

1. They tore down the old theater and put up a *soulless* office block.
2. The remake is technically perfect and completely *soulless*.
3. It's *soulless* work, but it pays the rent.

---

### sound

**Pronunciation:** /saʊnd/ &middot; *SOWND*
**Русский:** здоровый, крепкий; разумный; глубокий (о сне)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sounder*, *soundest*
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a loud sound*) and a verb (*That sounds good*). *safe and sound* is a fixed phrase.

Sound has 3 distinct senses.

**1. In good condition; not damaged or ill.** (крепкий, исправный, здоровый)

1. The inspector said the roof is *sound*.
2. The kids came back safe and *sound*.

**2. Sensible and based on good reasoning.** (разумный, обоснованный)

3. That's *sound* advice.
4. His plan is *sounder* than mine.

**3. Deep and unbroken, of sleep.** (глубокий, крепкий (о сне))

5. She's a *sound* sleeper — the storm never woke her.
6. He fell into a *sound* sleep on the couch.

---

### sound asleep

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊnd əˈsliːp/ &middot; *sownd-uh-SLEEP*
**Русский:** крепко спящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** fixed phrase — *sound* here is an old adverb meaning "deeply"
**Contrast:** Only after a verb. Never "a sound asleep baby" — before a noun use *a sleeping baby*.

Deeply asleep and hard to wake.

1. The baby was *sound asleep* by seven.
2. Both kids were *sound asleep* in the back seat.
3. He's *sound asleep* — don't wake him up.

---

### soundproof

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaʊndpruːf/ &middot; *SOWND-proof*
**Русский:** звуконепроницаемый, со звукоизоляцией
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *sound* + *-proof*, "that sound cannot get through"

Built so that sound cannot pass through.

1. The studio is completely *soundproof*.
2. They put in *soundproof* windows facing the highway.
3. It's not a *soundproof* room, so keep your voice down.

---

### soupy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːpi/ &middot; *SOO-pee*
**Русский:** жидкий, как суп; густой (о тумане)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *soupier*, *soupiest*

Soupy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too thin and watery, of food.** (жидкий, как суп)

1. The risotto came out *soupy*.
2. Add more rice if it still looks *soupy*.

**2. Thick and heavy, of fog or air.** (густой, вязкий (о тумане, воздухе))

3. We drove through *soupy* fog all morning.
4. The August air was *soupier* than ever.

---

### sour

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaʊər/ &middot; *SOW-ur*
**Русский:** кислый; прокисший; недовольный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sourer*, *sourest*
**Opposite:** *sweet*
**Contrast:** *go sour* is also used of plans and relationships: *The deal went sour*.

Sour has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Sharp in taste, like a lemon.** (кислый)

1. These cherries are too *sour* to eat raw.
2. She likes *sour* candy.

**2. Spoiled, of milk or cream.** (прокисший)

3. The milk has gone *sour*.
4. There was a *sour* smell in the fridge.

**3. Unfriendly and bad-tempered.** (недовольный, кислый (о виде))

5. He gave me a *sour* look.
6. Her mood got *sourer* as the day went on.

---

### soured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaʊərd/ &middot; *SOW-urd*
**Русский:** испортившийся; натянутый (об отношениях)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sour*

Soured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Turned unfriendly or bitter, of a relationship or mood.** (натянутый, испортившийся)

1. *Soured* relations between the two countries dragged on for years.
2. He left the company and a *soured* friendship behind him.

**2. Turned sour, of milk or cream.** (прокисший)

3. A carton of *soured* milk sat on the counter.
4. You can still bake with slightly *soured* milk.

---

### south

**Pronunciation:** /saʊθ/ &middot; *SOWTH*
**Русский:** южный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*the South*) and an adverb (*drive south*). For a region or its people use *southern*: *southern cooking*, not "south cooking".

On or facing the south side.

1. Their office is on the *south* side of the building.
2. A *south* wind brought warmer air.
3. We parked by the *south* entrance.

---

### South African

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθ ˈæfrɪkən/ &middot; *sowth-AF-ri-kuhn*
**Русский:** южноафриканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a South African*). Both words are capitalized, with no hyphen.

From South Africa, or having to do with it.

1. He's a *South African* journalist based in London.
2. We tried a *South African* wine with dinner.
3. Two of the players are *South African*.

---

### South American

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθ əˈmɛrɪkən/ &middot; *sowth-uh-MER-i-kuhn*
**Русский:** южноамериканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a South American*).

From South America, or having to do with it.

1. The bird is native to *South American* rainforests.
2. She writes about *South American* politics.
3. Most of the group is *South American*.

---

### South Korean

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθ kəˈriːən/ &middot; *sowth-kuh-REE-uhn*
**Русский:** южнокорейский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a South Korean*).

From South Korea, or having to do with it.

1. The company is *South Korean*.
2. He drives a *South Korean* car.
3. *South Korean* films have won major prizes.

---

### southbound

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsaʊθbaʊnd/ &middot; *SOWTH-bownd*
**Русский:** идущий на юг, южного направления
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *south* + *-bound*, "heading for"
**Opposite:** *northbound*

Traveling toward the south.

1. The *southbound* lanes are closed until noon.
2. We took the *southbound* train from Penn Station.
3. Most of the traffic this morning is *southbound*.

---

### southeast

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθˈiːst/ &middot; *sowth-EEST*
**Русский:** юго-восточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun and an adverb. For a whole region the usual adjective is *southeastern*.

In or facing the southeast.

1. The storm hit the *southeast* corner of the state.
2. A *southeast* wind is pushing the smoke inland.
3. Their farm is in *southeast* Kansas.

---

### southeastern

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθˈiːstərn/ &middot; *sowth-EE-sturn*
**Русский:** юго-восточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

In or from the southeast part of a country or region.

1. She grew up in *southeastern* Ohio.
2. The plant grows across the *southeastern* United States.
3. *Southeastern* Europe was hit hardest by the drought.

---

### southerly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌðərli/ &middot; *SUHTH-ur-lee*
**Русский:** южный (о ветре, направлении)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more southerly*, *most southerly*
**Contrast:** An *-ly* word that is an adjective, not an adverb. A *southerly* wind blows from the south, but a *southerly* course goes toward the south.

From the south, or toward the south.

1. A *southerly* wind kept the night warm.
2. We took a *more southerly* route to avoid the snow.
3. The island is the *most southerly* point of the country.

---

### southernmost

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌðərnmoʊst/ &middot; *SUHTH-urn-mohst*
**Русский:** самый южный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable — it is already a superlative
**Form:** *southern* + *-most*

Farthest to the south.

1. Key West is the *southernmost* city in the continental United States.
2. They reached the *southernmost* tip of the island at dusk.
3. This is the *southernmost* vineyard in the state.

---

### southwest

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθˈwɛst/ &middot; *sowth-WEST*
**Русский:** юго-западный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun and an adverb. For a whole region the usual adjective is *southwestern*.

In or facing the southwest.

1. A *southwest* wind carried the smoke into town.
2. The house has a *southwest* exposure and gets sun all afternoon.
3. They drove through *southwest* Texas for two days.

---

### southwestern

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsaʊθˈwɛstərn/ &middot; *sowth-WES-turn*
**Русский:** юго-западный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

In or from the southwest part of a country or region.

1. *Southwestern* cooking uses a lot of chiles.
2. The rain never reached the *southwestern* counties.
3. He moved to a small *southwestern* town for the dry air.

---

### sovereign

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːvrən/ &middot; *SAH-vruhn*
**Русский:** суверенный; верховный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — *the sovereign* is a king or queen.

Sovereign has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Independent and governing itself, of a country.** (суверенный, независимый)

1. Ukraine is a *sovereign* state.
2. They demanded *sovereign* control of their own waters.

**2. Holding the highest power.** (верховный)

3. In a monarchy the king is *sovereign*.
4. Congress holds *sovereign* authority over the budget.

---

### Soviet

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsoʊviət/ &middot; *SOH-vee-uht*
**Русский:** советский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *Soviet* means 'of the USSR', which was more than Russia: *a Soviet passport*, but *the Russian language*.

Belonging to the Soviet Union, the country that existed from 1922 to 1991.

1. My grandfather grew up in a *Soviet* apartment block.
2. The museum has a large collection of *Soviet* posters.
3. Both sides signed the treaty during the *Soviet* era.

---

### spaced-out

**Pronunciation:** /ˌspeɪst ˈaʊt/ &middot; *spayst-OWT*
**Русский:** отрешённый, рассеянный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spaced-out*, *most spaced-out*
**Form:** compound — participle + adverb

Not able to think clearly, as if your mind were somewhere else.

1. I was *spaced-out* from the cold medicine.
2. He gave me a *spaced-out* look and said nothing.
3. After the night shift she felt completely *spaced-out*.

---

### spacey

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspeɪsi/ &middot; *SPAY-see*
**Русский:** рассеянный, витающий в облаках
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *spacier*, *spaciest*
**Contrast:** Informal, and about a person's attention, not about space travel.

Dreamy and not paying attention to what is going on.

1. She's a little *spacey* before her first coffee.
2. He gets *spacier* when he hasn't slept.
3. He had a *spacey* grin and no idea where he was.

---

### spacious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspeɪʃəs/ &middot; *SPAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** просторный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spacious*, *most spacious*
**Form:** -ous from *space*
**Opposite:** *cramped*

Large inside, with plenty of room.

1. The kitchen is bright and *spacious*.
2. They moved into a *more spacious* apartment last spring.
3. The hotel lobby was surprisingly *spacious*.

---

### Spanish-speaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspænɪʃ ˌspiːkɪŋ/ &middot; *SPAN-ish-spee-king*
**Русский:** испаноязычный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Using Spanish as the main language.

1. She works in a *Spanish-speaking* neighborhood.
2. The clinic needs two *Spanish-speaking* nurses.
3. Mexico is the largest *Spanish-speaking* country in the world.

---

### spare

**Pronunciation:** /spɛr/ &middot; *SPAIR*
**Русский:** запасной, лишний; скупой, лаконичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spare*, *most spare*
**Contrast:** Before a noun *spare* usually means 'extra' (*a spare tire*); after a verb it more often means 'plain' or 'lean' (*the room was spare*).

Spare has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Extra, and kept in case you need it.** (запасной, лишний)

1. There's a *spare* key under the mat.
2. Do you have a *spare* minute?
3. We put the guests in the *spare* room.

**2. Plain and simple, with nothing added.** (скупой, лаконичный)

4. The room was *spare*: a bed, a chair, a lamp.
5. His prose is *spare* and fast.
6. The new design is *more spare* than the original.

**3. Thin, with no extra flesh.** (худощавый, сухощавый)

7. He was tall and *spare*.
8. Years of running left her lean and *spare*.

---

### sparing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *SPAIR-ing*
**Русский:** экономный, скупой (на что-л.)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sparing*, *most sparing*
**Takes:** *with* or *of* + noun; *in* + noun
**Form:** -ing from *spare*
**Contrast:** You will meet the adverb far more often: *use it sparingly*.

Using or giving only a little of something.

1. She is *sparing* with praise.
2. Be *sparing* with the salt.
3. *Sparing* use of color makes the room feel calm.

---

### sparkling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɑːrklɪŋ/ &middot; *SPAR-kling*
**Русский:** искрящийся; игристый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sparkling*, *most sparkling*

Sparkling has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Shining with many small points of light.** (искрящийся, сверкающий)

1. The lake was covered with *sparkling* ice.
2. She wore a *sparkling* silver dress.
3. His eyes were bright and *sparkling*.

**2. With bubbles in it, said of a drink.** (газированный, игристый)

4. Would you like still or *sparkling* water?
5. We opened a bottle of *sparkling* wine.

**3. Lively and brilliant, said of talk or a performance.** (блестящий, искромётный)

6. She gave a *sparkling* performance as Juliet.
7. The dinner was full of *sparkling* conversation.

---

### sparkly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɑːrkli/ &middot; *SPAR-klee*
**Русский:** блестящий, сверкающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sparkly*, *most sparkly*

Covered with small shiny points that catch the light.

1. She picked out a *sparkly* pair of shoes.
2. The card is covered in *sparkly* stars.
3. His new jacket is a little too *sparkly* for the office.

---

### sparse

**Pronunciation:** /spɑːrs/ &middot; *SPARS*
**Русский:** редкий, скудный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sparser*, *sparsest*
**Opposite:** *dense*

Small in number and spread thinly over a wide area.

1. The audience was *sparse* on Monday night.
2. *Sparse* pine trees dotted the hillside.
3. News about the crash is even *sparser* today than yesterday.

---

### spartan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɑːrtən/ &middot; *SPAR-tuhn*
**Русский:** спартанский, аскетичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spartan*, *most spartan*
**Form:** from *Sparta*, the ancient Greek city known for hard living
**Contrast:** Lowercase when it means plain and hard; capital *Spartan* means 'from ancient Sparta'.

Very plain and simple, with no comfort at all.

1. The dorm rooms are *spartan* but clean.
2. He keeps a *spartan* diet of rice and fish.
3. Her office is *spartan*: a desk, a chair, no pictures.

---

### spatial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspeɪʃəl/ &middot; *SPAY-shuhl*
**Русский:** пространственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *space*

Having to do with space and with where things sit in it.

1. Kids build *spatial* skills by playing with blocks.
2. She has excellent *spatial* awareness behind the wheel.
3. The test includes a *spatial* reasoning section.

---

### spayed

**Pronunciation:** /speɪd/ &middot; *SPAYD*
**Русский:** стерилизованная (о самке животного)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *spayed* is only for females; a male animal is *neutered*.

Operated on so that a female animal cannot have young.

1. All of our cats are *spayed*.
2. The shelter adopts out only *spayed* females.
3. She was already *spayed* when we got her.

---

### special

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛʃəl/ &middot; *SPESH-uhl*
**Русский:** особенный, особый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more special*, *most special*
**Takes:** *to* + person, for the 'important to someone' sense

Special has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not ordinary; better or more important than usual.** (особенный, необычный)

1. Today is a *special* day for our family.
2. You don't need anything *special*, just a pen.
3. That song is *more special* to me than any other.

**2. Meant for one particular purpose or group.** (специальный, особый)

4. He gets *special* treatment because of his last name.
5. The school has a *special* program for new arrivals.

---

### specialized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛʃəlaɪzd/ &middot; *SPESH-uh-lyzd*
**Русский:** специализированный, узкопрофильный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more specialized*, *most specialized*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *specialized*; British writing has *specialised*.

Made or trained for one narrow job or subject.

1. The surgery needs *specialized* equipment.
2. She has a *specialized* knowledge of tax law.
3. The work is *more specialized* than the title suggests.

---

### specific

**Pronunciation:** /spəˈsɪfɪk/ &middot; *spuh-SIF-ik*
**Русский:** конкретный, определённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more specific*, *most specific*
**Takes:** *to* + noun, for the 'belonging to one thing only' sense
**Preposition patterns:** [specific to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#specific-to)
**Opposite:** *general*

Specific has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Exact and clearly stated, not general.** (конкретный, точный)

1. Can you be *more specific* about the time?
2. She left me *specific* instructions.
3. I need a *specific* date, not 'sometime in May'.

**2. Belonging to one thing or group and to nothing else.** (свойственный только чему-л.)

4. This rule is *specific* to New York.
5. The disease is *specific* to one breed of dog.

---

### specified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛsɪfaɪd/ &middot; *SPES-uh-fyd*
**Русский:** указанный, оговорённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *specified* names the thing that was stated; *specific* describes how exact you are being.

Named or stated exactly, usually in writing.

1. Return the form by the *specified* date.
2. Use only the *specified* replacement parts.
3. Payment is due within the *specified* period.

---

### specious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspiːʃəs/ &middot; *SPEE-shuhs*
**Русский:** благовидный, ложно убедительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more specious*, *most specious*

Looking correct or reasonable at first, but actually false.

1. That's a *specious* argument.
2. The ad makes a *specious* claim about weight loss.
3. His reasoning sounds good, but it is *specious*.

---

### speckled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛkəld/ &middot; *SPEK-uhld*
**Русский:** крапчатый, пятнистый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more speckled*, *most speckled*
**Takes:** *with* + noun

Covered with small spots of another color.

1. The hen lays small brown *speckled* eggs.
2. His beard is *speckled* with gray.
3. The countertop is *speckled* black and white.

---

### speculative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛkjələtɪv/ &middot; *SPEK-yuh-luh-tiv*
**Русский:** предположительный; спекулятивный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more speculative*, *most speculative*

Speculative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Based on guessing rather than on facts.** (умозрительный, предположительный)

1. The report is *speculative* at this point.
2. That's a *speculative* explanation, nothing more.
3. Her second theory is even *more speculative* than the first.

**2. Done in the hope of a profit, with a real risk of losing the money.** (спекулятивный, рискованный)

4. He made a *speculative* bet on oil.
5. *Speculative* buying pushed housing prices up.

---

### speechless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspiːtʃləs/ &middot; *SPEECH-luhs*
**Русский:** лишившийся дара речи, онемевший
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — nearly always after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -less from *speech*

So surprised, angry or moved that you cannot say anything.

1. Her gift left me *speechless*.
2. He stood there *speechless* with rage.
3. I was *speechless* when they called my name.

---

### speedy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspiːdi/ &middot; *SPEE-dee*
**Русский:** быстрый, скорый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *speedier*, *speediest*
**Opposite:** *slow*

Happening or moving fast.

1. We wish you a *speedy* recovery.
2. Their reply was *speedier* than I expected.
3. He's a *speedy* little dog for his size.

---

### spellbinding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛlˌbaɪndɪŋ/ &middot; *SPEL-byn-ding*
**Русский:** завораживающий, захватывающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spellbinding*, *most spellbinding*
**Form:** compound — *spell* + *binding*
**Contrast:** *spellbinding* is the thing that holds you; *spellbound* is how you feel.

So interesting that you cannot look away or stop listening.

1. The last twenty minutes of the movie are *spellbinding*.
2. She told a *spellbinding* story about her escape.
3. His voice on the radio was *spellbinding*.

---

### spellbound

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛlbaʊnd/ &middot; *SPEL-bownd*
**Русский:** завороженный, очарованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *spell* + *bound*
**Contrast:** The listener is *spellbound*; the story is *spellbinding*.

So interested that you cannot move or look away, as if under a spell.

1. The children sat *spellbound* through the whole story.
2. The audience was *spellbound*.
3. He held us *spellbound* for an hour.

---

### spendthrift

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɛndθrɪft/ &middot; *SPEND-thrift*
**Русский:** расточительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *thrifty*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *he's a spendthrift*. The opposite quality is *thrifty*, not *spendthrifty*.

Spending money carelessly and far too fast.

1. His *spendthrift* habits ruined the business.
2. The paper attacked the mayor's *spendthrift* budget.
3. She married a charming *spendthrift* musician.

---

### spent

**Pronunciation:** /spɛnt/ &middot; *SPENT*
**Русский:** израсходованный; обессиленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also the past form of *spend*: *we spent the whole day there*.

Spent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Already used up, with nothing left in it.** (израсходованный, отработанный)

1. A pile of *spent* shells lay in the grass.
2. *Spent* fuel is stored on site.

**2. Completely out of energy.** (выбившийся из сил)

3. By the last mile I was *spent*.
4. The team looked *spent* after two overtimes.

---

### spherical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsfɛrɪkəl/ &middot; *SFER-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** сферический, шарообразный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *sphere*

Shaped like a ball.

1. The seeds are small and *spherical*.
2. The earth is not perfectly *spherical*.
3. They hung a *spherical* paper lamp over the table.

---

### spick-and-span

**Pronunciation:** /ˌspɪk ən ˈspæn/ &middot; *spik-uhn-SPAN*
**Русский:** безупречно чистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also written *spic-and-span*; both spellings are met in American writing.

Perfectly clean and tidy.

1. The kitchen was *spick-and-span* when we arrived.
2. She keeps the shop *spick-and-span*.
3. Everything was *spick-and-span* for the inspection.

---

### spicy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪsi/ &middot; *SPY-see*
**Русский:** острый, пряный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spicier*, *spiciest*

Spicy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a strong hot taste from pepper or other spices.** (острый, пряный)

1. This curry is *spicier* than the last one.
2. I ordered the *spicy* chicken.
3. She doesn't eat *spicy* food.

**2. A little shocking, about sex or scandal.** (пикантный, скабрёзный)

4. The book has a few *spicy* chapters.
5. He left out the *spicy* details.

---

### spiffy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɪfi/ &middot; *SPIF-ee*
**Русский:** нарядный, щегольской
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spiffier*, *spiffiest*

Neat and stylish, in a cheerful, informal way.

1. You look *spiffy* in that jacket.
2. They gave the lobby a *spiffy* new look.
3. His shoes were even *spiffier* than mine.

---

### spiked

**Pronunciation:** /spaɪkt/ &middot; *SPYKT*
**Русский:** с шипами; с добавлением алкоголя
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Spiked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having sharp points sticking out of it.** (шипованный, с шипами)

1. The dog wore a *spiked* collar.
2. *Spiked* shoes grip the ice.

**2. With alcohol or a drug secretly added.** (с подмешанным алкоголем)

3. Someone brought a bowl of *spiked* punch.
4. She thinks her drink was *spiked*.

---

### spiky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪki/ &middot; *SPY-kee*
**Русский:** колючий; торчащий
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spikier*, *spikiest*

Covered with sharp points, or standing up in stiff points.

1. The plant has long *spiky* leaves.
2. He had *spiky* black hair.
3. Her hair was even *spikier* in high school.

---

### spinal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪnəl/ &middot; *SPY-nuhl*
**Русский:** спинной, позвоночный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *spine*

Having to do with the backbone.

1. She had *spinal* surgery in May.
2. The accident damaged his *spinal* cord.
3. The doctor ordered a *spinal* X-ray.

---

### spindly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɪndli/ &middot; *SPIND-lee*
**Русский:** тонкий, долговязый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spindlier*, *spindliest*

Long, thin and weak-looking.

1. The tomato plant had *spindly* stems.
2. A *spindly* teenager answered the door.
3. The chair's legs look even *spindlier* than they are.

---

### spineless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪnləs/ &middot; *SPYN-luhs*
**Русский:** бесхребетный, малодушный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spineless*, *most spineless*
**Form:** -less from *spine*

Spineless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too weak to stand up for yourself or for other people.** (бесхребетный, безвольный)

1. The committee was *spineless* about the whole thing.
2. He's too *spineless* to say no.
3. Critics called the statement *spineless*.

**2. Having no backbone, or no sharp spines.** (беспозвоночный; без колючек)

4. Jellyfish are *spineless* animals.
5. They planted a *spineless* variety of cactus.

---

### spiral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪrəl/ &middot; *SPY-ruhl*
**Русский:** спиральный, винтовой
**Type:** shape
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a spiral of smoke*) and a verb (*prices spiraled*).

Winding in a curve around a center, going up or outward.

1. We took the *spiral* staircase to the roof.
2. She writes in a *spiral* notebook.
3. The shell has a tight *spiral* pattern.

---

### spiraling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪrəlɪŋ/ &middot; *SPY-ruh-ling*
**Русский:** стремительно растущий, выходящий из-под контроля
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*: *spiraling*; British writing doubles it.

Rising fast and getting out of control.

1. *Spiraling* rents pushed families out of the city.
2. They blamed *spiraling* costs for the delay.
3. The country faces a *spiraling* debt problem.

---

### spirited

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɪrɪtɪd/ &middot; *SPIR-ih-tid*
**Русский:** энергичный, темпераментный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spirited*, *most spirited*
**Form:** -ed from *spirit*
**Contrast:** Very common in compounds: *high-spirited*, *low-spirited*, *public-spirited*.

Full of energy and determination.

1. They put up a *spirited* defense.
2. It was a *spirited* debate that ran past midnight.
3. Her playing is *more spirited* than technically perfect.

---

### spiritual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɪrɪtʃuəl/ &middot; *SPIR-ih-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** духовный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spiritual*, *most spiritual*
**Opposite:** *material*

Spiritual has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with the soul and the inner life rather than the body or money.** (духовный, внутренний)

1. She went on a *spiritual* retreat.
2. The book asks *spiritual* questions.
3. He became *more spiritual* after his illness.

**2. Connected with religion and its teaching.** (духовный, религиозный)

4. The monks are the village's *spiritual* leaders.
5. She turned to her *spiritual* adviser.

---

### spiteful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaɪtfəl/ &middot; *SPYT-fuhl*
**Русский:** злобный, мстительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spiteful*, *most spiteful*
**Form:** -ful from *spite*
**Opposite:** *kind*

Wanting to hurt someone on purpose, out of bad feeling.

1. That was a *spiteful* thing to say.
2. She gets *spiteful* when she loses.
3. He wrote a *spiteful* review of his old boss's book.

---

### splashy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsplæʃi/ &middot; *SPLASH-ee*
**Русский:** броский, яркий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *splashier*, *splashiest*

Big, bright and meant to be noticed.

1. The company made a *splashy* announcement.
2. She wore a *splashy* red coat.
3. This year's opening was even *splashier* than last year's.

---

### splendid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsplɛndɪd/ &middot; *SPLEN-did*
**Русский:** великолепный, роскошный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more splendid*, *most splendid*

Very good, or beautiful and impressive to look at.

1. We had a *splendid* time in Chicago.
2. The view from the top is *splendid*.
3. She wore a *splendid* green dress.

---

### split

**Pronunciation:** /splɪt/ &middot; *SPLIT*
**Русский:** расколотый, разделённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *over* or *on* + noun, for the 'divided in opinion' sense
**Contrast:** Also a verb whose forms never change: *split*, *split*, *split*.

Split has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Divided into parts, or into two sides that disagree.** (разделённый, расколотый)

1. The court reached a *split* decision.
2. The town is *split* over the new school.

**2. Broken open along a line.** (треснувший, расщеплённый)

3. She trimmed her *split* ends.
4. The bag was *split* down the side.

---

### splitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsplɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *SPLIT-ing*
**Русский:** раскалывающий (о головной боли)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — almost always with *headache*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Very bad, said of a headache that feels as if your head were breaking open.

1. I woke up with a *splitting* headache.
2. She had a *splitting* headache all afternoon.
3. The noise gave him a *splitting* headache.

---

### spoiled

**Pronunciation:** /spɔɪld/ &middot; *SPOYLD*
**Русский:** избалованный; испорченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spoiled*, *most spoiled*
**Contrast:** American English uses *spoiled* for both meanings; *spoilt* is British.

Spoiled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Given everything and allowed to behave badly.** (избалованный)

1. He's a *spoiled* kid who never hears no.
2. Their youngest is the *most spoiled* of the three.

**2. No longer good to eat.** (испорченный, протухший)

3. The milk was *spoiled*.
4. Throw out any *spoiled* meat.

---

### spoken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspoʊkən/ &middot; *SPOH-kuhn*
**Русский:** устный, разговорный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also the past participle of *speak*, and the second half of *soft-spoken*, *well-spoken*.

Said out loud, not written down.

1. Her *spoken* English is better than her writing.
2. The exam has a *spoken* section.
3. We had a *spoken* agreement, nothing on paper.

---

### spongy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspʌndʒi/ &middot; *SPUN-jee*
**Русский:** губчатый, пружинящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spongier*, *spongiest*

Soft and full of air, springing back when you press it.

1. The ground was *spongy* after the rain.
2. She baked a light, *spongy* cake.
3. The old mattress got *spongier* every year.

---

### spontaneous

**Pronunciation:** /spɑːnˈteɪniəs/ &middot; *spahn-TAY-nee-uhs*
**Русский:** спонтанный, непроизвольный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spontaneous*, *most spontaneous*

Done suddenly, out of feeling and without planning.

1. We took a *spontaneous* trip to the coast.
2. The crowd broke into *spontaneous* applause.
3. She's *more spontaneous* than her sister.

---

### spooky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspuːki/ &middot; *SPOO-kee*
**Русский:** жуткий, страшноватый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spookier*, *spookiest*

Strange and a little frightening.

1. The old house looks *spooky* at night.
2. That's a *spooky* coincidence.
3. The basement is even *spookier* with the lights off.

---
