### sporadic

**Pronunciation:** /spəˈrædɪk/ &middot; *spuh-RAD-ik*
**Русский:** спорадический, нерегулярный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sporadic*, *most sporadic*
**Opposite:** *constant*

Happening now and then, with no regular pattern.

1. There was *sporadic* gunfire through the night.
2. Her attendance has been *sporadic* all semester.
3. We get *sporadic* emails from him.

---

### sporting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɔːrtɪŋ/ &middot; *SPOR-ting*
**Русский:** спортивный; благородный, честный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Sporting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with sports.** (спортивный)

1. He buys his gear at a *sporting* goods store.
2. The town has a long *sporting* tradition.

**2. Fair, giving the other side a real chance.** (честный, благородный)

3. You have a *sporting* chance of winning.
4. Letting him go first was a *sporting* gesture.

---

### sportsmanlike

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɔːrtsmənlaɪk/ &middot; *SPORTS-muhn-lyk*
**Русский:** спортивный (о поведении), честный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sportsmanlike*, *most sportsmanlike*
**Form:** compound — *sportsman* + -like
**Opposite:** *unsportsmanlike*

Behaving fairly and with respect toward the other side in a game.

1. Shaking hands after the loss was *sportsmanlike*.
2. He made a *sportsmanlike* apology to the referee.
3. Her response was gracious and *sportsmanlike*.

---

### sporty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɔːrti/ &middot; *SPOR-tee*
**Русский:** спортивный (по виду); увлекающийся спортом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sportier*, *sportiest*

Sporty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Looking fast and stylish, the way sports cars and sports clothes do.** (спортивного вида)

1. She drives a *sporty* little hatchback.
2. The new model is *sportier* than the old one.

**2. Good at sports, or fond of playing them.** (спортивный, любящий спорт)

3. He's the *sporty* one in the family.
4. She was never very *sporty*.

---

### spotted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɑːtɪd/ &middot; *SPAH-tid*
**Русский:** пятнистый, в крапинку
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also the past of *spot*: *I spotted her in the crowd*.

Covered with round marks of another color.

1. A *spotted* dog ran across the yard.
2. She wore a black and white *spotted* scarf.
3. The eggs are pale blue and *spotted*.

---

### spotty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspɑːti/ &middot; *SPAH-tee*
**Русский:** неровный, нестабильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spottier*, *spottiest*
**Contrast:** In American English *spotty* means uneven; in British English it usually means 'covered in pimples'.

Good in some parts and bad in others; not steady.

1. The cell service out here is *spotty*.
2. He has a *spotty* work record.
3. Coverage gets *spottier* once you leave town.

---

### spousal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspaʊzəl/ &middot; *SPOW-zuhl*
**Русский:** супружеский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *spouse*

Having to do with a husband or a wife.

1. She receives *spousal* support.
2. The plan offers *spousal* benefits.
3. The form asks for *spousal* consent.

---

### sprawling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsprɔːlɪŋ/ &middot; *SPRAW-ling*
**Русский:** раскинувшийся, разросшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sprawling*, *most sprawling*

Spreading out over a large area without a neat shape.

1. They live on a *sprawling* ranch outside Austin.
2. Phoenix is a *sprawling* city.
3. The novel is a *sprawling* family story.

---

### sprightly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspraɪtli/ &middot; *SPRYT-lee*
**Русский:** бодрый, резвый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sprightlier*, *sprightliest*

Lively and quick on your feet, often said of an older person.

1. Her grandmother is still *sprightly* at ninety.
2. He walked down the hall with a *sprightly* step.
3. She seemed *sprightlier* after the trip.

---

### spring-loaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsprɪŋ ˌloʊdɪd/ &middot; *SPRING-loh-did*
**Русский:** подпружиненный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Fitted with a spring, so that it shuts or pops open by itself.

1. The box has a *spring-loaded* lid.
2. The gate is *spring-loaded* and closes behind you.
3. Use a *spring-loaded* clamp to hold the boards.

---

### springlike

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsprɪŋlaɪk/ &middot; *SPRING-lyk*
**Русский:** весенний, по-весеннему тёплый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more springlike*, *most springlike*
**Form:** compound — *spring* + -like

Warm and fresh, the way early spring feels.

1. We had a *springlike* afternoon in February.
2. The weather turned *springlike* over the weekend.
3. There was a *springlike* smell in the air.

---

### springy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsprɪŋi/ &middot; *SPRING-ee*
**Русский:** упругий, пружинистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *springier*, *springiest*

Going back into shape right after you press or step on it.

1. The new track feels *springy* underfoot.
2. She has a *springy* walk.
3. This mattress is *springier* than the old one.

---

### spry

**Pronunciation:** /spraɪ/ &middot; *SPRY*
**Русский:** бодрый, шустрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spryer*, *spryest*
**Contrast:** The comparative forms are also spelled *sprier*, *spriest*.

Still active and quick, especially at an older age.

1. He's *spry* for eighty-five.
2. A *spry* old man sold us the tickets.
3. She's *spryer* than people half her age.

---

### spunky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspʌŋki/ &middot; *SPUNG-kee*
**Русский:** задорный, боевой
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *spunkier*, *spunkiest*

Brave and full of energy, often said of someone small.

1. She's a *spunky* little kid.
2. The team is young and *spunky*.
3. The *spunkiest* of the puppies got picked first.

---

### spur-of-the-moment

**Pronunciation:** /ˌspɜːr əv ðə ˈmoʊmənt/ &middot; *spur-uhv-thuh-MOH-muhnt*
**Русский:** спонтанный, импульсивный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Before a noun it takes hyphens; after a verb it is the phrase *on the spur of the moment*.

Decided suddenly, with no planning at all.

1. It was a *spur-of-the-moment* decision.
2. They took a *spur-of-the-moment* trip to Vegas.
3. This was a *spur-of-the-moment* purchase.

---

### spurious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈspjʊriəs/ &middot; *SPYUUR-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** ложный, поддельный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more spurious*, *most spurious*

False, though made to look real or reasonable.

1. The site is full of *spurious* health claims.
2. He was arrested on *spurious* charges.
3. The link between the two numbers is *spurious*.

---

### squalid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwɑːlɪd/ &middot; *SKWAH-lid*
**Русский:** убогий, грязный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more squalid*, *most squalid*

Squalid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dirty, run-down and unpleasant to live in.** (убогий, запущенный)

1. The families live in *squalid* conditions.
2. It was a *squalid* little room over a bar.
3. The camp was *squalid* and overcrowded.

**2. Low and dishonest, with nothing decent about it.** (низкий, гнусный)

4. The trial revealed a *squalid* deal between the two men.
5. It's a *squalid* little scandal.

---

### square-jawed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌskwɛr ˈdʒɔːd/ &middot; *skwair-JAWD*
**Русский:** с квадратной челюстью
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed

Having a strong, square-looking jaw.

1. A *square-jawed* officer met us at the door.
2. He is tall and *square-jawed*.
3. The poster showed a *square-jawed* hero in uniform.

---

### squat

**Pronunciation:** /skwɑːt/ &middot; *SKWAHT*
**Русский:** приземистый, коренастый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *squatter*, *squattest*
**Contrast:** The comparative *squatter* looks exactly like the noun *a squatter*, someone living in an empty building.

Short and thick, sitting low to the ground.

1. A *squat* brick building stood on the corner.
2. The bottle is *squat* and heavy.
3. He was *squatter* and stronger than his brother.

---

### squeaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwiːki/ &middot; *SKWEE-kee*
**Русский:** скрипучий, писклявый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *squeakier*, *squeakiest*
**Contrast:** In *squeaky clean* it means 'completely clean', or 'with no scandal in your past'.

Making a short high sound when it moves, or when someone speaks.

1. The third stair is *squeaky*.
2. He has a thin, *squeaky* voice.
3. My desk chair gets *squeakier* every week.

---

### squeamish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwiːmɪʃ/ &middot; *SKWEE-mish*
**Русский:** брезгливый, впечатлительный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more squeamish*, *most squeamish*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*

Easily made sick or upset by blood, dirt or anything unpleasant.

1. I'm too *squeamish* to watch surgery.
2. Don't read this if you're *squeamish* about needles.
3. He gets *squeamish* around blood.

---

### squeezable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwiːzəbəl/ &middot; *SKWEE-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** мягкий, поддающийся сжатию
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more squeezable*, *most squeezable*
**Form:** -able from *squeeze*

Soft enough to press with your hand.

1. The honey comes in a *squeezable* bottle.
2. The toy is soft and *squeezable*.
3. Pick an avocado that is just *squeezable*.

---

### squiggly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwɪɡli/ &middot; *SKWIG-lee*
**Русский:** волнистый, извилистый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more squiggly*, *most squiggly*

Made of short wavy lines that twist back and forth.

1. He drew a *squiggly* line under the word.
2. The chart has a *squiggly* red arrow on it.
3. Her signature is just a *squiggly* mark.

---

### squishy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈskwɪʃi/ &middot; *SKWISH-ee*
**Русский:** мягкий, податливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *squishier*, *squishiest*

Soft and wet enough to change shape when you press it.

1. The mud was *squishy* under our boots.
2. She gave me a *squishy* stress ball.
3. These peaches are *squishier* than I like.

---

### Sri Lankan

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsriː ˈlɑːŋkən/ &middot; *sree-LAHNG-kuhn*
**Русский:** шри-ланкийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Both words keep a capital letter, and *a Sri Lankan* is also the noun for a person.

From Sri Lanka, the island country south of India.

1. We had dinner at a *Sri Lankan* restaurant.
2. Her parents are *Sri Lankan*.
3. *Sri Lankan* tea is sold all over the world.

---

### stabbing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstæbɪŋ/ &middot; *STAB-ing*
**Русский:** колющий (о боли)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the crime: *a stabbing downtown*.

Sudden and sharp, said of a pain that feels like a knife.

1. She felt a *stabbing* pain in her side.
2. A *stabbing* ache ran up his leg.
3. The headache came in *stabbing* bursts.

---

### stabilizing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪbəlaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *STAY-buh-ly-zing*
**Русский:** стабилизирующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American spelling is *stabilizing*; British writing has *stabilising*.

Making something steadier and less likely to change or fall.

1. Her grandmother was a *stabilizing* force in the family.
2. The drug has a *stabilizing* effect on the heart rate.
3. Two *stabilizing* cables hold the tower in place.

---

### stable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪbəl/ &middot; *STAY-buhl*
**Русский:** устойчивый, стабильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stable*, *most stable*
**Opposite:** *unstable*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a stable* is a building for horses.

Stable has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Firm and not likely to move or fall over.** (устойчивый, прочный)

1. Make sure the ladder is *stable* before you climb.
2. Put the heavy books on the bottom shelf to keep the case *stable*.

**2. Staying about the same; not changing much.** (стабильный, постоянный)

3. Prices have been *stable* all year.
4. She's looking for a *stable* job with benefits.

**3. Calm and not easily upset, or no longer getting worse.** (уравновешенный; в стабильном состоянии)

5. He's *more stable* now that he's back on his medication.
6. The patient is in *stable* condition after surgery.

---

### stacked

**Pronunciation:** /stækt/ &middot; *STAKT*
**Русский:** сложенный стопкой; заставленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** In *the odds are stacked against us* it means the situation was arranged unfairly.

Piled up, or covered with things in piles.

1. The table was *stacked* with books.
2. *Stacked* boxes filled the hallway.
3. The sink is *stacked* high with dishes.

---

### staged

**Pronunciation:** /steɪdʒd/ &middot; *STAYJD*
**Русский:** постановочный; поэтапный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Staged has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Set up to look real when it is not.** (постановочный, инсценированный)

1. The photo was obviously *staged*.
2. It was a *staged* protest for the cameras.
3. The fight at the end looked *staged*.

**2. Done in planned steps, one after another.** (поэтапный)

4. The company announced a *staged* rollout.
5. They agreed to a *staged* withdrawal of troops.

---

### staggering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstæɡərɪŋ/ &middot; *STAG-ur-ing*
**Русский:** ошеломляющий, потрясающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more staggering*, *most staggering*

So big or so surprising that it is hard to believe.

1. The repairs cost a *staggering* amount of money.
2. A *staggering* number of people showed up.
3. The team's comeback was *staggering*.

---

### stagnant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstæɡnənt/ &middot; *STAG-nuhnt*
**Русский:** стоячий; застойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stagnant*, *most stagnant*

Stagnant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not moving, and often dirty, said of water.** (стоячий, затхлый)

1. Mosquitoes breed in *stagnant* water.
2. The pond was green and *stagnant*.

**2. Not growing or changing when it should be.** (застойный, топчущийся на месте)

3. Wages have been *stagnant* for a decade.
4. The housing market is *stagnant* right now.

---

### staid

**Pronunciation:** /steɪd/ &middot; *STAYD*
**Русский:** чопорный, степенный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more staid*, *most staid*
**Contrast:** Sounds exactly like *stayed*, the past of *stay*.

Serious, quiet and rather old-fashioned.

1. The firm has a *staid* image.
2. He is less *staid* than he looks.
3. It was a *staid* dinner party with no music.

---

### stained

**Pronunciation:** /steɪnd/ &middot; *STAYND*
**Русский:** в пятнах, запачканный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** *stained glass* is colored on purpose, so it is the one place where *stained* is not a complaint.

Marked with dirt or color that will not come out.

1. His shirt was *stained* with coffee.
2. She threw out the *stained* tablecloth.
3. The ceiling is *stained* from the leak.

---

### stainless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪnləs/ &middot; *STAYN-luhs*
**Русский:** нержавеющий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — almost always about steel
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *stain*

Made of steel that does not rust or stain.

1. We put in a *stainless* sink.
2. The pot is heavy *stainless* steel.
3. All the appliances are *stainless*.

---

### stale

**Pronunciation:** /steɪl/ &middot; *STAYL*
**Русский:** чёрствый, несвежий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *staler*, *stalest*
**Opposite:** *fresh*

Stale has 2 distinct senses.

**1. No longer fresh, said of food or air.** (чёрствый, затхлый)

1. The bread is *stale*.
2. She threw out a bag of *stale* chips.
3. The air in the room was *stale* and warm.

**2. No longer new or interesting.** (избитый, приевшийся)

4. His jokes get *staler* every year.
5. The whole argument feels *stale* by now.

---

### stalemated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪlmeɪtɪd/ &middot; *STAYL-may-tid*
**Русский:** зашедший в тупик
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *stalemate*, a drawn position in chess

Stuck, with neither side able to win or move forward.

1. The talks are *stalemated*.
2. A *stalemated* war dragged on for years.
3. The vote left the council *stalemated*.

---

### Stalinist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːlənɪst/ &middot; *STAH-luh-nist*
**Русский:** сталинский, сталинистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized, and also a noun: *a Stalinist*.

Having to do with Stalin's rule, or with that kind of harsh, total control.

1. She wrote a book about the *Stalinist* purges.
2. The ministry is a heavy *Stalinist* building.
3. His management style is almost *Stalinist*.

---

### stalwart

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɔːlwərt/ &middot; *STAWL-wurt*
**Русский:** стойкий, верный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stalwart*, *most stalwart*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a party stalwart* is a long-time loyal member.

Loyal and dependable, staying with something through hard times.

1. She was a *stalwart* supporter of the library.
2. He remained a *stalwart* ally to the end.
3. The team's *most stalwart* fans sat through the rain.

---

### stand-alone

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstænd əˌloʊn/ &middot; *STAND-uh-lohn*
**Русский:** автономный, самостоятельный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + adverb

Working on its own, without needing anything else.

1. It's a *stand-alone* app, with no account needed.
2. The novel is a *stand-alone* story, not part of a series.
3. They sell a *stand-alone* version of the tool.

---

### stand-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstænd ʌp/ &middot; *STAND-up*
**Русский:** сольный комедийный; порядочный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the art form: *she does stand-up on weekends*.

Stand-up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done alone in front of an audience, telling jokes.** (стендап-, сольный комедийный)

1. He does *stand-up* comedy on weekends.
2. She got her start at a *stand-up* club in Chicago.

**2. Honest and dependable, in informal speech.** (порядочный, надёжный)

3. He's a *stand-up* guy.
4. Thanks for covering for me — that was a *stand-up* move.

---

### standard

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstændərd/ &middot; *STAN-durd*
**Русский:** стандартный, обычный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a high standard*, *safety standards*.

Standard has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Usual and normal; what you would expect.** (обычный, типовой)

1. That's the *standard* answer they give everyone.
2. A two-year warranty is *standard* on this model.

**2. The same for everyone, and officially accepted.** (стандартный, единый)

3. All the rooms have a *standard* layout.
4. The test uses *standard* written English.

---

### standardized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstændərdaɪzd/ &middot; *STAN-dur-dyzd*
**Русский:** стандартизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American spelling is *standardized*; British writing has *standardised*.

Made the same everywhere, by one set of rules.

1. The kids take *standardized* tests in March.
2. Parts are *standardized* across all the models.
3. *Standardized* forms make the paperwork faster.

---

### standby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstændbaɪ/ &middot; *STAND-by*
**Русский:** резервный; без брони (о билете)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun and the phrase *on standby*: *the crew is on standby*.

Kept ready in case it is needed, or waiting for a free place.

1. The hospital has a *standby* generator.
2. She got a *standby* ticket for the late flight.
3. There's a long *standby* list at the gate.

---

### standing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstændɪŋ/ &middot; *STAN-ding*
**Русский:** постоянный, действующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*in good standing*) and the participle of *stand*.

Standing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Always there, going on without having to be arranged again.** (постоянный, действующий)

1. We have a *standing* invitation to their place.
2. It's a *standing* joke in the office.
3. The committee is a *standing* one, not temporary.

**2. Done on your feet.** (стоячий)

4. She got a *standing* ovation.
5. By eight o'clock there was only *standing* room left.

---

### standoffish

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstændˈɔːfɪʃ/ &middot; *stand-AW-fish*
**Русский:** сдержанный, неприветливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more standoffish*, *most standoffish*
**Opposite:** *friendly*

Cool and unfriendly, keeping other people at a distance.

1. She seemed *standoffish* at first.
2. Don't be *standoffish* with the new neighbors.
3. He isn't rude, just a little *standoffish*.

---

### staple

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪpəl/ &middot; *STAY-puhl*
**Русский:** основной, главный (о продукте)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun twice over: *rice is a staple*, and the little metal *staple* that holds paper together.

Used or eaten all the time; basic and main.

1. Rice is a *staple* food in much of Asia.
2. Beans are a *staple* crop in this region.
3. Jeans are a *staple* item in her closet.

---

### star

**Pronunciation:** /stɑːr/ &middot; *STAR*
**Русский:** звёздный, выдающийся
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a movie star*, *the stars came out*.

Best and most admired in a group.

1. He's the team's *star* player.
2. She was the *star* witness at the trial.
3. Their *star* student won a scholarship.

---

### star-shaped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːr ʃeɪpt/ &middot; *STAR-shaypt*
**Русский:** звёздообразный, в форме звезды
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Having the shape of a star.

1. She cut the cookies into *star-shaped* pieces.
2. The leaf is *star-shaped*.
3. They hung *star-shaped* lights over the porch.

---

### starchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːrtʃi/ &middot; *STAR-chee*
**Русский:** крахмалистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *starchier*, *starchiest*

Containing a lot of starch.

1. Potatoes and rice are *starchy* foods.
2. The doctor told him to cut back on *starchy* side dishes.
3. This variety of potato is *starchier* than the red one.

---

### stark

**Pronunciation:** /stɑːrk/ &middot; *STARK*
**Русский:** резкий, разительный; голый, суровый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *starker*, *starkest*
**Contrast:** In *stark naked* and *stark raving mad* it is an old adverb meaning 'completely'.

Stark has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very clear and hard to ignore, often unpleasantly so.** (разительный, резкий)

1. The photos are a *stark* reminder of the war.
2. The two reports stand in *stark* contrast.
3. The difference between the neighborhoods is even *starker* today.

**2. Bare and empty, with nothing soft or decorative.** (голый, суровый)

4. The room was *stark* and white.
5. They drove through a *stark* landscape of rock and ice.

---

### stark naked

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstɑːrk ˈneɪkɪd/ &middot; *stahrk NAY-kid*
**Русский:** совершенно голый
**Type:** quality; also emphasizing
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *he was stark naked*, not *a stark naked man*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *stark* here means 'completely'
**Contrast:** *Stark* as an intensifier lives in only a couple of fixed phrases: *stark naked*, *stark raving mad*. On its own *stark* means bare or harsh: *a stark white room*.

Wearing no clothes at all.

1. He ran out of the sauna *stark naked*.
2. The baby was *stark naked* and perfectly happy about it.
3. I opened the door and there he stood, *stark naked*.

---

### starlit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːrlɪt/ &middot; *STAHR-lit*
**Русский:** звёздный, освещённый звёздами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a starlit night*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *star* + *lit*

Lit only by the light of the stars.

1. They walked home along a *starlit* beach.
2. We ate dinner outside on a warm, *starlit* evening.
3. The *starlit* sky over the desert was unbelievable.

---

### starry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːri/ &middot; *STAHR-ee*
**Русский:** звёздный, усыпанный звёздами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *starrier*, *starriest*
**Form:** -y from *star*

Full of stars, or showing a lot of stars.

1. It was a cold, *starry* night in late October.
2. The sky gets *starrier* the farther you drive from the city.
3. Out in the desert the sky was *starry* from one horizon to the other.

---

### starry-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstɑːriˈaɪd/ &middot; *stahr-ee-YD*
**Русский:** наивно-восторженный, витающий в облаках
**Type:** quality; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more starry-eyed*, *most starry-eyed*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** compound — *starry* + *-eyed*

So full of hope and admiration that you cannot see the problems.

1. He arrived in Los Angeles *starry-eyed* and broke.
2. She is still *starry-eyed* about the new job.
3. A few *starry-eyed* fans waited outside the hotel all night.

---

### starting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːrtɪŋ/ &middot; *STAHR-ting*
**Русский:** начальный, стартовый; основной (о составе)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a starting salary*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *start*

Starting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. At the beginning of something, before anything changes.** (начальный, стартовый, исходный)

1. The *starting* salary is fifty thousand a year.
2. Let's take her question as our *starting* point.
3. The *starting* price for the base model is under thirty thousand.

**2. In sports: chosen to play from the first minute, not from the bench.** (основной, стартовый (об игроке, составе))

4. He is the *starting* quarterback this season.
5. She made the *starting* lineup as a freshman.

---

### startled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːrtəld/ &middot; *STAHR-tuhld*
**Русский:** испуганный, вздрогнувший от неожиданности
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more startled*, *most startled*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Contrast:** *startled* is the person, *startling* is the thing that does it: *a startled cat*, *a startling noise*.

Suddenly surprised or a little frightened by something you did not expect.

1. She looked *startled* when I said her name.
2. A *startled* deer jumped out of the ditch.
3. He was even *more startled* by the news than I was.

---

### startling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːrtlɪŋ/ &middot; *STAHR-tling*
**Русский:** поразительный, ошеломляющий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more startling*, *most startling*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** The *-ing* form is the thing; the *-ed* form is the person: *a startling result*, *a startled reader*.

So surprising that it makes you stop short.

1. The report contains some *startling* numbers.
2. There is a *startling* resemblance between the two brothers.
3. The results were *more startling* than anyone had expected.

---

### starved

**Pronunciation:** /stɑːrvd/ &middot; *STAHRVD*
**Русский:** лишённый (чего-л.); изголодавшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more starved*, *most starved*
**Takes:** *of* or *for* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *starve*

Starved has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not given enough of something it badly needs.** (лишённый необходимого, обделённый)

1. Rural hospitals have been *starved* of money for years.
2. Those kids are *starved* for attention.
3. A plant *starved* of light will never flower.

**2. Informal: extremely hungry.** (страшно голодный, изголодавшийся)

4. I'm absolutely *starved* — when is dinner?
5. We were *starved* by the time we got home.

---

### state-controlled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪt kənˌtroʊld/ &middot; *STAYT-kuhn-trohld*
**Русский:** государственный, контролируемый государством
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle
**Opposite:** *private*

Owned and run by the government rather than by private owners.

1. *State-controlled* television carried the speech live.
2. Most of the oil industry there is *state-controlled*.
3. The paper is *state-controlled*, so nobody believes a word of it.

---

### stated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪtɪd/ &middot; *STAY-tid*
**Русский:** заявленный, указанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the stated reason*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *state* (to say officially)

Given officially as the reason or the aim — often with a hint that the real one is different.

1. Her *stated* reason for leaving was the commute.
2. The *stated* goal of the program is to cut emissions in half.
3. He never goes beyond the company's *stated* policy.

---

### stateless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪtləs/ &middot; *STAYT-luhs*
**Русский:** не имеющий гражданства, апатрид
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *state* (country)

Having no country that recognizes you as a citizen.

1. Thousands of children are born *stateless* every year.
2. A *stateless* person cannot get a passport.
3. The treaty is meant to protect *stateless* refugees.

---

### stately

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪtli/ &middot; *STAYT-lee*
**Русский:** величественный, представительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stately*, *most stately*
**Form:** -ly adjective from *state* (grandeur) — not an adverb
**Contrast:** *Stately* ends in *-ly* but is an adjective: *a stately home*, never *he walked stately*.

Large, dignified and impressive, in a slow and formal way.

1. They live in a *stately* old house on the hill.
2. The procession moved at a *stately* pace.
3. The hotel lobby is *stately* but a little cold.

---

### statewide

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsteɪtˈwaɪd/ &middot; *stayt-WYD*
**Русский:** общештатный, в масштабе штата
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *state* + *-wide*

Covering a whole US state.

1. The ban is now *statewide*.
2. She won the *statewide* election by nine points.
3. There is a *statewide* shortage of math teachers.

---

### static

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstætɪk/ &middot; *STAT-ik*
**Русский:** статичный, неподвижный, неизменный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more static*, *most static*
**Opposite:** *dynamic*

Static has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not moving and not changing.** (неподвижный, неизменный, застывший)

1. Prices have stayed *static* since the spring.
2. The design is completely *static* — nothing on the page moves.
3. The population of the town is more or less *static*.

**2. Of electricity that builds up on a surface instead of flowing.** (статический (об электричестве))

4. *Static* electricity made her hair stand straight up.
5. The carpet gives me a *static* shock every winter.

---

### stationary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsteɪʃəˌnɛri/ &middot; *STAY-shuh-nair-ee*
**Русский:** неподвижный, стоящий на месте
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *moving*
**Contrast:** *stationary* (not moving) and *stationery* (paper and envelopes, a noun) sound the same: *a stationary car*, *a box of stationery*.

Standing still; not moving.

1. The truck hit a *stationary* car at the light.
2. Traffic was *stationary* for almost an hour.
3. She rides a *stationary* bike at the gym every morning.

---

### statistical

**Pronunciation:** /stəˈtɪstɪkəl/ &middot; *stuh-TIS-tih-kuhl*
**Русский:** статистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *statistical evidence*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *statistics*

Having to do with numbers collected and analyzed to show a pattern.

1. There is no *statistical* evidence for that claim.
2. The paper includes a full *statistical* analysis of the survey.
3. A *statistical* error of two percent is normal here.

---

### statuesque

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstætʃuˈɛsk/ &middot; *stach-oo-ESK*
**Русский:** статная, величественная
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more statuesque*, *most statuesque*
**Form:** -esque from *statue*

Of a woman: tall, well-built and dignified, like a statue.

1. A *statuesque* woman in a red dress crossed the lobby.
2. She is *statuesque* and towers over everyone in the photo.
3. The actress looked *statuesque* on the red carpet.

---

### statutory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstætʃəˌtɔːri/ &middot; *STACH-uh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** установленный законом, законодательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a statutory limit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ory from *statute*

Required or fixed by a written law.

1. There is a *statutory* limit on how much they can charge.
2. Employers have to give the *statutory* minimum notice.
3. Missing the deadline carries a *statutory* penalty.

---

### staunch

**Pronunciation:** /stɔːntʃ/ &middot; *STAWNCH*
**Русский:** верный, стойкий, убеждённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *stauncher*, *staunchest*
**Opposite:** *half-hearted*

Very loyal and firm in supporting a person, a party or a belief.

1. She is a *staunch* supporter of the public library.
2. He became one of her *staunchest* defenders.
3. The senator is a *staunch* opponent of the tax.

---

### stay-at-home

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsteɪ ət ˈhoʊm/ &middot; *stay-uht-HOHM*
**Русский:** сидящий дома (о родителе), домашний
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stay-at-home dad*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — a whole phrase used as one adjective

Staying at home to raise the children instead of working outside the house.

1. He has been a *stay-at-home* dad for six years.
2. Her mother was a *stay-at-home* mom in the seventies.
3. The article compares *stay-at-home* and working parents.

---

### steadfast

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛdfæst/ &middot; *STED-fast*
**Русский:** непоколебимый, стойкий, верный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more steadfast*, *most steadfast*
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** compound — *stead* (place) + *fast* (firmly fixed)
**Opposite:** *wavering*

Staying loyal or firm and refusing to change, whatever happens.

1. She has been a *steadfast* friend for thirty years.
2. He was *steadfast* in his refusal to sign.
3. Their support for the team stayed *steadfast* through a terrible season.

---

### steady

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛdi/ &middot; *STED-ee*
**Русский:** устойчивый; ровный, постоянный; надёжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *steadier*, *steadiest*
**Opposite:** *shaky*

Steady has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Firm and not shaking or moving.** (устойчивый, твёрдый, не дрожащий)

1. Hold the ladder *steady* while I climb up.
2. A surgeon needs *steady* hands.
3. His voice was *steadier* than I expected.

**2. Going on at the same rate without stopping or jumping around.** (ровный, постоянный, равномерный)

4. There has been a *steady* rise in rents since 2020.
5. We drove at a *steady* sixty miles an hour.
6. A *steady* rain fell all afternoon.

**3. Regular and dependable, so you can count on it.** (надёжный, постоянный (о работе, человеке))

7. He finally found a *steady* job with benefits.
8. She has a *steady* boyfriend now.

---

### stealthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛlθi/ &middot; *STEL-thee*
**Русский:** скрытный, крадущийся, незаметный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stealthier*, *stealthiest*
**Form:** -y from *stealth*

Moving or done quietly and secretly so that nobody notices.

1. The cat made a *stealthy* move toward the bird.
2. It was a *stealthy* attempt to change the rules without a vote.
3. Cats are *stealthier* than any dog will ever be.

---

### steam-powered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstiːm ˌpaʊərd/ &middot; *STEEM-pow-urd*
**Русский:** паровой, работающий на пару
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle

Driven by the pressure of steam.

1. The museum has a working *steam-powered* locomotive.
2. Early factories were *steam-powered*.
3. He restores *steam-powered* tractors as a hobby.

---

### steamed

**Pronunciation:** /stiːmd/ &middot; *STEEMD*
**Русский:** приготовленный на пару; разозлённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *steam*

Steamed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cooked over boiling water rather than in it.** (приготовленный на пару, паровой)

1. We had rice and *steamed* broccoli.
2. The fish comes *steamed*, with ginger and scallions.

**2. Informal: angry and showing it.** (разозлённый, взбешённый)

3. He was pretty *steamed* about the delay.
4. Don't get *steamed* — it was an honest mistake.

---

### steaming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstiːmɪŋ/ &middot; *STEE-ming*
**Русский:** дымящийся, горячий (с паром)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *steam*

So hot that steam is rising from it.

1. She set a *steaming* bowl of soup in front of me.
2. The horses stood *steaming* in the cold air.
3. He carried in two *steaming* mugs of coffee.

---

### steamy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstiːmi/ &middot; *STEE-mee*
**Русский:** душный, влажный от пара; пикантный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *steamier*, *steamiest*
**Form:** -y from *steam*

Steamy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hot and wet with steam or humid air.** (душный, жаркий и влажный, запотевший)

1. The bathroom was *steamy* after his shower.
2. August in Houston is hot and *steamy*.
3. It was the *steamiest* week of the summer.

**2. Informal: full of sexual excitement.** (откровенный, эротический, пикантный)

4. The book has a few *steamy* scenes.
5. They had a *steamy* affair one summer.

---

### steely

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstiːli/ &middot; *STEE-lee*
**Русский:** стальной, твёрдый, решительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a steely stare*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more steely*, *most steely*
**Form:** -y from *steel*

Steely has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard, cold and completely determined.** (твёрдый, непреклонный, стальной (о характере))

1. She gave him a *steely* stare and said nothing.
2. Behind the smile there is *steely* determination.

**2. Of the cold gray-blue color of steel.** (стального цвета, серо-голубой)

3. He has *steely* gray eyes.
4. The lake was a flat *steely* blue that morning.

---

### steep

**Pronunciation:** /stiːp/ &middot; *STEEP*
**Русский:** крутой; резкий; непомерный (о цене)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *steeper*, *steepest*
**Opposite:** *gentle*

Steep has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Rising or falling sharply, at a big angle.** (крутой, обрывистый)

1. The path up to the lake is short but *steep*.
2. This is the *steepest* street in the city.
3. There was a *steep* drop on the other side of the wall.

**2. Of a rise or fall in numbers: large and fast.** (резкий, стремительный (о росте, падении))

4. The company reported a *steep* drop in sales.
5. There has been a *steep* rise in the cost of insurance.

**3. Informal, of a price: much higher than it should be.** (непомерный, заоблачный (о цене))

6. Forty dollars for parking seems a little *steep*.
7. The rent is *steep*, but the place is beautiful.

---

### stellar

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛlər/ &middot; *STEL-ur*
**Русский:** выдающийся, блестящий; звёздный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stellar*, *most stellar*

Stellar has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Excellent; far better than the usual standard.** (блестящий, выдающийся, великолепный)

1. She gave a *stellar* performance in the second act.
2. His grades were not exactly *stellar* that semester.

**2. Technical: having to do with stars.** (звёздный, относящийся к звёздам)

3. The telescope measures *stellar* distances.
4. A supernova is the end of a *stellar* life cycle.

---

### step-by-step

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstɛp baɪ ˈstɛp/ &middot; *step-by-STEP*
**Русский:** пошаговый, поэтапный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *step-by-step instructions*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — a whole phrase used as one adjective
**Contrast:** Before a noun it is hyphenated; after a verb it becomes the adverb phrase *step by step*: *a step-by-step guide*, but *we did it step by step*.

Explained or done one stage at a time, in order.

1. The manual has *step-by-step* instructions with pictures.
2. She wrote a *step-by-step* guide for new hires.
3. It is a *step-by-step* process, and you cannot skip ahead.

---

### stereo

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛrioʊ/ &middot; *STER-ee-oh*
**Русский:** стереофонический, стерео
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *stereo sound*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** short for *stereophonic*
**Opposite:** *mono*
**Contrast:** The thing in your living room is a noun: *I bought a new stereo*. Only the sound sense is an adjective: *a stereo recording*.

Of sound: coming from two separate channels, so it seems to spread out in space.

1. The band's first *stereo* recording came out in 1963.
2. Older television sets did not have *stereo* sound.
3. Headphones give you the full *stereo* effect.

---

### stereotyped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛriəˌtaɪpt/ &middot; *STER-ee-uh-typt*
**Русский:** шаблонный, стереотипный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stereotyped*, *most stereotyped*
**Form:** -ed from *stereotype*

Shown in a fixed, oversimplified way that ignores what people are really like.

1. The movie is full of *stereotyped* characters.
2. Textbooks used to give a very *stereotyped* picture of family life.
3. Her role was *stereotyped* and gave her nothing to do.

---

### stereotypical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstɛriəˈtɪpɪkəl/ &middot; *ster-ee-uh-TIP-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** типичный в худшем смысле, стереотипный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stereotypical*, *most stereotypical*
**Form:** -ical from *stereotype*

Matching a tired, oversimplified idea of what a group or thing is like.

1. He is the *stereotypical* absent-minded professor.
2. The ad plays on *stereotypical* ideas about teenagers.
3. Nothing about her job is *stereotypical* for an engineer.

---

### sterile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛrəl/ &middot; *STER-uhl*
**Русский:** стерильный; бесплодный; безжизненный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sterile*, *most sterile*
**Opposite:** *fertile*

Sterile has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Completely free of germs.** (стерильный, обеззараженный)

1. Cover the cut with a *sterile* bandage.
2. The needles come in *sterile* packaging.
3. Operating rooms have to be kept *sterile*.

**2. Unable to produce children, young or crops.** (бесплодный, неплодородный)

4. The treatment can leave a patient *sterile*.
5. Years of salt water left the soil *sterile*.

**3. Lacking warmth, life or new ideas.** (безжизненный, стерильный, бесплодный (о споре))

6. The lobby is clean but *sterile*, like a dentist's office.
7. The debate has become *sterile* — nobody learns anything.

---

### sterilized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɛrəˌlaɪzd/ &middot; *STER-uh-lyzd*
**Русский:** стерилизованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sterilize*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *sterilized*; British books print *sterilised*.

Sterilized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cleaned of all germs, usually with heat or chemicals.** (стерилизованный, обеззараженный)

1. Use *sterilized* jars for the jam.
2. Every instrument on the tray has been *sterilized*.

**2. Of an animal or person: operated on so that they cannot have young.** (стерилизованный (о животном))

3. All the cats at the shelter are *sterilized* before adoption.
4. The dog was *sterilized* at six months.

---

### sterling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɜːrlɪŋ/ &middot; *STUR-ling*
**Русский:** превосходный; высокопробный (о серебре)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sterling work*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Sterling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Excellent, and deserving real praise.** (превосходный, безупречный)

1. She did a *sterling* job of organizing the whole trip.
2. He has a reputation for *sterling* honesty.

**2. Of silver: of the standard high quality, 92.5 percent pure.** (высокопробный, стерлинговый (о серебре))

3. The spoons are *sterling* silver, not plate.
4. Look for a *sterling* mark on the back.

---

### stern

**Pronunciation:** /stɜːrn/ &middot; *STURN*
**Русский:** строгий, суровый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sterner*, *sternest*
**Takes:** *with* + person
**Opposite:** *gentle*

Serious and strict, and not ready to forgive.

1. Our coach was *stern* but fair.
2. She gave the boys a *stern* look over her glasses.
3. The judge had a *sterner* warning for the second driver.

---

### stewed

**Pronunciation:** /stuːd/ &middot; *STOOD*
**Русский:** тушёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *stew*

Cooked slowly in liquid in a covered pot.

1. We had *stewed* tomatoes with the chicken.
2. She serves *stewed* apples over oatmeal.
3. The beef is *stewed* for three hours until it falls apart.

---

### stick-on

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪk ˌɑːn/ &middot; *STIK-ahn*
**Русский:** самоклеящийся, приклеивающийся
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *stick-on labels*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + particle

Made with glue on the back so you can just press it into place.

1. I put *stick-on* labels on all the storage bins.
2. The kids covered the window with *stick-on* stars.
3. You can buy *stick-on* hooks that hold a few pounds.

---

### sticky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪki/ &middot; *STIK-ee*
**Русский:** липкий; душный; щекотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stickier*, *stickiest*
**Form:** -y from *stick*

Sticky has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with something that glues itself to whatever it touches.** (липкий, клейкий)

1. The kids' hands were *sticky* with candy.
2. Use a *sticky* note to mark the page.
3. The dough gets *stickier* if you add more water.

**2. Of weather: hot and damp in an uncomfortable way.** (душный, влажный и жаркий)

4. It was a *sticky* afternoon with no wind at all.
5. July here is hot and *sticky*.

**3. Informal: awkward and difficult to get out of.** (щекотливый, затруднительный)

6. The question about money got a little *sticky*.
7. He got himself into a *sticky* situation with his landlord.

---

### stiff

**Pronunciation:** /stɪf/ &middot; *STIF*
**Русский:** жёсткий; затёкший; чопорный; сильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stiffer*, *stiffest*
**Opposite:** *flexible*

Stiff has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Hard and difficult to bend or move.** (жёсткий, негнущийся, тугой)

1. The card is printed on *stiff* paper.
2. This drawer is *stiff* — you have to pull hard.
3. Beat the egg whites until they are *stiff*.

**2. Of a body part: sore and hard to move.** (затёкший, одеревеневший, ноющий)

4. I woke up with a *stiff* neck.
5. My legs were *stiffer* the second day than the first.

**3. Formal and unfriendly in the way someone behaves.** (чопорный, скованный, натянутый)

6. He gave a *stiff* little bow and left.
7. The conversation stayed polite but *stiff*.

**4. Stronger or more severe than usual.** (сильный, суровый (о ветре, наказании, конкуренции))

8. There is *stiff* competition for those jobs.
9. He poured himself a *stiff* drink.
10. A *stiff* breeze came off the water.

---

### stifled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstaɪfəld/ &middot; *STY-fuhld*
**Русский:** подавленный, сдавленный, приглушённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stifled laugh*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *stifle*

Held back so that it is barely heard or never comes out.

1. There was a *stifled* laugh from the back row.
2. She answered with a *stifled* yawn.
3. The report describes years of *stifled* dissent.

---

### stifling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstaɪflɪŋ/ &middot; *STY-fling*
**Русский:** удушающий, душный; подавляющий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stifling*, *most stifling*
**Form:** -ing from *stifle*

Stifling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So hot and airless that it is hard to breathe.** (душный, удушливый)

1. The heat in the attic was *stifling*.
2. We sat through a *stifling* August afternoon with no fan.

**2. So controlling that it leaves no room to think or act freely.** (подавляющий, угнетающий)

3. She left a *stifling* job where nobody could question anything.
4. He found small-town life *stifling*.

---

### still

**Pronunciation:** /stɪl/ &middot; *STIL*
**Русский:** неподвижный; тихий; негазированный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stiller*, *stillest*
**Opposite:** *moving*
**Contrast:** Most of the time *still* is an adverb (*She is still here*, *Are you still mad?*). The adjective means not moving: *Sit still*, *the still water*.

Still has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not moving at all.** (неподвижный, застывший)

1. Hold *still* while I take the picture.
2. The water was so *still* you could see every cloud in it.
3. The lake is *stiller* at dawn than at any other hour.

**2. Quiet and calm, with nothing stirring.** (тихий, безветренный, спокойный)

4. It was a warm, *still* evening in June.
5. The house went completely *still* after they left.

**3. Of a drink: without bubbles.** (негазированный)

6. Would you like sparkling or *still* water?
7. They only sell *still* water at that counter.

---

### stillborn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪlbɔːrn/ &middot; *STIL-born*
**Русский:** мертворождённый; провалившийся с самого начала
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *still* (motionless) + *born*

Stillborn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Born dead.** (мертворождённый)

1. Their first child was *stillborn*.
2. The clinic keeps records of *stillborn* births.

**2. Of a plan or idea: failing before it ever gets going.** (мертворождённый, провалившийся в самом начале)

3. The peace plan was *stillborn* — nobody would sign it.
4. His campaign was *stillborn* by the end of January.

---

### stilted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪltɪd/ &middot; *STIL-tid*
**Русский:** натянутый, искусственный, неестественный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stilted*, *most stilted*
**Opposite:** *natural*

Of speech or writing: stiff and unnatural, as if nobody really talks that way.

1. The dialogue in the film is painfully *stilted*.
2. We had a *stilted* conversation about the weather.
3. His English is correct but *stilted*.

---

### stimulating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪmjəˌleɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *STIM-yuh-lay-ting*
**Русский:** интересный, будоражащий; стимулирующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stimulating*, *most stimulating*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the effect
**Opposite:** *dull*

Stimulating has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making you interested and full of new ideas.** (интересный, будоражащий мысль)

1. It was the most *stimulating* class I took all year.
2. She finds the work *stimulating* even after ten years.

**2. Making the body or mind more active.** (стимулирующий, возбуждающий)

3. Coffee has a *stimulating* effect that lasts a few hours.
4. Cold water on your face is surprisingly *stimulating*.

---

### stinging

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪŋɪŋ/ &middot; *STING-ing*
**Русский:** жгучий, острый (о боли); язвительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stinging pain*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *sting*

Stinging has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Causing a sharp burning pain on the skin.** (жгучий, колющий)

1. The soap left a *stinging* sensation in my eyes.
2. A *stinging* wind blew snow into our faces.

**2. Of words: sharp and meant to hurt.** (язвительный, резкий, обидный)

3. The review was a *stinging* attack on the whole company.
4. She had a *stinging* reply ready for him.

---

### stingy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪndʒi/ &middot; *STIN-jee*
**Русский:** скупой, жадный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stingier*, *stingiest*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Opposite:** *generous*

Unwilling to spend or give, even when there is plenty.

1. He is famously *stingy* with tips.
2. Don't be *stingy* — put more cheese on it.
3. The new boss is *stingier* about overtime than the last one.

---

### stinking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪŋkɪŋ/ &middot; *STING-king*
**Русский:** вонючий; проклятый (усилительно)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *stink*

Stinking has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Smelling very bad.** (вонючий, зловонный)

1. A *stinking* pile of garbage sat by the curb.
2. The whole basement was damp and *stinking*.

**2. Informal, before a noun: used to show anger or disgust at something.** (проклятый, чёртов (для усиления))

3. I don't want your *stinking* money.
4. He wouldn't move his *stinking* car out of the driveway.

---

### stinky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɪŋki/ &middot; *STING-kee*
**Русский:** вонючий, дурно пахнущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stinkier*, *stinkiest*
**Form:** -y from *stink*

Informal: smelling bad — the everyday word, common with kids.

1. Take off those *stinky* socks before you come in.
2. The dog rolled in something *stinky* again.
3. This is the *stinkiest* cheese in the store.

---

### stirring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɜːrɪŋ/ &middot; *STUR-ing*
**Русский:** волнующий, вдохновляющий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stirring*, *most stirring*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

Making people feel strong emotion, usually pride or excitement.

1. She gave a *stirring* speech at the memorial.
2. The film ends with a *stirring* piece of music.
3. His account of the rescue was *stirring*.

---

### stock

**Pronunciation:** /stɑːk/ &middot; *STAHK*
**Русский:** стандартный, шаблонный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stock answer*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Stock* is much more often a noun (*shares of stock*, *chicken stock*). As an adjective it only goes before a noun and means standard or routine.

Stock has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Used so often that it is no longer fresh; ready-made.** (шаблонный, дежурный, заезженный)

1. He gave the *stock* answer about needing more data.
2. The speech was full of *stock* phrases.

**2. Of a standard kind that a store or factory keeps ready.** (стандартный, серийный, со склада)

3. The shelves come in four *stock* sizes.
4. It is a *stock* model with no extras.

---

### stock-still

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstɑːkˈstɪl/ &middot; *stahk-STIL*
**Русский:** неподвижно, как вкопанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb like *stand*, *sit* or *be*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *stock* (a tree trunk) + *still*

Completely motionless.

1. The deer stood *stock-still* in the middle of the road.
2. He sat *stock-still* until the noise stopped.
3. She was *stock-still*, listening for the car.

---

### stocked

**Pronunciation:** /stɑːkt/ &middot; *STAHKT*
**Русский:** заполненный товаром, укомплектованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually with an adverb: *well stocked*, *fully stocked*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *stock*

Filled with a supply of goods or food.

1. The pantry is fully *stocked* for the week.
2. It is a small store, but it is well *stocked*.
3. The pond is *stocked* with trout every spring.

---

### stocky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːki/ &middot; *STAH-kee*
**Русский:** коренастый, плотный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stockier*, *stockiest*
**Opposite:** *lanky*

Short, solid and strong-looking.

1. A *stocky* man in a gray coat opened the door.
2. He is *stocky* like his father.
3. The *stockier* players get pushed into the line.

---

### stodgy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːdʒi/ &middot; *STAH-jee*
**Русский:** скучный, старомодный, чопорный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stodgier*, *stodgiest*
**Opposite:** *lively*
**Contrast:** In American English *stodgy* is about people and institutions. British writers also use it for heavy food: *a stodgy pudding*.

Dull, old-fashioned and much too serious.

1. The bank has a *stodgy* image it is trying to shake.
2. He is less *stodgy* than he looks in that suit.
3. The magazine got *stodgier* after the new editor arrived.

---

### stoic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstoʊɪk/ &middot; *STOH-ik*
**Русский:** стойкий, невозмутимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stoic*, *most stoic*
**Form:** from the Stoic philosophers of ancient Greece

Accepting pain or trouble calmly, without complaining.

1. She was *stoic* about the whole diagnosis.
2. He kept a *stoic* silence through the entire meeting.
3. The crew was *more stoic* about the delay than the passengers.

---

### stolid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɑːlɪd/ &middot; *STAH-lid*
**Русский:** флегматичный, невозмутимый, бесстрастный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stolid*, *most stolid*
**Opposite:** *excitable*

Showing almost no emotion and hard to excite or upset.

1. The guard stood there with a *stolid* expression.
2. He is a *stolid* man who says maybe twenty words a day.
3. She stayed *stolid* while everyone around her panicked.

---

### stone-cold

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstoʊnˈkoʊld/ &middot; *stohn-KOHLD*
**Русский:** холодный как лёд; абсолютно, совершенно
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *stone* used to mean 'completely'

Stone-cold has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Completely cold, with no warmth left at all.** (совершенно холодный, остывший)

1. My coffee was *stone-cold* by the time I sat down.
2. The radiators were *stone-cold* all weekend.

**2. Informal, before a noun or adjective: completely, with no doubt about it.** (абсолютно, стопроцентно)

3. He was *stone-cold* sober the whole night.
4. That album is a *stone-cold* classic.

---

### stoned

**Pronunciation:** /stoʊnd/ &middot; *STOHND*
**Русский:** под кайфом, обкуренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable

Informal: high from marijuana.

1. He was too *stoned* to follow the plot.
2. Half the audience was *stoned*.
3. They got *stoned* and ordered three pizzas.

---

### stony

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstoʊni/ &middot; *STOH-nee*
**Русский:** каменистый; каменный, холодный (о взгляде)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stonier*, *stoniest*
**Form:** -y from *stone*

Stony has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with or full of stones.** (каменистый)

1. Nothing grows in that *stony* soil.
2. The path down to the beach is steep and *stony*.
3. The ground gets *stonier* as you climb.

**2. Showing no feeling and no friendliness at all.** (каменный, ледяной, безучастный)

4. My question was met with *stony* silence.
5. She gave him a *stony* look and turned away.

---

### stooped

**Pronunciation:** /stuːpt/ &middot; *STOOPT*
**Русский:** сутулый, сгорбленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *stoop*
**Opposite:** *upright*

With the back and shoulders bent permanently forward.

1. A *stooped* old man shuffled to the counter.
2. Years at a desk left him *stooped*.
3. She recognized his *stooped* shoulders from across the street.

---

### store-bought

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɔːr ˌbɔːt/ &middot; *STOR-bawt*
**Русский:** покупной, магазинный
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *store-bought cookies*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle
**Opposite:** *homemade*
**Contrast:** *Store-bought* is the American word; British English says *shop-bought*.

Bought ready-made in a store instead of made at home.

1. She brought *store-bought* cookies and apologized for it.
2. Use fresh basil, not the *store-bought* sauce.
3. A *store-bought* crust saves you an hour.

---

### storied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɔːrid/ &middot; *STOR-eed*
**Русский:** прославленный, легендарный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a storied career*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *story* — full of stories worth telling

Famous for a long history that people tell stories about.

1. He ended a *storied* career with one last title.
2. The Celtics are one of the most *storied* franchises in basketball.
3. They play in a *storied* old ballpark downtown.

---

### stormy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɔːrmi/ &middot; *STOR-mee*
**Русский:** бурный, штормовой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stormier*, *stormiest*
**Form:** -y from *storm*
**Opposite:** *calm*

Stormy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With strong wind and rain.** (штормовой, ненастный, бурный)

1. It was a *stormy* night and the power went out.
2. The sea looked *stormy* even from the hotel.
3. February was the *stormiest* month in years.

**2. Full of angry argument and strong feeling.** (бурный, полный ссор)

4. They had a *stormy* marriage that lasted nine years.
5. The council meeting turned *stormy* after the vote.

---

### stout

**Pronunciation:** /staʊt/ &middot; *STOWT*
**Русский:** полный, плотный; крепкий; стойкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stouter*, *stoutest*

Stout has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of a person: rather fat and solid — a gentler word than *fat*.** (полный, дородный, плотный)

1. A *stout* woman in a straw hat was selling tickets.
2. He has gotten *stouter* since he retired.

**2. Thick and strong, and not likely to break.** (крепкий, прочный, толстый)

3. Wear *stout* boots — the trail is rough.
4. The door is held by a *stout* oak beam.

**3. Brave and determined, in the face of something hard.** (стойкий, решительный, упорный)

5. The town put up a *stout* defense of its school.
6. He remains a *stout* supporter of the program.

---

### straight

**Pronunciation:** /streɪt/ &middot; *STRAYT*
**Русский:** прямой; ровный; честный; подряд; неразбавленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *straighter*, *straightest*
**Opposite:** *crooked*
**Contrast:** *Straight* is also a very common adverb: *Go straight ahead*, *Come straight home*. And do not confuse it with *strait* (a narrow channel of water).

Straight has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Going one way without bending or curving.** (прямой, не изогнутый)

1. Draw a *straight* line from here to the corner.
2. She has long *straight* hair.
3. This road is *straighter* than the old one through the hills.

**2. Level, even, or hanging in the right position.** (ровный, прямо висящий)

4. Is the picture *straight*?
5. Your tie isn't *straight*.

**3. Honest and direct, with nothing hidden.** (прямой, откровенный, честный)

6. Just give me a *straight* answer.
7. I'll be *straight* with you: we can't afford it.

**4. One after another, with no break — always before a noun.** (подряд, кряду)

8. They have won five *straight* games.
9. It rained for ten *straight* days.

**5. Of a strong drink: with nothing added.** (неразбавленный, чистый (о напитке))

10. He drinks his whiskey *straight*.
11. I'll take a *straight* bourbon, no ice.

---

### straightforward

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/ &middot; *strayt-FOR-wurd*
**Русский:** простой, понятный; прямой, откровенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more straightforward*, *most straightforward*
**Form:** compound — *straight* + *forward*
**Opposite:** *complicated*

Straightforward has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Easy to do or understand, with no hidden complications.** (простой, несложный, понятный)

1. The application process is pretty *straightforward*.
2. It should be a *straightforward* repair.
3. Her explanation was *more straightforward* than anything in the manual.

**2. Of a person: honest and saying plainly what they think.** (прямой, откровенный, честный)

4. He is *straightforward* about what he wants.
5. I like her *straightforward* way of dealing with people.

---

### strained

**Pronunciation:** /streɪnd/ &middot; *STRAYND*
**Русский:** натянутый, напряжённый; растянутый (о мышце)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strained*, *most strained*
**Form:** -ed from *strain*

Strained has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of a relationship or atmosphere: tense and close to breaking.** (натянутый, напряжённый)

1. Relations between the two countries are *strained*.
2. Dinner was polite but *strained*.

**2. Of a smile, voice or look: forced, not natural.** (натянутый, вымученный)

3. She answered with a *strained* smile.
4. His voice sounded *strained* on the phone.

**3. Of a muscle: hurt by being stretched too far.** (растянутый, потянутый)

5. He is out for two weeks with a *strained* hamstring.
6. Ice a *strained* muscle for the first day.

---

### straitlaced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstreɪtˈleɪst/ &middot; *strayt-LAYST*
**Русский:** чопорный, пуританский, строгих правил
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more straitlaced*, *most straitlaced*
**Form:** compound — *strait* (tight) + *laced*, from tightly laced corsets
**Opposite:** *permissive*
**Contrast:** The first part is *strait* (narrow, tight), not *straight*. Also written *strait-laced*.

Having very strict, old-fashioned ideas about how people should behave.

1. Her parents were *straitlaced* and hated the whole idea.
2. It is a *straitlaced* little town with no bars.
3. He is less *straitlaced* than he pretends to be.

---

### stranded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrændɪd/ &middot; *STRAN-did*
**Русский:** застрявший, брошенный без помощи
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *strand* (to leave on a shore)

Left in a place with no way to get away from it.

1. The storm left hundreds of travelers *stranded* at the airport.
2. We were *stranded* on the side of the highway for two hours.
3. A *stranded* whale was pushed back out with the tide.

---

### strapless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstræpləs/ &middot; *STRAP-luhs*
**Русский:** без бретелек
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *strap*

Of a dress or top: made with no straps over the shoulders.

1. She wore a *strapless* black dress.
2. The gown is *strapless*, so bring a wrap.
3. *Strapless* tops are hard to keep in place.

---

### strapping

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstræpɪŋ/ &middot; *STRAP-ing*
**Русский:** рослый и крепкий, здоровенный
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a strapping young man*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of a young person: tall, strong and healthy-looking.

1. Her son is a *strapping* eighteen-year-old now.
2. Two *strapping* farmhands carried the beam by themselves.
3. He grew into a *strapping* kid who could lift anything.

---

### strategic

**Pronunciation:** /strəˈtiːdʒɪk/ &middot; *struh-TEE-jik*
**Русский:** стратегический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strategic*, *most strategic*
**Form:** -ic from *strategy*

Strategic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done as part of a long-term plan to reach an important goal.** (стратегический, продуманный на перспективу)

1. The hire was a *strategic* move, not a favor.
2. We need to be *more strategic* about where we spend the money.

**2. Military: important for winning a war or holding a position.** (стратегический (военный))

3. The bridge has *strategic* value for both sides.
4. The treaty limits *strategic* weapons.

---

### stratified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrætəˌfaɪd/ &middot; *STRAT-uh-fyd*
**Русский:** слоистый; расслоённый (об обществе)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stratified*, *most stratified*
**Form:** -ed from *stratify*, from *stratum* (a layer)

Stratified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Arranged in clear layers, one on top of another.** (слоистый, расположенный слоями)

1. The cliff shows *stratified* bands of red rock.
2. The lake is *stratified* in summer, with warm water on top.

**2. Of a society: divided into sharply separated classes.** (расслоённый, разделённый на классы)

3. It was a rigidly *stratified* society.
4. The city is *more stratified* by income than it was in 1980.

---

### stray

**Pronunciation:** /streɪ/ &middot; *STRAY*
**Русский:** бездомный (о животном); случайный, отбившийся
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stray dog*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A stray* is also a noun for a lost animal: *She took in a stray*.

Stray has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an animal: lost, or living on the street with no owner.** (бездомный, бродячий, потерявшийся)

1. A *stray* cat has been sleeping on our porch.
2. The shelter is full of *stray* dogs.

**2. Separated from the rest, or there only by accident.** (случайный, шальной, отбившийся)

3. A *stray* bullet went through the window.
4. She tucked a *stray* hair behind her ear.

---

### streaming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstriːmɪŋ/ &middot; *STREE-ming*
**Русский:** потоковый (о видео); струящийся, текущий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun in the internet sense
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *stream*

Streaming has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sent over the internet in a continuous flow, so you watch or listen as it arrives.** (потоковый, стриминговый)

1. We pay for three *streaming* services and watch one.
2. The concert is available as a *streaming* video.
3. Most of their money now comes from *streaming* revenue.

**2. Flowing or pouring steadily.** (струящийся, текущий, заливаемый)

4. She came in with her eyes *streaming*.
5. The windows were *streaming* with rain.

---

### streamlined

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstriːmlaɪnd/ &middot; *STREEM-lynd*
**Русский:** обтекаемый; упрощённый, оптимизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more streamlined*, *most streamlined*
**Form:** -ed from *streamline*

Streamlined has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shaped smoothly so that air or water flows past it easily.** (обтекаемый)

1. The new train has a *streamlined* nose.
2. Dolphins have a perfectly *streamlined* body.

**2. Made simpler and faster by cutting out unnecessary steps.** (упрощённый, оптимизированный)

3. The application is *streamlined* — it takes ten minutes now.
4. They want a *more streamlined* approval process.

---

### street smart

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstriːt ˈsmɑːrt/ &middot; *street SMAHRT*
**Русский:** знающий жизнь, бывалый, житейски смекалистый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more street smart*, *most street smart*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Contrast:** Before a noun it usually takes a hyphen: *a street-smart kid*, but *she is street smart*.

Good at handling the dangers and tricks of life in a city.

1. He is *street smart* in a way you cannot learn in school.
2. She was sixteen and already *street smart*.
3. You need someone *more street smart* than an intern for that job.

---

### streetwise

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstriːtwaɪz/ &middot; *STREET-wyz*
**Русский:** бывалый, знающий уличную жизнь
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more streetwise*, *most streetwise*
**Form:** compound — *street* + *-wise* (knowing about)

Experienced enough to look after yourself in a rough part of a city.

1. The kids on that block are *streetwise* by ten.
2. He plays a *streetwise* detective who breaks every rule.
3. She is *streetwise* enough not to walk there alone.

---

### strenuous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɛnjuəs/ &middot; *STREN-yoo-uhs*
**Русский:** напряжённый, тяжёлый, энергичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strenuous*, *most strenuous*

Strenuous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Needing a lot of physical effort and energy.** (тяжёлый, требующий больших усилий)

1. Avoid *strenuous* exercise for a week after the surgery.
2. The hike is long but not *strenuous*.

**2. Of effort or opposition: energetic and determined.** (энергичный, настойчивый (о возражениях, усилиях))

3. The bill passed over the *strenuous* objections of the mayor.
4. They made *strenuous* efforts to find the owner.

---

### stressed

**Pronunciation:** /strɛst/ &middot; *STREST*
**Русский:** в стрессе, издёрганный; ударный (о слоге)
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stressed*, *most stressed*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; also *stressed out*
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Contrast:** The person is *stressed*; the thing that does it is *stressful*: *a stressful week leaves you stressed*.

Stressed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worried and tense because of too much pressure.** (в стрессе, напряжённый, издёрганный)

1. She has been *stressed* about the move for weeks.
2. Everyone is *stressed* out by the deadline.
3. He looked *more stressed* than I had ever seen him.

**2. Of a syllable: said with more force than the ones around it.** (ударный (о слоге))

4. In *record* the noun, the first syllable is *stressed*.
5. Mark the *stressed* syllable in each word.

---

### stressful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɛsfəl/ &middot; *STRES-fuhl*
**Русский:** напряжённый, стрессовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stressful*, *most stressful*
**Form:** -ful from *stress*
**Opposite:** *relaxing*
**Contrast:** *stressful* describes the situation, *stressed* the person: *a stressful job*, *a stressed employee*.

Causing worry and pressure.

1. Moving is one of the most *stressful* things you can do.
2. It was a *stressful* week at work.
3. Flying with a toddler is *more stressful* than any meeting.

---

### stretched

**Pronunciation:** /strɛtʃt/ &middot; *STRECHT*
**Русский:** растянутый; работающий на пределе
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stretched*, *most stretched*
**Form:** -ed from *stretch*

Stretched has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pulled out so it is longer, wider or tighter than before.** (растянутый, натянутый)

1. The canvas is *stretched* over a wooden frame.
2. The collar is *stretched* out of shape.

**2. Of money, staff or time: barely enough for what has to be done.** (на пределе, в обрез)

3. The department is *stretched* thin this month.
4. Our budget is *more stretched* than it was last year.

---

### stretchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɛtʃi/ &middot; *STRECH-ee*
**Русский:** тянущийся, эластичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stretchier*, *stretchiest*
**Form:** -y from *stretch*

Able to stretch easily and go back to its shape.

1. These pants are *stretchy* and comfortable.
2. Look for a *stretchy* fabric with some spandex in it.
3. The gray ones are *stretchier* than the black.

---

### stricken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɪkən/ &middot; *STRIK-uhn*
**Русский:** поражённый, сражённый (болезнью, горем)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often right after the noun: *a town stricken by drought*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* or *by* + noun
**Form:** an old past participle of *strike*; also the second half of compounds — *grief-stricken*, *panic-stricken*

Badly hit by illness, grief or some other trouble.

1. He was *stricken* with pneumonia in his last year.
2. Aid reached the *stricken* villages after three days.
3. She sat there *stricken*, unable to say a word.

---

### strict

**Pronunciation:** /strɪkt/ &middot; *STRIKT*
**Русский:** строгий; точный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stricter*, *strictest*
**Takes:** *with* + person; *about* + noun
**Opposite:** *lenient*

Strict has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Demanding that rules be obeyed, and not forgiving when they are not.** (строгий, требовательный)

1. My parents were *strict* about bedtime.
2. She is *strict* with her students but they love her.
3. The new principal is much *stricter* than the old one.

**2. Of a rule, order or meaning: exact, and to be followed exactly.** (строгий, точный, неукоснительный)

4. He gave us *strict* instructions not to open it.
5. In the *strict* sense of the word, it isn't a fruit.

---

### strident

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstraɪdənt/ &middot; *STRY-duhnt*
