**Русский:** резкий, пронзительный; агрессивный (о тоне)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strident*, *most strident*

Strident has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Loud and harsh, in a way that hurts the ear.** (резкий, пронзительный, скрипучий)

1. A *strident* alarm went off down the hall.
2. Her voice got *strident* when she was tired.

**2. Expressing an opinion so forcefully that it puts people off.** (резкий, напористый, агрессивный)

3. The paper has taken a *more strident* tone since the election.
4. He is one of the *most strident* critics of the plan.

---

### striking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstraɪkɪŋ/ &middot; *STRY-king*
**Русский:** поразительный, заметный; эффектный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more striking*, *most striking*
**Form:** -ing from *strike*

Striking has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So unusual or clear that you notice it right away.** (поразительный, бросающийся в глаза)

1. There is a *striking* difference between the two drafts.
2. The *most striking* thing about the house is the light.
3. The similarity is *striking*.

**2. Attractive in a bold, unusual way.** (эффектный, яркий (о внешности))

4. She is a *striking* woman with silver hair.
5. The building is *striking* from every angle.

---

### stringent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɪndʒənt/ &middot; *STRIN-juhnt*
**Русский:** жёсткий, строгий (о требованиях)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stringent*, *most stringent*
**Opposite:** *lax*

Of rules or standards: strict, detailed and hard to meet.

1. California has *stringent* emissions rules.
2. The lab follows *stringent* safety procedures.
3. Banks face *more stringent* requirements than they did in 2007.

---

### stringy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɪŋi/ &middot; *STRING-ee*
**Русский:** жилистый, волокнистый; сальными прядями (о волосах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stringier*, *stringiest*
**Form:** -y from *string*

Stringy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of food: full of tough fibers that are hard to chew.** (жилистый, волокнистый)

1. The beef was dry and *stringy*.
2. Old celery gets *stringier* the longer you keep it.

**2. Of hair: thin, greasy and hanging in separate pieces.** (висящий сальными прядями)

3. His hair was long and *stringy*.
4. She pushed her wet, *stringy* bangs off her forehead.

---

### striped

**Pronunciation:** /straɪpt/ &middot; *STRYPT*
**Русский:** полосатый, в полоску
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *stripe*
**Contrast:** One syllable, rhyming with *wiped* — not *STRY-pid*.

Having stripes.

1. He wore a blue *striped* shirt.
2. The awning is *striped* green and white.
3. She picked the *striped* wallpaper for the hallway.

---

### stripped-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstrɪptˈdaʊn/ &middot; *stript-DOWN*
**Русский:** урезанный, упрощённый, базовый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a stripped-down version*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — participle + particle

With everything extra taken away, leaving only the basics.

1. The free plan is a *stripped-down* version of the app.
2. They played a *stripped-down* set with two guitars.
3. He bought the *stripped-down* model with no navigation.

---

### strong

**Pronunciation:** /strɔːŋ/ &middot; *STRAWNG*
**Русский:** сильный; прочный; крепкий (о вкусе, напитке); твёрдый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stronger*, *strongest*
**Opposite:** *weak*

Strong has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Having a lot of physical power.** (сильный, крепкий физически)

1. You have to be *strong* to carry that up the stairs.
2. She is the *strongest* swimmer on the team.
3. He is *stronger* now than he was before the injury.

**2. Well made and not easily broken or damaged.** (прочный, крепкий)

4. Use a *strong* box for the books.
5. The bridge is *strong* enough for trucks.

**3. Of a taste, smell or drink: intense, with a lot of flavor or alcohol.** (крепкий, резкий, насыщенный)

6. I like my coffee *strong*.
7. There was a *strong* smell of gas in the kitchen.

**4. Firmly felt or hard to argue with.** (твёрдый, убедительный (о мнении, доводах))

8. She has *strong* opinions about the schools.
9. There is *strong* evidence that the drug works.

---

### strong-arm

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrɔːŋ ˌɑːrm/ &middot; *STRAWNG-ahrm*
**Русский:** силовой, основанный на нажиме
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *strong-arm tactics*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Contrast:** It is also a verb: *they strong-armed him into signing*.

Using force or heavy pressure to get what you want.

1. The union accused management of *strong-arm* tactics.
2. He built the company with *strong-arm* methods nobody talks about.
3. They used *strong-arm* pressure on the smaller suppliers.

---

### strong-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstrɔːŋˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *strawng-MYN-did*
**Русский:** решительный, с твёрдыми убеждениями
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strong-minded*, *most strong-minded*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Having firm opinions and not easily talked out of them.

1. She is *strong-minded* and says exactly what she thinks.
2. It takes a *strong-minded* judge to rule against her own party.
3. The committee needs someone *more strong-minded* than him.

---

### strong-willed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstrɔːŋˈwɪld/ &middot; *strawng-WILD*
**Русский:** волевой, упрямый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strong-willed*, *most strong-willed*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *weak-willed*

Determined to do what you want, even when people push back.

1. Their youngest is *strong-willed* and will not be rushed.
2. You need to be *strong-willed* to finish a program like that.
3. He is the *most strong-willed* person I know.

---

### structural

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrʌktʃərəl/ &middot; *STRUHK-chuh-ruhl*
**Русский:** структурный; несущий, конструктивный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *structure*

Structural has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with the frame that holds a building up.** (конструктивный, несущий (о стене, повреждении))

1. The inspector found no *structural* damage.
2. That wall is *structural*, so you cannot just take it out.

**2. Built into the way a system is organized, not caused by one event.** (структурный, системный)

3. The deficit is *structural*, not a one-year problem.
4. They are looking at *structural* changes to the whole department.

---

### structured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrʌktʃərd/ &middot; *STRUHK-churd*
**Русский:** структурированный, упорядоченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more structured*, *most structured*
**Form:** -ed from *structure*
**Opposite:** *unstructured*

Organized into a clear plan or order.

1. The class is very *structured* — everything is planned to the minute.
2. *Structured* interviews ask every candidate the same questions.
3. He does better with a *more structured* schedule.

---

### struggling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstrʌɡlɪŋ/ &middot; *STRUHG-ling*
**Русский:** испытывающий трудности; бедствующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun (*a struggling artist*); after a verb it is the progressive verb, not the adjective
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *struggle*

Having a hard time succeeding or making enough money.

1. He was a *struggling* actor for ten years before he got a part.
2. The program helps *struggling* readers in third grade.
3. *Struggling* farmers got some relief from the new law.

---

### strung-out

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstrʌŋ ˈaʊt/ &middot; *struhng-OWT*
**Русский:** под кайфом, в ломке; измотанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — after a verb as two words (*he was strung out*), hyphenated before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more strung out*, *most strung out*
**Takes:** *on* + a drug
**Form:** compound — participle *strung* + *out*
**Contrast:** Informal. The two meanings blur, but the drug one is the stronger of the two — be careful about using it lightly.

Strung-out has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Badly affected by a drug you cannot stop taking.** (сидящий на наркотиках, в ломке)

1. He was *strung out* on painkillers for most of that year.
2. She looked *strung out* — pale, thin, not sleeping.

**2. Worn out and on edge from stress or too little sleep.** (издёрганный, измотанный)

3. By finals week everyone is *strung out*.
4. I'm too *strung out* to make any big decisions right now.
5. He's even *more strung out* than he was last month.

---

### stubborn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌbərn/ &middot; *STUH-burn*
**Русский:** упрямый; трудноустранимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stubborn*, *most stubborn*
**Opposite:** *flexible*

Stubborn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Refusing to change your mind, even when you should.** (упрямый, несговорчивый)

1. My grandfather is the *most stubborn* man I know.
2. Don't be so *stubborn* — just let me help you.
3. She's *stubborn* about doing everything herself.

**2. Hard to get rid of or deal with, of a thing.** (стойкий, трудновыводимый)

4. There's a *stubborn* stain on the carpet.
5. She has a *stubborn* cough that won't go away.

---

### stubby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌbi/ &middot; *STUH-bee*
**Русский:** короткий и толстый, приземистый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stubbier*, *stubbiest*
**Form:** -y from *stub*

Short and thick.

1. He has short, *stubby* fingers.
2. The dog has a *stubby* little tail.
3. This pencil is even *stubbier* than the last one.

---

### stuck

**Pronunciation:** /stʌk/ &middot; *STUHK*
**Русский:** застрявший; в тупике
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually after a verb: *the window is stuck*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + a place (*stuck in traffic*); *on* + a problem; *with* + something you can't get rid of
**Preposition patterns:** [stuck in](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#stuck-with-stuck-in-stuck-on) &middot; [stuck on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#stuck-with-stuck-in-stuck-on) &middot; [stuck with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#stuck-with-stuck-in-stuck-on)
**Form:** -ed form of *stick*

Stuck has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Held fast and unable to move.** (застрявший, заклинивший)

1. The window is *stuck* — I can't get it open.
2. We were *stuck* in traffic for an hour.

**2. Unable to go on because you don't know what to do next.** (застопорившийся, зашедший в тупик)

3. I'm *stuck* on question four.
4. If you get *stuck*, ask the TA.

**3. Forced to keep something or someone you don't want.** (вынужденный терпеть, которому навязали)

5. I got *stuck* with the whole bill.
6. We're *stuck* with this couch until we can afford a new one.

---

### stuck-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌstʌk ˈʌp/ &middot; *stuhk-UHP*
**Русский:** высокомерный, зазнавшийся
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stuck-up*, *most stuck-up*
**Form:** compound — participle *stuck* + *up*
**Contrast:** Informal, and always about a person who acts superior. Nothing to do with *stuck* meaning 'unable to move'.

Acting as if you are better than other people.

1. The other kids thought she was *stuck-up*.
2. He's not *stuck-up*, he's just shy.
3. Nobody is *more stuck-up* than that whole crowd.

---

### studded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌdɪd/ &middot; *STUH-did*
**Русский:** усыпанный, усеянный; с заклёпками
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *stud*

Studded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having small metal pieces or jewels set into the surface.** (с заклёпками, инкрустированный)

1. He wore a *studded* leather jacket.
2. She had on a *studded* belt and heavy boots.

**2. Dotted all over with something.** (усыпанный, усеянный)

3. The night sky was *studded* with stars.
4. The cast is *studded* with names you'll recognize.

---

### studied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌdid/ &middot; *STUH-deed*
**Русский:** нарочитый, обдуманный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a studied calm*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *study*
**Contrast:** As an adjective it does not mean 'that has been studied'. It means done on purpose while trying to look natural: *studied indifference*.

Done on purpose and carefully worked out, while meant to look effortless.

1. He answered with *studied* calm.
2. Her *studied* indifference fooled nobody.
3. There was a *studied* casualness about the whole performance.

---

### studious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstuːdiəs/ &middot; *STOO-dee-uhs*
**Русский:** прилежный, усердный в учёбе
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more studious*, *most studious*
**Opposite:** *lazy*

Spending a lot of time studying and enjoying it.

1. She was a quiet, *studious* kid.
2. He's *more studious* than his brother ever was.
3. The *studious* ones sat in the front row.

---

### stuffed

**Pronunciation:** /stʌft/ &middot; *STUHFT*
**Русский:** набитый; фаршированный; объевшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *stuff*
**Contrast:** *Stuffed up* is a different thing: it means your nose is blocked by a cold.

Stuffed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Filled with something, especially soft filling or food.** (набитый; фаршированный)

1. The kids sleep with their *stuffed* animals.
2. We had *stuffed* peppers for dinner.

**2. So full of food that you can't eat any more.** (объевшийся, наевшийся до отвала)

3. No more for me — I'm *stuffed*.
4. Everyone was *stuffed* by the time the pie came out.

---

### stuffy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌfi/ &middot; *STUH-fee*
**Русский:** душный; чопорный; заложенный (о носе)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *stuffier*, *stuffiest*

Stuffy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Warm and airless, with no fresh air.** (душный, спёртый)

1. It's *stuffy* in here — can we open a window?
2. The bedroom gets *stuffier* as the night goes on.

**2. Formal, old-fashioned and no fun.** (чопорный, замшелый)

3. It's a *stuffy* old club where you still need a tie.
4. His parents are nice, just a little *stuffy*.

**3. Blocked, of your nose.** (заложенный (о носе))

5. I have a *stuffy* nose and a sore throat.
6. My nose gets *stuffy* every spring.

---

### stunned

**Pronunciation:** /stʌnd/ &middot; *STUHND*
**Русский:** ошеломлённый, потрясённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb; before a noun in *a stunned silence*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stunned*, *most stunned*
**Takes:** *by* + noun; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ed from *stun*
**Contrast:** *Stunned* is how the person feels; *stunning* is what causes it. *I was stunned*, but *the news was stunning*.

So shocked that you can't react.

1. We were *stunned* by the news.
2. There was a *stunned* silence in the room.
3. I was *stunned* that nobody said anything.

---

### stunning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌnɪŋ/ &middot; *STUH-ning*
**Русский:** потрясающий, сногсшибательный; ошеломляющий
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stunning*, *most stunning*
**Form:** -ing from *stun*
**Contrast:** The pair to watch: *stunning* describes the thing (*a stunning view*), *stunned* describes the person (*I was stunned*).

Stunning has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely beautiful or impressive.** (потрясающий, великолепный)

1. She looked *stunning* in that dress.
2. The view from the top is *stunning*.
3. It's the *most stunning* building in the city.

**2. So surprising that it shocks people.** (ошеломляющий, сенсационный)

4. The team pulled off a *stunning* upset.
5. It was a *stunning* reversal for the company.

---

### stunted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstʌntɪd/ &middot; *STUHN-tid*
**Русский:** чахлый, недоразвитый, задержавшийся в росте
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stunted*, *most stunted*
**Form:** -ed from *stunt*, to hold growth back
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with the noun *stunt* in *a movie stunt*.

Kept from growing or developing fully.

1. The trees along the ridge are *stunted* by the wind.
2. Poor nutrition leads to *stunted* growth.
3. His emotional development was *stunted* by those years.

---

### stupefied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstuːpɪfaɪd/ &middot; *STOO-pi-fyd*
**Русский:** ошеломлённый, оцепеневший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stupefied*, *most stupefied*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *stupefy*

So shocked, tired or bored that you can barely think.

1. We sat there *stupefied* by the heat.
2. He stared at the screen with a *stupefied* look.
3. The audience was simply *stupefied*.

---

### stupendous

**Pronunciation:** /stuːˈpɛndəs/ &middot; *stoo-PEN-duhs*
**Русский:** колоссальный, изумительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stupendous*, *most stupendous*

Amazingly big or amazingly good.

1. They did a *stupendous* amount of work in one week.
2. The final number was *stupendous* — the whole theater stood up.
3. That's the *most stupendous* thing I've ever seen.

---

### sturdy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstɜːrdi/ &middot; *STUR-dee*
**Русский:** крепкий, прочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sturdier*, *sturdiest*
**Opposite:** *flimsy*

Sturdy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Strongly built and not easily broken.** (прочный, добротный)

1. We need a *sturdy* table for the printer.
2. These boots are *sturdier* than the ones I had.

**2. Strong and solid-looking, of a person or animal.** (крепкий, коренастый)

3. He was a short, *sturdy* man with big hands.
4. The pony is small but *sturdy*.

---

### stylish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstaɪlɪʃ/ &middot; *STY-lish*
**Русский:** стильный, модный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stylish*, *most stylish*
**Form:** -ish from *style*

Fashionable and good-looking.

1. She always looks *stylish*, even in jeans.
2. It's a small but very *stylish* hotel.
3. His apartment is the *most stylish* place I've been in.

---

### stylistic

**Pronunciation:** /staɪˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *sty-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** стилистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *style*
**Contrast:** *Stylistic* is about style in writing or art; *stylish* is about looking good. A *stylistic* choice is not the same as a *stylish* one.

Having to do with the style of a piece of writing, music or art.

1. It's a *stylistic* choice, not a grammar rule.
2. The two albums have *stylistic* differences but the same band.
3. The edits were purely *stylistic*.

---

### stylized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈstaɪlaɪzd/ &middot; *STY-lyzd*
**Русский:** стилизованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more stylized*, *most stylized*
**Form:** -ed from *stylize*
**Contrast:** American spelling *stylized*; British *stylised*.

Drawn or done in an artificial style instead of a realistic one.

1. The logo is a *stylized* bird.
2. The fight scenes are highly *stylized*.
3. It's a *stylized* version of a 1950s diner.

---

### suave

**Pronunciation:** /swɑːv/ &middot; *SWAHV*
**Русский:** обходительный, учтивый, лощёный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suave*, *most suave*
**Contrast:** Often a little negative: *suave* suggests charm you don't quite trust. Said SWAHV, not 'soo-AHV'.

Smooth, confident and polite, usually a man.

1. He's a *suave* guy in an expensive suit.
2. The host was *suave* and a little too smooth.
3. He was the *most suave* person at the party.

---

### sub-Saharan

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌb səˈhɛrən/ &middot; *suhb-suh-HAIR-uhn*
**Русский:** расположенный к югу от Сахары
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sub-Saharan Africa*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *Saharan*

Of the part of Africa south of the Sahara desert.

1. Most of the program's work is in *sub-Saharan* Africa.
2. *Sub-Saharan* countries were hit hardest by the drought.
3. She studies *sub-Saharan* trade routes.

---

### subarctic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbˈɑːrktɪk/ &middot; *suhb-ARK-tik*
**Русский:** субарктический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a subarctic climate*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *arctic*

Of the cold region just south of the Arctic.

1. They spent a winter at a *subarctic* research station.
2. *Subarctic* forests cover much of Canada.
3. The town has a *subarctic* climate with short summers.

---

### subatomic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbəˈtɑːmɪk/ &middot; *suhb-uh-TAH-mik*
**Русский:** субатомный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *subatomic particles*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *atomic*

Smaller than an atom, or happening inside one.

1. Protons and electrons are *subatomic* particles.
2. The lab does *subatomic* physics.
3. At the *subatomic* level the rules are different.

---

### subconscious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbˈkɑːnʃəs/ &middot; *suhb-KAHN-shuhs*
**Русский:** подсознательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a subconscious fear*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *conscious*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *your subconscious*. The near-synonym *unconscious* is the one doctors use for someone who has passed out.

Happening in your mind without you knowing it.

1. It was a *subconscious* decision — I didn't plan it.
2. She has a *subconscious* fear of open water.
3. *Subconscious* bias shows up in who gets called back.

---

### subdued

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈduːd/ &middot; *suhb-DOOD*
**Русский:** притихший, подавленный; приглушённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more subdued*, *most subdued*
**Form:** -ed from *subdue*

Subdued has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Quieter and less cheerful than usual.** (притихший, подавленный)

1. Everyone was *subdued* after the meeting.
2. The crowd was strangely *subdued* for a playoff game.

**2. Soft and not bright, of light or color.** (приглушённый (о свете, цвете))

3. The restaurant has *subdued* lighting.
4. She painted the walls in *subdued* grays.

---

### subhuman

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbˈhjuːmən/ &middot; *suhb-HYOO-muhn*
**Русский:** нечеловеческий, скотский
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — before a noun (*subhuman conditions*) or after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *human*
**Contrast:** An ugly word. Calling people *subhuman* is the language of propaganda; *subhuman conditions* is the safer, ordinary use.

Below what any human being should have to live with, or treated as less than human.

1. The families lived in *subhuman* conditions.
2. The camp's *subhuman* conditions shocked the inspectors.
3. The propaganda painted them as *subhuman*.

---

### subject

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌbdʒɪkt/ &middot; *SUHB-jikt*
**Русский:** подверженный, зависящий (от)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, always with *to*: *subject to change*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [subject to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#subject-to)
**Contrast:** The adjective is stressed on the first syllable like the noun, SUHB-jikt; the verb *subject* is suhb-JEKT. As an adjective it never stands alone — it always has *to*.

Likely to be affected by something, or depending on it.

1. All prices are *subject* to change without notice.
2. The plan is *subject* to the board's approval.
3. This area is *subject* to flooding in the spring.

---

### subjective

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈdʒɛktɪv/ &middot; *suhb-JEK-tiv*
**Русский:** субъективный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more subjective*, *most subjective*
**Opposite:** *objective*
**Contrast:** *Subjective* is based on one person's feelings; *objective* is based on facts anyone can check.

Based on personal feelings and opinions rather than facts.

1. Taste in music is completely *subjective*.
2. That's a *subjective* judgment, not a fact.
3. Grading essays is *more subjective* than grading math.

---

### subjunctive

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈdʒʌŋktɪv/ &middot; *suhb-JUHNGK-tiv*
**Русский:** сослагательный (о наклонении)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the subjunctive mood*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *use the subjunctive*. In American English you meet it in *I insist that he be there* and *If I were you*.

Of the verb form used for wishes, demands and unreal situations.

1. English has a *subjunctive* mood, but it's easy to miss.
2. *Subjunctive* forms show up after *insist*, *demand* and *suggest*.
3. The teacher marked it wrong because the *subjunctive* form was missing.

---

### sublime

**Pronunciation:** /səˈblaɪm/ &middot; *suh-BLYM*
**Русский:** возвышенный, величественный; изумительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sublime*, *most sublime*
**Contrast:** Also a noun in *from the sublime to the ridiculous*. Said suh-BLYM, not like *subliminal*.

So beautiful or great that it fills you with wonder.

1. The view from the ridge was *sublime*.
2. Her performance was nothing short of *sublime*.
3. The pie was *sublime* — I had two pieces.

---

### subliminal

**Pronunciation:** /sʌbˈlɪmɪnəl/ &middot; *suhb-LIM-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** подсознательный, подпороговый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Affecting your mind below the level where you notice it.

1. The ad was accused of using *subliminal* messages.
2. There's a *subliminal* push to buy more than you need.
3. The effect is *subliminal* — you don't notice it happening.

---

### submerged

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈmɜːrdʒd/ &middot; *suhb-MURJD*
**Русский:** погружённый под воду, затопленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *submerge*

Under the surface of the water.

1. Half the town was *submerged* after the storm.
2. *Submerged* rocks make the channel dangerous.
3. The car sat *submerged* in six feet of water.

---

### submissive

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈmɪsɪv/ &middot; *suhb-MIS-iv*
**Русский:** покорный, послушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more submissive*, *most submissive*
**Opposite:** *assertive*

Always doing what other people want, without arguing.

1. He was raised to be quiet and *submissive*.
2. The dog rolled over in a *submissive* pose.
3. Her tone got *more submissive* the longer the boss talked.

---

### subordinate

**Pronunciation:** /səˈbɔːrdɪnət/ &middot; *suh-BOR-duh-nuht*
**Русский:** подчинённый, второстепенный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [subordinate to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#subordinate-to)
**Contrast:** The adjective and the noun end in a schwa, suh-BOR-duh-nuht; the verb ends -nate, suh-BOR-duh-nayt.

Subordinate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Lower in rank or less important than something else.** (подчинённый, второстепенный)

1. Everything else is *subordinate* to safety.
2. She holds a *subordinate* position in the department.

**2. In grammar, of a clause that depends on a main clause.** (придаточный (о предложении))

3. *Subordinate* clauses start with words like *because* and *although*.
4. Underline the *subordinate* clause in each sentence.

---

### subsequent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌbsɪkwənt/ &middot; *SUHB-si-kwuhnt*
**Русский:** последующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *subsequent years*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun, in formal writing: *subsequent to the hearing*
**Opposite:** *previous*
**Contrast:** Formal. *Subsequent* just means later; *consequent* means caused by it.

Coming after something else in time.

1. The *subsequent* investigation found nothing.
2. In *subsequent* years the town grew fast.
3. Each *subsequent* payment is smaller than the last.

---

### subservient

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈsɜːrviənt/ &middot; *suhb-SUR-vee-uhnt*
**Русский:** раболепный, угодливый; подчинённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more subservient*, *most subservient*
**Takes:** *to* + noun

Too willing to obey, or kept in a lower position.

1. He hated the *subservient* tone his coworkers used with the boss.
2. The board became *subservient* to the CEO.
3. She refuses to be *subservient* to anyone.

---

### subsidiary

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈsɪdiɛri/ &middot; *suhb-SID-ee-air-ee*
**Русский:** вспомогательный, второстепенный; дочерний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Contrast:** Also a common noun: *a subsidiary of the company*.

Less important than the main thing, and connected to it.

1. Money was a *subsidiary* concern at that point.
2. She played a *subsidiary* role in the negotiations.
3. These questions are *subsidiary* to the main one.

---

### subsidized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌbsɪdaɪzd/ &middot; *SUHB-si-dyzd*
**Русский:** субсидируемый, дотационный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *subsidize*
**Contrast:** American spelling *subsidized*; British *subsidised*.

Paid for in part by a government or an employer, so it costs less.

1. They live in *subsidized* housing.
2. The bus passes are *subsidized* by the city.
3. *Subsidized* school lunches keep a lot of kids fed.

---

### substandard

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbˈstændərd/ &middot; *suhb-STAN-durd*
**Русский:** некачественный, не отвечающий нормам
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *standard*

Below the quality that is required or expected.

1. The building went up with *substandard* materials.
2. Tenants complained about *substandard* heating.
3. The work was *substandard* and had to be redone.

---

### substantial

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈstænʃəl/ &middot; *suhb-STAN-shuhl*
**Русский:** значительный, существенный; солидный
**Type:** quantity; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more substantial*, *most substantial*
**Contrast:** *Substantial* is about size or solidity; *substantive* is about real content. A *substantial* meal fills you up; a *substantive* discussion gets somewhere.

Substantial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large in amount or important enough to matter.** (значительный, существенный)

1. They made a *substantial* donation to the school.
2. There's been *substantial* progress since March.

**2. Solidly built, or big and filling.** (солидный, основательный; сытный)

3. We had a *substantial* breakfast before the hike.
4. The desk is old but *substantial*.

---

### substantive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌbstəntɪv/ &middot; *SUHB-stuhn-tiv*
**Русский:** существенный, содержательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more substantive*, *most substantive*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *substantial*: *substantive* changes change the meaning, *substantial* changes are simply big.

Dealing with the real content of something, not the surface.

1. The draft needs *substantive* changes, not just edits.
2. For the first time they had a *substantive* discussion.
3. Nothing *substantive* came out of the meeting.

---

### substitute

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/ &middot; *SUHB-sti-toot*
**Русский:** замещающий, запасной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a substitute teacher*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*find a substitute*) and a verb (*substitute butter for oil*). As an adjective it only goes before a noun — you cannot say *the teacher was substitute*.

Taking the place of the usual person or thing.

1. We had a *substitute* teacher all week.
2. The coach put in a *substitute* goalie in the third period.
3. The district keeps a list of *substitute* drivers.

---

### subterranean

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbtəˈreɪniən/ &middot; *suhb-tuh-RAY-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** подземный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a subterranean tunnel*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *aboveground*

Under the ground.

1. The city has a whole *subterranean* network of tunnels.
2. *Subterranean* rivers run under the limestone.
3. They found a *subterranean* chamber beneath the church.

---

### subtropical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌbˈtrɑːpɪkəl/ &middot; *suhb-TRAH-pi-kuhl*
**Русский:** субтропический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a subtropical climate*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** sub- + *tropical*

Of the warm regions just outside the tropics.

1. South Florida has a *subtropical* climate.
2. *Subtropical* plants won't survive a hard freeze.
3. The storm formed in *subtropical* waters.

---

### suburban

**Pronunciation:** /səˈbɜːrbən/ &middot; *suh-BUR-buhn*
**Русский:** пригородный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suburban*, *most suburban*
**Contrast:** *Urban* is the city itself, *suburban* the ring of towns around it, *rural* the countryside.

Suburban has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In or of the suburbs, the residential areas outside a city.** (пригородный)

1. They moved to a *suburban* school district.
2. *Suburban* traffic is worst around three o'clock.

**2. Typical of the suburbs — comfortable, ordinary and a little dull.** (обывательский, мещанский)

3. The whole movie makes fun of *suburban* life.
4. This street feels *more suburban* than the rest of the city.

---

### subversive

**Pronunciation:** /səbˈvɜːrsɪv/ &middot; *suhb-VUR-siv*
**Русский:** подрывной; крамольный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more subversive*, *most subversive*
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a person: *the government called them subversives*.

Quietly working against a government, a system or accepted ideas.

1. The book was banned as *subversive*.
2. Her comedy is quietly *subversive*.
3. They were charged with *subversive* activity.

---

### succeeding

**Pronunciation:** /səkˈsiːdɪŋ/ &middot; *suhk-SEE-ding*
**Русский:** последующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *succeeding generations*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *succeed* in its older sense, to follow
**Contrast:** Here *succeeding* means following, not doing well. *Each succeeding year* means each year after it.

Coming after, one after another.

1. Each *succeeding* year the festival got bigger.
2. *Succeeding* generations told the story differently.
3. The rule was kept by every *succeeding* administration.

---

### successive

**Pronunciation:** /səkˈsɛsɪv/ &middot; *suhk-SES-iv*
**Русский:** следующий один за другим, последовательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *three successive wins*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Successive* means one after another; *successful* means it worked. *Three successive wins* is three in a row.

Following one after the other without a break.

1. It rained for five *successive* days.
2. She won three *successive* elections.
3. Each *successive* draft got shorter.

---

### succinct

**Pronunciation:** /səkˈsɪŋkt/ &middot; *suhk-SINGKT*
**Русский:** краткий, лаконичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more succinct*, *most succinct*
**Opposite:** *long-winded*

Saying a lot in very few words.

1. Her answer was *succinct* and clear.
2. Keep the summary *succinct* — one page at most.
3. The second draft is *more succinct* than the first.

---

### succulent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌkjələnt/ &middot; *SUHK-yuh-luhnt*
**Русский:** сочный (о еде); суккулентный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more succulent*, *most succulent*
**Contrast:** Also a noun for plants like aloe and jade: *a shelf of succulents*.

Succulent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Juicy and delicious, of meat or fruit.** (сочный, аппетитный)

1. The lamb was *succulent* and fell off the bone.
2. We ate *succulent* peaches all through August.

**2. Thick and fleshy, of a plant that stores water.** (суккулентный, мясистый)

3. Cactus and aloe are *succulent* plants.
4. *Succulent* leaves hold water through the dry season.

---

### Sudanese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːdəˈniːz/ &middot; *soo-duh-NEEZ*
**Русский:** суданский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ese from *Sudan*
**Contrast:** Like all *-ese* words it adds no *-s* for people: *two Sudanese students*. For one person say *a Sudanese man*, not *a Sudanese*.

Of or from Sudan, or its people.

1. The *Sudanese* students meet on Fridays.
2. Her father is *Sudanese*.
3. *Sudanese* coffee is served with ginger.

---

### sudden

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌdən/ &middot; *SUH-duhn*
**Русский:** внезапный, неожиданный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sudden*, *most sudden*
**Contrast:** The adverb is *suddenly*, never *sudden*. *All of a sudden* is a fixed phrase meaning suddenly.

Happening quickly and without warning.

1. There was a *sudden* drop in temperature.
2. A *sudden* noise woke everyone up.
3. His death was *sudden* — nobody had time to say goodbye.

---

### sufficient

**Pronunciation:** /səˈfɪʃənt/ &middot; *suh-FISH-uhnt*
**Русский:** достаточный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Opposite:** *insufficient*
**Contrast:** More formal than *enough*, and it goes before the noun: *sufficient evidence*, but *enough evidence*.

As much as is needed.

1. There wasn't *sufficient* evidence to charge anyone.
2. One cup of rice is *sufficient* for two people.
3. The money was *sufficient* to cover the repairs.

---

### suffocating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌfəkeɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *SUH-fuh-kay-ting*
**Русский:** удушающий; давящий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suffocating*, *most suffocating*
**Form:** -ing from *suffocate*

Suffocating has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So hot and airless that it is hard to breathe.** (удушливый, душный)

1. The bus was *suffocating* by noon.
2. A *suffocating* heat hung over the city.

**2. So controlling that you feel trapped.** (давящий, не дающий вздохнуть)

3. She found the relationship *suffocating*.
4. It was a *suffocating* little town to grow up in.

---

### Sufi

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːfi/ &middot; *SOO-fee*
**Русский:** суфийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *Sufi poetry*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a person: *a Sufi*. The tradition itself is *Sufism*, the mystical side of Islam.

Of the mystical tradition within Islam.

1. She teaches *Sufi* poetry in translation.
2. The *Sufi* orders spread across North Africa.
3. We heard *Sufi* music at the festival.

---

### sugar-coated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃʊɡər ˌkoʊtɪd/ &middot; *SHUU-gur-koh-tid*
**Русский:** покрытый сахаром; приукрашенный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *sugar* + participle *coated*

Sugar-coated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with a layer of sugar.** (в сахарной глазури, покрытый сахаром)

1. *Sugar-coated* cereal is basically dessert.
2. The pills are *sugar-coated* so kids will take them.

**2. Made to sound nicer than it really is.** (приукрашенный, подслащённый)

3. He gave us the *sugar-coated* version of the story.
4. Her report was *sugar-coated*, but the numbers were bad.

---

### sugarless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃʊɡərləs/ &middot; *SHUU-gur-luhs*
**Русский:** без сахара
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sugarless gum*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *sugar*
**Contrast:** *Sugar-free* is the more common label in American stores; *sugarless* is mostly used for gum and candy.

Made without sugar.

1. She chews *sugarless* gum all day.
2. They sell a *sugarless* version of the syrup.
3. *Sugarless* candy usually uses sorbitol.

---

### sugary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃʊɡəri/ &middot; *SHUU-guh-ree*
**Русский:** сладкий, приторный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sugary*, *most sugary*

Sugary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Containing a lot of sugar, or too sweet.** (сахаристый, слишком сладкий)

1. The kids drink way too many *sugary* sodas.
2. The sauce was thin and *sugary*.

**2. Too sweet in the way someone speaks or smiles.** (приторный, слащавый)

3. She answered in a *sugary* voice that fooled nobody.
4. He gave me a *sugary* smile and walked off.

---

### suggestive

**Pronunciation:** /səˈdʒɛstɪv/ &middot; *suh-JES-tiv*
**Русский:** наводящий на мысль; двусмысленный, фривольный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suggestive*, *most suggestive*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [suggestive of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#suggestive-of)
**Contrast:** Watch out for the second meaning: *a suggestive remark* is usually about sex, not about making a suggestion.

Suggestive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making you think of something; hinting at it.** (наводящий на мысль о чём-то)

1. The symptoms are *suggestive* of the flu.
2. The room had a smell *suggestive* of old books.

**2. Hinting at sex.** (двусмысленный, фривольный)

3. The lyrics are pretty *suggestive*.
4. He made a *suggestive* comment and the table went quiet.

---

### suicidal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːɪˈsaɪdəl/ &middot; *soo-uh-SY-duhl*
**Русский:** суицидальный; самоубийственный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suicidal*, *most suicidal*
**Form:** -al from *suicide*

Suicidal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Wanting to kill yourself.** (склонный к самоубийству, суицидальный)

1. He called the hotline because he was feeling *suicidal*.
2. *Suicidal* thoughts are a reason to get help right away.

**2. So dangerous or foolish that it could destroy you.** (самоубийственный, гибельный)

3. Raising prices now would be *suicidal*.
4. It was a *suicidal* charge across open ground.

---

### suited

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːtɪd/ &middot; *SOO-tid*
**Русский:** подходящий, годный (для)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, with *to* or *for*: *well suited to the job*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but almost always with *well*, *better* or *ideally*: *better suited to the job*
**Takes:** *to* or *for* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *suit*
**Contrast:** *Suited* needs *well*, *better* or *ideally* in front of it; plain *He is suited* sounds wrong. *Suitable* is the word that goes before a noun: *a suitable candidate*.

Right for a particular job, person or situation.

1. She's well *suited* to this kind of work.
2. The house isn't *suited* for a family of six.
3. Of the three, he's the best *suited* to run the meeting.

---

### sulky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌlki/ &middot; *SUHL-kee*
**Русский:** надутый, обиженный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sulkier*, *sulkiest*

Silently angry and showing it, the way a child does.

1. He's been *sulky* ever since we said no.
2. She gave me a *sulky* look and went to her room.
3. The longer we waited, the *sulkier* he got.

---

### sullen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌlən/ &middot; *SUH-luhn*
**Русский:** угрюмый, хмурый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sullen*, *most sullen*
**Contrast:** *Sullen* is grown-up and heavy; *sulky* is childish. Neither one is *solemn*, which is serious without the anger.

Sullen has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Quiet and bad-tempered, refusing to talk.** (угрюмый, мрачно молчаливый)

1. The boy sat *sullen* and quiet through dinner.
2. She answered with a *sullen* shrug.

**2. Dark and gloomy, of the sky or weather.** (хмурый, пасмурный)

3. A *sullen* gray sky hung over the lake.
4. The morning was cold and *sullen*.

---

### sultry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌltri/ &middot; *SUHL-tree*
**Русский:** знойный, душный; чувственный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sultrier*, *sultriest*

Sultry has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hot and humid, of the weather.** (знойный, душный)

1. It was a *sultry* August afternoon.
2. August in Houston is *sultrier* than anything I grew up with.

**2. Attractive in a slow, sexual way.** (чувственный, томный)

3. She has a *sultry* voice.
4. He gave her a *sultry* look across the bar.

---

### Sumerian

**Pronunciation:** /suːˈmɛriən/ &middot; *soo-MER-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** шумерский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the people and the language. Not *Sumatran*, which is about the island of Sumatra.

Of the ancient people of southern Mesopotamia, or their language.

1. The museum has a room of *Sumerian* tablets.
2. *Sumerian* is the oldest written language we can still read.
3. They spent the semester on *Sumerian* myths.

---

### summa cum laude

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʊmə kʊm ˈlaʊdə/ &middot; *suu-muh kuum LOW-duh*
**Русский:** с наивысшим отличием (о дипломе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a summa cum laude graduate*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Latin, 'with highest praise'. It is used even more often as an adverb after *graduate*: *She graduated summa cum laude*. The three levels, lowest first, are *cum laude*, *magna cum laude*, *summa cum laude*.

Graduating with the highest level of honors.

1. The program takes mostly *summa cum laude* graduates.
2. She put her *summa cum laude* honors right at the top of her résumé.
3. He was the only *summa cum laude* student in his class that year.

---

### summary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌməri/ &middot; *SUH-muh-ree*
**Русский:** суммарный, упрощённый (о процедуре)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *summary judgment*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Far more common as a noun (*write a summary*). The adjective is legal or official and means done fast, skipping the usual steps: *summary judgment*, *summary dismissal*.

Done quickly and without the usual procedure.

1. The judge granted *summary* judgment for the defense.
2. He was fired in a *summary* dismissal with no hearing.
3. The report described *summary* executions in the border towns.

---

### summery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌməri/ &middot; *SUH-muh-ree*
**Русский:** летний, по-летнему тёплый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more summery*, *most summery*
**Form:** -y from *summer*
**Contrast:** Said exactly like *summary*, but it means 'like summer': *a summery dress*, *summery weather*.

Like summer, or right for summer.

1. She wore a light, *summery* dress.
2. It's unusually *summery* for April.
3. The menu turns *summery* in June — lots of tomatoes and corn.

---

### sumptuous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌmptʃuəs/ &middot; *SUHMP-choo-uhs*
**Русский:** роскошный, пышный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sumptuous*, *most sumptuous*

Expensive and grand, in a way that shows.

1. They served a *sumptuous* dinner.
2. The hotel lobby is *sumptuous* — marble everywhere.
3. It was the *most sumptuous* meal I've had in years.

---

### sun-drenched

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌn ˌdrɛntʃt/ &middot; *SUHN-drencht*
**Русский:** залитый солнцем
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a sun-drenched patio*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *sun* + participle *drenched*
**Contrast:** Travel-brochure English. It almost never comes after a verb.

Getting a lot of bright sunshine.

1. We ate lunch on a *sun-drenched* patio.
2. The brochure promised *sun-drenched* beaches.
3. It's a *sun-drenched* apartment with windows on three sides.

---

### sun-dried

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌn ˌdraɪd/ &middot; *SUHN-dryd*
**Русский:** высушенный на солнце, вяленый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sun-dried tomatoes*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *sun* + participle *dried*

Dried in the sun instead of by machine.

1. Add *sun-dried* tomatoes to the pasta.
2. They sell *sun-dried* apricots by the pound.
3. *Sun-dried* bricks are still used in the region.

---

### sunburned

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌnbɜːrnd/ &middot; *SUHN-burnd*
**Русский:** обгоревший на солнце
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — degree comes from *badly* or *a little*: *badly sunburned*
**Form:** compound — noun *sun* + participle *burned*
**Contrast:** American English says *sunburned*; *sunburnt* is mostly British. *Tanned* is the brown you want, *sunburned* is the red you don't.

Red and sore from too much sun.

1. My shoulders are *sunburned*.
2. A *sunburned* tourist came in asking for aloe.
3. We got back from the lake *sunburned* and happy.

---

### sundry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌndri/ &middot; *SUHN-dree*
**Русский:** разный, всякий
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sundry items*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Formal or joking, and usually in the phrases *various and sundry* or *all and sundry* (everybody). The noun *sundries* means small odds and ends.

Of several different kinds, not worth listing one by one.

1. The drawer is full of pens, cords and *sundry* junk.
2. She handles billing, scheduling and *sundry* other tasks.
3. Various and *sundry* relatives showed up for dinner.

---

### sunken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌŋkən/ &middot; *SUHNG-kuhn*
**Русский:** затонувший; впалый; углублённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a sunken ship*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** the old participle of *sink*; the modern one is *sunk*
**Contrast:** *Sunken* only goes before a noun. After a verb you need *sunk*: *a sunken ship*, but *the ship has sunk*.

Sunken has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Lying under the water after sinking.** (затонувший, потопленный)

1. Divers found the *sunken* ship in 300 feet of water.
2. They mapped the *sunken* village under the reservoir.

**2. Hollow, of eyes or cheeks.** (впалый, ввалившийся)

3. He had *sunken* cheeks and dark circles under his eyes.
4. Her *sunken* eyes made her look exhausted.

**3. Built lower than the floor or ground around it.** (углублённый, заглублённый)

5. The house has a *sunken* living room.
6. There's a *sunken* garden behind the church.

---

### sunlit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌnlɪt/ &middot; *SUHN-lit*
**Русский:** освещённый солнцем, солнечный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a sunlit room*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *sun* + participle *lit*

Lit by the sun.

1. We sat in a *sunlit* corner of the café.
2. The *sunlit* kitchen is the best room in the house.
3. Dust floated in the *sunlit* air.

---

### sunny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌni/ &middot; *SUH-nee*
**Русский:** солнечный; жизнерадостный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sunnier*, *sunniest*
**Form:** -y from *sun*

Sunny has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With a lot of sunshine.** (солнечный, ясный)

1. It'll be *sunny* and warm all weekend.
2. They have a *sunny* apartment on the top floor.
3. Saturday was *sunnier* than Sunday.

**2. Cheerful and positive.** (жизнерадостный, светлый)

4. She has a *sunny* personality.
5. He keeps a *sunny* outlook even when things go wrong.

---

### sunny-side up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌni saɪd ˈʌp/ &middot; *suh-nee-syd-UHP*
**Русский:** глазунья, жаренная с одной стороны
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** after the noun — *two eggs sunny-side up*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Only about fried eggs, and it follows the noun. The usual alternatives in a diner are *over easy*, *over hard* and *scrambled*.

Fried on one side only, so the yolk stays whole and runny.

1. I'll have two eggs *sunny-side up*.
2. He likes his eggs *sunny-side up* with rye toast.
3. The plate came with hash browns and an egg *sunny-side up*.

---

### super

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːpər/ &middot; *SOO-pur*
**Русский:** отличный, классный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Informal. *Super* is also used in front of other adjectives as an intensifier (*super tired*, *super expensive*) — that is an adverb, not this adjective. In a city apartment building, *the super* is the superintendent.

Very good indeed.

1. We had a *super* time at the lake.
2. That's *super* — thanks for setting it up.
3. The food was *super*, and the service was fast.

---

### superb

**Pronunciation:** /suːˈpɜːrb/ &middot; *soo-PURB*
**Русский:** превосходный, великолепный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme adjective: *absolutely superb*, not *very superb*

Excellent — as good as it gets.

1. The pasta was *superb*.
2. She gave a *superb* performance.
3. The seats were *superb* — right behind home plate.

---

### supercharged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːpərtʃɑːrdʒd/ &middot; *SOO-pur-chahrjd*
**Русский:** с наддувом; наэлектризованный, заряженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** super- + -ed from *charge*

Supercharged has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Fitted with a supercharger for extra engine power.** (с нагнетателем, форсированный)

1. The truck has a *supercharged* V8.
2. A *supercharged* engine eats gas.

**2. Full of energy or strong feeling.** (наэлектризованный, накалённый)

3. The room was *supercharged* with excitement.
4. It was a *supercharged* political season.

---

### supercilious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/ &middot; *soo-pur-SIL-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** высокомерный, надменный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more supercilious*, *most supercilious*

Acting as if other people are beneath you.

1. The waiter had a *supercilious* way of taking orders.
2. He was polite but faintly *supercilious*.
3. Nothing ruins a tour like a *supercilious* guide.

---

### superficial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃəl/ &middot; *soo-pur-FISH-uhl*
**Русский:** поверхностный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more superficial*, *most superficial*
**Opposite:** *thorough*

Superficial has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not deep or careful; only looking at the surface of a subject.** (поверхностный, неглубокий)

1. He has a *superficial* knowledge of the subject.
2. The review was *superficial* — nobody actually read the code.

**2. Only on the surface, of a wound or damage.** (поверхностный (о ране, повреждении))

3. The cuts were *superficial* and healed in a week.
4. The car had *superficial* damage to the bumper.

**3. Caring only about looks and appearances, of a person.** (пустой, поверхностный (о человеке))

5. She called the whole scene shallow and *superficial*.
6. He's not *superficial* — he just likes nice clothes.

---

### superfluous

**Pronunciation:** /suːˈpɜːrfluəs/ &middot; *soo-PUR-floo-uhs*
**Русский:** лишний, излишний
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The stress is on the second syllable, soo-PUR-floo-uhs, not on the *-flu-*.

Not needed, because there is already enough.

1. Half the paragraph is *superfluous* — cut it.
2. He added a *superfluous* apology at the end.
3. With the chart right there, the table is *superfluous*.

---

### superhuman

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈhjuːmən/ &middot; *soo-pur-HYOO-muhn*
**Русский:** сверхчеловеческий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *superhuman strength*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** super- + *human*

Greater than any normal person could manage.

1. It took a *superhuman* effort to finish on time.
2. The character has *superhuman* strength.
3. Nobody expects *superhuman* patience, but some would help.

---

### superimposed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərɪmˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *soo-pur-im-POHZD*
**Русский:** наложенный (сверху)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb, with *on*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *on* or *over* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *superimpose*

Placed on top of something else so both can be seen.

1. The subtitles are *superimposed* on the picture.
2. A grid was *superimposed* over the map.
3. The *superimposed* images show how far the glacier moved.

---

### superlative

**Pronunciation:** /suːˈpɜːrlətɪv/ &middot; *soo-PUR-luh-tiv*
**Русский:** превосходный; превосходной степени
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Two jobs, and they feel unrelated: *superlative service* means excellent, while *the superlative form* is the grammar term. Also a noun: *biggest* is a superlative.

Superlative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the very highest quality.** (превосходный, наилучший)

1. The staff gave *superlative* service.
2. It was a *superlative* piece of reporting.

**2. In grammar, of the form that says 'the most'.** (превосходной степени)

3. *Biggest* is the *superlative* form of *big*.
4. Use *the* in front of a *superlative* adjective.

---

### supernatural

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈnætʃərəl/ &middot; *soo-pur-NACH-uh-ruhl*
**Русский:** сверхъестественный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** super- + *natural*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the supernatural*.

Not explainable by the laws of nature — ghosts, magic and the like.

1. The show is full of *supernatural* stuff — ghosts, curses, the works.
2. He doesn't believe in anything *supernatural*.
3. *Supernatural* powers are standard in that genre.

---

### supersonic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈsɑːnɪk/ &middot; *soo-pur-SAH-nik*
**Русский:** сверхзвуковой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *supersonic flight*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** super- + *sonic*
**Opposite:** *subsonic*

Faster than the speed of sound.

1. The Concorde was a *supersonic* jet.
2. *Supersonic* flight over land is banned in the US.
3. They tested a *supersonic* missile last spring.

---

### superstitious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈstɪʃəs/ &middot; *soo-pur-STISH-uhs*
**Русский:** суеверный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more superstitious*, *most superstitious*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** -ous from *superstition*

Believing that certain things bring good or bad luck.

1. My grandmother was *superstitious* about birds in the house.
2. Ballplayers are the *most superstitious* people I know.
3. It's a *superstitious* habit, but I still knock on wood.

---

### supervised

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsuːpərvaɪzd/ &middot; *SOO-pur-vyzd*
**Русский:** контролируемый, под присмотром
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — degree comes from *closely* or *loosely*: *closely supervised*
**Form:** -ed from *supervise*
**Opposite:** *unsupervised*

Watched over by someone in charge.

1. The kids get one hour of *supervised* swimming.
2. He has *supervised* visits twice a month.
3. The lab work is closely *supervised*.

---

### supervisory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsuːpərˈvaɪzəri/ &middot; *soo-pur-VY-zuh-ree*
**Русский:** руководящий, надзорный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a supervisory role*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with managing other workers.

1. She moved into a *supervisory* position last year.
2. The job has no *supervisory* duties.
3. *Supervisory* staff meet on Mondays.

---

### supple

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌpəl/ &middot; *SUH-puhl*
**Русский:** гибкий, мягкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *suppler*, *supplest*, or *more supple*, *most supple*
**Opposite:** *stiff*

Supple has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Bending easily without cracking, of leather or cloth.** (мягкий, податливый)

1. The jacket is old but still *supple*.
2. The leather gets *more supple* with use.

**2. Able to bend and move easily, of a body.** (гибкий, пластичный)

3. Yoga keeps her *supple*.
4. Dancers stay *supple* into their fifties.

---

### supplemental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌplɪˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *suhp-luh-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** дополнительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *supplemental income*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Supplemental* is the everyday American form — *supplemental insurance*, *Supplemental Security Income*. *Supplementary* means the same thing and sounds more British.

Added to something to make it complete or bigger.

1. He drives evenings for *supplemental* income.
2. The plan comes with *supplemental* dental coverage.
3. Students got a *supplemental* reading list.

---

### supplementary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsʌpləˈmɛntəri/ &middot; *suhp-luh-MEN-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** дополнительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Contrast:** The same meaning as *supplemental*, which Americans reach for more often. In geometry, *supplementary angles* add up to 180 degrees.

Added to something else as an extra part.

1. The packet has *supplementary* materials for teachers.
2. These notes are *supplementary* to the main text.
3. *Supplementary* angles add up to 180 degrees.

---

### supporting

**Pronunciation:** /səˈpɔːrtɪŋ/ &middot; *suh-POR-ting*
**Русский:** второстепенный (о роли); подтверждающий; несущий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a supporting role*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *support*
**Contrast:** *Supporting* describes the part something plays (*a supporting actor*); *supportive* describes a person who helps you (*a supportive friend*).

Supporting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Playing a smaller part next to the main one.** (второстепенный (о роли, актёре))

1. She won an Oscar for Best *Supporting* Actress.
2. He had a *supporting* role in two movies that year.

**2. Backing something up — a claim, or a structure.** (подтверждающий; несущий)

3. The lawyer filed *supporting* documents.
4. Don't cut into a *supporting* wall.

---

### supportive

**Pronunciation:** /səˈpɔːrtɪv/ &middot; *suh-POR-tiv*
**Русский:** поддерживающий, участливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more supportive*, *most supportive*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Opposite:** *unsupportive*
**Contrast:** Not *supporting*: a *supportive* friend helps you, a *supporting* actor has the second-biggest part.

Giving help, encouragement and sympathy.

1. Her family has been very *supportive*.
2. He's *supportive* of the whole idea.
3. I wish my boss were *more supportive*.

---

### supposed

**Pronunciation:** /səˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *suh-POHZD*
**Русский:** предполагаемый, мнимый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the supposed cure*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *suppose*
**Contrast:** Two pronunciations. Before a noun it is suh-POHZD and means 'so-called'. In *be supposed to* it is suh-POHST and means 'ought to': *You're supposed to call first*.

Said to be so, but probably not true.

1. The *supposed* cure did nothing at all.
2. They arrested the *supposed* leader of the group.
3. His *supposed* alibi fell apart in an hour.

---

### suppressed

**Pronunciation:** /səˈprɛst/ &middot; *suh-PREST*
**Русский:** подавленный; скрываемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *suppress*

Suppressed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Held back so it doesn't show, of a feeling or a sound.** (подавленный, сдерживаемый)

1. She answered with *suppressed* anger.
2. There was a *suppressed* laugh at the back of the room.

**2. Kept from being published or made known.** (скрытый, утаённый)

3. The report was *suppressed* for two years.
4. *Suppressed* evidence can get a case thrown out.

---

### supreme

**Pronunciation:** /səˈpriːm/ &middot; *suh-PREEM*
**Русский:** высший, верховный; величайший
**Type:** quality; also emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun; only the fixed phrase *reign supreme* comes after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable

Supreme has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Highest in rank, power or authority.** (верховный, высший)

1. The *Supreme* Court agreed to hear the case.
2. He was the *supreme* commander of the allied forces.
3. In this house the cat reigns *supreme*.

**2. Greatest possible in degree.** (величайший, высочайший)

4. It took a *supreme* effort to keep from laughing.
5. She testified with *supreme* confidence.

---

### sure-fire

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃʊrˌfaɪər/ &middot; *SHUUR-fy-ur*
**Русский:** беспроигрышный, верный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a sure-fire hit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *sure* + *fire*
**Contrast:** Often written as one word: *surefire*. It is informal, and it stays before the noun.

Certain to work or to succeed.

1. Lemon bars are a *sure-fire* crowd-pleaser.
2. There's no *sure-fire* way to get a toddler to sleep.
3. Asking about his kids is a *sure-fire* way to start a conversation.

---

### sure-footed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃʊrˈfʊtɪd/ &middot; *shuur-FUUT-id*
**Русский:** уверенно ступающий; уверенный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a sure-footed mule*, *The mule is sure-footed*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sure-footed*, *most sure-footed*
**Form:** compound — *sure* + *-footed*

Sure-footed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to walk or climb without slipping.** (не оступающийся, уверенно идущий)

1. Goats are remarkably *sure-footed* on bare rock.
2. You want a *sure-footed* horse on this trail.

**2. Confident and unlikely to make a mistake.** (уверенный, не делающий промахов)

3. The new mayor has been *sure-footed* in her first month.
4. It's a *sure-footed* performance from a young actor.

---

### surface-to-air

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɜːrfəs tə ˈɛr/ &middot; *SUR-fuhs-tuh-AIR*
**Русский:** класса «земля — воздух»
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a surface-to-air missile*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *to* + noun

Fired from the ground at something flying.

1. The base is defended by *surface-to-air* missiles.
2. They brought the drone down with a *surface-to-air* system.
3. *Surface-to-air* batteries ring the capital.

---

### surgical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *SUR-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** хирургический; точечный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *surgical gloves*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *surgery*

Surgical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with surgery.** (хирургический)

1. The nurse handed him a *surgical* mask.
2. This needs a *surgical* solution, not more pills.

**2. Done with great precision, hitting only what is aimed at.** (точечный, ювелирно точный)

3. The air force called it a *surgical* strike.
4. She cut the paragraph with *surgical* precision.

---

### surging

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrdʒɪŋ/ &middot; *SUR-jing*
**Русский:** резко растущий; вздымающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *surging prices*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *surge*

Surging has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Rising quickly and by a lot.** (резко растущий)

1. *Surging* rents pushed families out of the city.
2. The company blamed *surging* costs for the layoffs.

**2. Moving forward in a powerful wave.** (вздымающийся, напирающий)

3. The *surging* crowd carried us toward the gate.
4. *Surging* water tore the dock loose.

---

### surly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrli/ &middot; *SUR-lee*
**Русский:** угрюмый, неприветливый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both — *a surly waiter*, *He got surly*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *surlier*, *surliest*
**Opposite:** *cheerful*

Rude and unfriendly in a bad-tempered way.

1. The waiter was *surly* and slow.
2. He gave me a *surly* look and went back to his phone.
3. Teenagers get *surlier* when they're tired.

---

### surplus

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrpləs/ &middot; *SUR-pluhs*
**Русский:** избыточный, излишний
**Type:** quantity; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *surplus cash*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a budget surplus*, *the city has a surplus*. The opposite noun is *a deficit*.

Left over after what is needed has been used.

1. The store sells *surplus* army gear.
2. We put the *surplus* money into savings.
3. *Surplus* grain is sold abroad.

---

### surreal

**Pronunciation:** /səˈriːəl/ &middot; *suh-REE-uhl*
**Русский:** сюрреалистический; нереальный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a surreal scene*, *The whole thing felt surreal*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more surreal*, *most surreal*
**Form:** from *surrealism*

So strange that it feels like a dream and not like real life.

1. The whole evening felt *surreal*.
2. It was *surreal* to see my own face on a billboard.
3. The movie has a *surreal*, dreamlike quality.

---

### surreptitious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɜːrəpˈtɪʃəs/ &middot; *sur-uhp-TISH-uhs*
**Русский:** тайный, скрытный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a surreptitious glance*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more surreptitious*, *most surreptitious*
**Opposite:** *open*

Done quietly and in secret, so that nobody notices.

1. She took a *surreptitious* glance at her phone.
2. He made a *surreptitious* copy of the file.
3. They exchanged *surreptitious* looks across the table.

---

### surrogate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrəɡət/ &middot; *SUR-uh-guht*
**Русский:** суррогатный; замещающий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a surrogate mother*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the surrogate gave birth in March*; in politics a *surrogate* is someone who speaks for a candidate.

Taking the place of someone or something else.

1. Her aunt was a *surrogate* mother to her.
2. The couple is working with a *surrogate* mother.
3. Test scores are a *surrogate* measure of what kids actually learned.

---

### surrounding

**Pronunciation:** /səˈraʊndɪŋ/ &middot; *suh-ROWN-ding*
**Русский:** окружающий, окрестный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the surrounding hills*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *surround*
**Contrast:** The plural noun *surroundings* means окружение, обстановка: *The cat is nervous in new surroundings*.

Lying all around a place.

1. The fire spread to the *surrounding* woods.
2. People came from Dallas and the *surrounding* towns.
3. You can see the *surrounding* countryside from the tower.

---

### surviving

**Pronunciation:** /sərˈvaɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *sur-VY-ving*
**Русский:** оставшийся в живых; сохранившийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the surviving crew*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *survive*

Surviving has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Still alive after others have died.** (оставшийся в живых)

1. He is the last *surviving* member of the band.
2. The *surviving* passengers were flown home.

**2. Still in existence after a long time.** (сохранившийся, уцелевший)

3. It's the only *surviving* copy of the letter.
4. The *surviving* sections of the wall date from 1780.

---

### susceptible

**Pronunciation:** /səˈsɛptəbəl/ &middot; *suh-SEP-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** восприимчивый, подверженный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *He is susceptible to flattery*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more susceptible*, *most susceptible*
**Takes:** *to* + noun (what affects you)
**Preposition patterns:** [susceptible of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#susceptible-to-susceptible-of) &middot; [susceptible to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#susceptible-to-susceptible-of)
**Form:** -ible from Latin *suscipere*
**Opposite:** *immune*, *resistant*

Susceptible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Easily affected or harmed by something.** (подверженный, уязвимый)

1. Older people are more *susceptible* to the flu.
2. These plants are *susceptible* to frost.

**2. Easily influenced by other people or by feelings.** (восприимчивый, легко поддающийся)

3. He's *susceptible* to flattery.
4. Young fans are *susceptible* to this kind of advertising.

---

### suspect

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsʌspɛkt/ &middot; *SUS-pekt*
**Русский:** подозрительный, сомнительный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a suspect package*, *His motives are suspect*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suspect*, *most suspect*
**Contrast:** Stress separates the words: the adjective and the noun are SUS-pekt (*suspect data*, *the suspect fled*), the verb is suh-SPEKT (*I suspect he knows*).

Probably dishonest, unreliable or dangerous.

1. His motives are *suspect*.
2. Police blew up a *suspect* package at the station.
3. The numbers in that report look *suspect* to me.

---

### suspected

**Pronunciation:** /səˈspɛktɪd/ &middot; *suh-SPEK-tid*
**Русский:** предполагаемый, подозреваемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a suspected leak*; after a verb it is the passive verb, not the adjective
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *suspect*
**Contrast:** *Suspected* is what people believe is true (*a suspected case of measles*); *suspicious* is what looks wrong (*suspicious behavior*).

Believed, but not proved, to be the thing named.

1. Doctors reported two *suspected* cases of measles.
2. The *suspected* cause is a gas leak.
3. Police arrested the *suspected* driver an hour later.

---

### suspended

**Pronunciation:** /səˈspɛndɪd/ &middot; *suh-SPEN-did*
**Русский:** подвешенный; приостановленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a suspended ceiling*, *Her license is suspended*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *suspend*

Suspended has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hanging from something above.** (подвешенный)

1. A microphone was *suspended* over the stage.
2. The kitchen has *suspended* lights over the island.

**2. Officially stopped for a period of time.** (приостановленный, условный)

3. He got a *suspended* sentence and no jail time.
4. Her license is *suspended* until March.

---

### suspenseful

**Pronunciation:** /səˈspɛnsfəl/ &middot; *suh-SPENS-fuhl*
**Русский:** напряжённый, держащий в напряжении
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a suspenseful movie*, *The ending is suspenseful*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suspenseful*, *most suspenseful*
**Form:** -ful from *suspense*

Keeping you tense and eager to know what happens next.

1. The last twenty minutes are really *suspenseful*.
2. She writes *suspenseful* thrillers set in small towns.
3. It's a quiet book, but the ending is *suspenseful*.

---

### suspicious

**Pronunciation:** /səˈspɪʃəs/ &middot; *suh-SPISH-uhs*
**Русский:** подозревающий; подозрительный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both — *suspicious behavior*, *She was suspicious*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more suspicious*, *most suspicious*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing* (who or what you suspect); *that*-clause
**Preposition patterns:** [suspicious of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#suspicious-of)
**Form:** -ous from *suspicion*
**Opposite:** *trusting* (feeling); *innocent* (looking)
**Contrast:** It works both ways: *He is suspicious* can mean he suspects someone, or that he looks like a criminal — the *of*-phrase settles it. *Suspicious* is how something looks; *suspected* is what people believe.

Suspicious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Feeling that something is wrong or that someone is lying.** (подозревающий, недоверчивый)

1. She was *suspicious* of his sudden generosity.
2. I'm *suspicious* that they knew all along.

**2. Making people think that something is wrong.** (подозрительный, вызывающий подозрение)

3. Neighbors reported a *suspicious* van on the street.
4. There was nothing *suspicious* about the fire.

---

### sustainable

**Pronunciation:** /səˈsteɪnəbəl/ &middot; *suh-STAY-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** экологичный, устойчивый; посильный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — *sustainable farming*, *The pace isn't sustainable*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sustainable*, *most sustainable*
**Form:** -able from *sustain*
**Opposite:** *unsustainable*

Sustainable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Using resources in a way that does not use them up or damage the planet.** (экологически устойчивый)

1. The farm uses *sustainable* methods.
2. Bamboo is a more *sustainable* material than plastic.

**2. Able to be kept going at the same level.** (посильный, который можно выдержать)

3. That pace isn't *sustainable* over a whole season.
4. They want growth that is *sustainable* without more debt.

---

### sustained

**Pronunciation:** /səˈsteɪnd/ &middot; *suh-STAYND*
**Русский:** длительный, непрерывный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sustained applause*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sustain*

Continuing at the same level for a long time.

1. The speech got a full minute of *sustained* applause.
2. It will take a *sustained* effort to fix the schools.
3. The region has seen *sustained* growth for a decade.

---

### svelte

**Pronunciation:** /svɛlt/ &middot; *SVELT*
**Русский:** стройный, изящный
**Type:** quality; also size
**Position:** both — *a svelte dancer*, *She looked svelte*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more svelte*, *most svelte*
**Contrast:** A complimentary word, used of people and of sleek objects; *skinny* and *scrawny* are the unkind ones.

Slim and elegant.

1. She looked *svelte* in a plain black dress.
2. The new laptop is *svelte* enough to disappear in a bag.
3. He came back from the trip tanned and *svelte*.

---

### swampy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswɑːmpi/ &middot; *SWAHM-pee*
**Русский:** болотистый, топкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *swampy ground*, *The yard is swampy*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *swampier*, *swampiest*
**Form:** -y from *swamp*

Soft, wet and muddy like a swamp.
