1. The back half of the yard is *swampy* after a storm.
2. They built the road across *swampy* ground.
3. The trail gets *swampier* the closer you get to the lake.

---

### swanky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswæŋki/ &middot; *SWANG-kee*
**Русский:** шикарный, роскошный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a swanky hotel*, *The place is swanky*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *swankier*, *swankiest*
**Contrast:** Informal and a little teasing — it hints that the money is on display. *Elegant* is the straight compliment.

Expensive and stylish in a showy way.

1. They had dinner at a *swanky* hotel downtown.
2. It's a *swanky* neighborhood — nothing sells under a million.
3. His new office is *swankier* than mine.

---

### swarthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswɔːrði/ &middot; *SWOR-thee*
**Русский:** смуглый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a swarthy sailor*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *swarthier*, *swarthiest*
**Contrast:** Mostly met in older fiction, and often stereotyping when used of people today; *dark-skinned* or *olive-skinned* is the neutral choice.

Having dark skin.

1. The captain was a *swarthy* man with a gold tooth.
2. In the novel every villain is *swarthy* and foreign.
3. He was *swarthier* than his brothers.

---

### swashbuckling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswɑːʃˌbʌklɪŋ/ &middot; *SWAHSH-buk-ling*
**Русский:** лихой, авантюрный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a swashbuckling pirate*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *swash* + *buckling*, from old sword fighting

Full of bold, romantic adventure, like an old pirate story.

1. It's an old-fashioned *swashbuckling* adventure movie.
2. He plays a *swashbuckling* pirate captain.
3. She wrote *swashbuckling* tales of the high seas.

---

### sweeping

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswiːpɪŋ/ &middot; *SWEE-ping*
**Русский:** радикальный, широкий; огульный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *sweeping changes*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sweeping*, *most sweeping*
**Form:** -ing from *sweep*

Sweeping has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Affecting a great many things; wide in scope.** (радикальный, всеобъемлющий)

1. The new law makes *sweeping* changes to the tax code.
2. The changes are more *sweeping* than anyone expected.

**2. Too general — made without looking at the details.** (огульный, слишком общий)

3. That's a *sweeping* generalization about an entire country.
4. He made *sweeping* claims he couldn't back up.

**3. Wide and curving, of a view or a line.** (широкий, размашистый)

5. The house has a *sweeping* view of the bay.
6. A *sweeping* staircase curves up from the lobby.

---

### sweet

**Pronunciation:** /swiːt/ &middot; *SWEET*
**Русский:** сладкий; милый, добрый; приятный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a sweet apple*, *The tea is too sweet*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *sweeter*, *sweetest*
**Takes:** *of* + person in *That's sweet of you*; *on* + person, informal (*He's sweet on her*)
**Opposite:** *sour*, *bitter* (taste); *dry* (of wine)
**Contrast:** *Sweet* is the taste itself; *sweetened* means sugar was added. As a noun, *sweets* is candy; British English uses *a sweet* for dessert, American English says *dessert*.

Sweet has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Tasting of sugar.** (сладкий)

1. The corn was *sweet* and fresh.
2. This lemonade is too *sweet* for me.
3. Georgia peaches are *sweeter* than the ones at the store.

**2. Kind, gentle and lovable.** (милый, добрый)

4. What a *sweet* little kid.
5. It was *sweet* of you to call.

**3. Pleasant to smell, to hear or to experience.** (приятный, сладостный)

6. The *sweet* smell of cut grass came through the window.
7. After that season, the win was especially *sweet*.

---

### sweet-smelling

**Pronunciation:** /ˌswiːtˈsmɛlɪŋ/ &middot; *sweet-SMEL-ing*
**Русский:** душистый, благоухающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *sweet-smelling flowers*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sweet-smelling*, *most sweet-smelling*
**Form:** compound — adjective + *-ing* participle
**Opposite:** *foul-smelling*

Having a pleasant smell.

1. The room was full of *sweet-smelling* flowers.
2. She uses a *sweet-smelling* soap from the farmers market.
3. *Sweet-smelling* smoke drifted over from the grill.

---

### sweetened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswiːtənd/ &middot; *SWEE-tuhnd*
**Русский:** подслащённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *sweetened tea*, *The yogurt is already sweetened*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *sweeten*
**Opposite:** *unsweetened*
**Contrast:** *Sweetened* means sugar or syrup was added; *sweet* is just the taste. The canned milk is *sweetened condensed milk*, never *sweet condensed milk*.

Made sweet by adding sugar or another sweetener.

1. He drinks *sweetened* iced tea by the gallon.
2. The recipe calls for *sweetened* condensed milk.
3. This yogurt is lightly *sweetened* with honey.

---

### swell

**Pronunciation:** /swɛl/ &middot; *SWEL*
**Русский:** отличный, классный (разг., устар.)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a swell idea*, *That's swell*
**Comparison:** not gradable — no *sweller*
**Contrast:** Dated and informal: you meet it in old movies and in dry sarcasm (*Oh, that's just swell*). *Swell* is also a verb (*My ankle swelled up*) and a noun (*a big swell out past the reef*).

Very good — an old-fashioned way of saying great.

1. That's a *swell* idea.
2. We had a *swell* time at the lake.
3. Oh, *swell* — now the printer is broken.

---

### sweltering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswɛltərɪŋ/ &middot; *SWEL-tur-ing*
**Русский:** изнуряюще жаркий, знойный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both — *a sweltering afternoon*, *It was sweltering*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sweltering*, *most sweltering*
**Form:** -ing from *swelter*
**Opposite:** *freezing*

Uncomfortably hot and humid.

1. It was *sweltering* in the car.
2. They practiced through a *sweltering* August afternoon.
3. The kitchen gets *sweltering* when the oven is on.

---

### swift

**Pronunciation:** /swɪft/ &middot; *SWIFT*
**Русский:** быстрый, стремительный; скорый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a swift reply*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *swifter*, *swiftest*
**Form:** adverb *swiftly*
**Opposite:** *slow*
**Contrast:** *Swift* is more formal than *fast* and goes with actions and reactions more than with machines: *a swift response*, but *a fast car*. A *swift* is also a small bird.

Swift has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening or done very quickly.** (скорый, незамедлительный)

1. The response from the city was *swift*.
2. He gave a *swift* nod and walked out.

**2. Moving fast.** (быстрый, стремительный)

3. The river is *swifter* here than it is downstream.
4. A *swift* current pulled the boat sideways.

---

### swollen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈswoʊlən/ &middot; *SWOH-luhn*
**Русский:** распухший, опухший; вздувшийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a swollen ankle*, *My hand is swollen*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more swollen*, *most swollen*
**Form:** past participle of *swell*

Swollen has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Bigger than normal because of injury or illness.** (опухший, распухший)

1. Her ankle was *swollen* and purple.
2. He woke up with *swollen* glands.

**2. Filled past its usual size, especially with water.** (вздувшийся, разлившийся)

3. The creek was *swollen* with snowmelt.
4. *Swollen* rivers flooded the low fields.

---

### sworn

**Pronunciation:** /swɔːrn/ &middot; *SWORN*
**Русский:** данный под присягой; заклятый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *sworn testimony*, *sworn enemies*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *swear*

Sworn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Given or made under oath.** (данный под присягой)

1. The lawyer read his *sworn* statement aloud.
2. Only *sworn* officers can make the arrest.

**2. Declared once and for good — of enemies and promises.** (заклятый, клятвенный)

3. The two families have been *sworn* enemies for years.
4. He is a *sworn* opponent of the whole program.

---

### sycophantic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪkəˈfæntɪk/ &middot; *sik-uh-FAN-tik*
**Русский:** льстивый, подхалимский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *sycophantic praise*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more sycophantic*, *most sycophantic*
**Form:** -ic from *sycophant*
**Contrast:** A false friend: Russian *сикофант* is a slanderer or informer, while English *sycophant* is a подхалим — someone who flatters a powerful person.

Praising a powerful person in an insincere way to get something.

1. The interview was *sycophantic* from the first question.
2. He's surrounded by *sycophantic* aides who agree with everything.
3. Her review of the album is embarrassingly *sycophantic*.

---

### symbiotic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪmbaɪˈɑːtɪk/ &middot; *sim-by-AH-tik*
**Русский:** симбиотический; взаимовыгодный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a symbiotic relationship*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *symbiosis*

Symbiotic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Describing two living things that live together and help each other.** (симбиотический)

1. Clownfish and anemones have a *symbiotic* relationship.
2. The fungus forms a *symbiotic* bond with the tree roots.

**2. Describing two people or groups who each need the other.** (взаимовыгодный, взаимозависимый)

3. Reporters and campaigns have a *symbiotic* relationship.
4. The two companies grew in a *symbiotic* way.

---

### symbolic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪmˈbɑːlɪk/ &middot; *sim-BAH-lik*
**Русский:** символический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a symbolic gesture*, *The dove is symbolic of peace*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more symbolic*, *most symbolic*
**Takes:** *of* + noun (what it stands for)
**Form:** -ic from *symbol*

Symbolic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Standing for something else.** (символизирующий, символический)

1. The dove is *symbolic* of peace.
2. Every color in the painting is *symbolic*.

**2. Done to make a point rather than to have a real effect.** (символический, формальный)

3. The fine was *symbolic* — twenty dollars.
4. It was a *symbolic* vote with no legal force.

---

### symmetrical

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈmɛtrɪkəl/ &middot; *si-MET-ri-kuhl*
**Русский:** симметричный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both — *a symmetrical design*, *The garden is symmetrical*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more symmetrical*, *most symmetrical*
**Form:** -al from *symmetry*
**Opposite:** *asymmetrical*
**Contrast:** Also written *symmetric*, mostly in math and science: *a symmetric matrix*.

Having two halves that match exactly.

1. The garden is perfectly *symmetrical*.
2. Her face is more *symmetrical* than most.
3. Draw a *symmetrical* pattern on both sides of the fold.

---

### symphonic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪmˈfɑːnɪk/ &middot; *sim-FAH-nik*
**Русский:** симфонический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *symphonic music*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *symphony*
**Contrast:** The orchestra itself is a *symphony orchestra*, with the noun: *symphonic orchestra* sounds wrong to Americans.

Of or like a symphony and the large orchestra that plays one.

1. He writes *symphonic* music for films.
2. The band's new album has a *symphonic* sound.
3. A *symphonic* poem tells a story without words.

---

### symptomatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪmptəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *simp-tuh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** симптоматичный; с симптомами
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb, with *of*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun (the bigger problem)
**Preposition patterns:** [symptomatic of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#symptomatic-of)
**Form:** -ic from *symptom*
**Opposite:** *asymptomatic* (in medicine)

Symptomatic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Being a sign of a larger problem.** (симптоматичный, показательный)

1. The delays are *symptomatic* of a bigger management problem.
2. Empty storefronts are *symptomatic* of the whole town's decline.

**2. Showing the symptoms of a disease.** (с проявившимися симптомами)

3. Only *symptomatic* patients were tested at first.
4. She tested positive but never became *symptomatic*.

---

### synchronized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪŋkrənaɪzd/ &middot; *SING-kruh-nyzd*
**Русский:** синхронизированный, синхронный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *synchronized swimming*, *The clocks are synchronized*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *synchronize*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *-ized*; *synchronised* is British. The Olympic sport is officially *artistic swimming* now, but people still say *synchronized swimming*.

Made to happen at the same time or at the same speed.

1. The audio and the video aren't *synchronized*.
2. She swam on a *synchronized* swimming team in college.
3. All the traffic lights on Main are *synchronized*.

---

### syncopated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪŋkəpeɪtɪd/ &middot; *SING-kuh-pay-tid*
**Русский:** синкопированный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a syncopated beat*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *syncopate*

Having the musical accent on the off beats instead of the strong ones.

1. The song has a *syncopated* beat you can't sit still to.
2. Ragtime is built on *syncopated* rhythms.
3. The drums stay *syncopated* through the whole verse.

---

### synonymous

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈnɑːnɪməs/ &middot; *si-NAH-nuh-muhs*
**Русский:** синонимичный; неразрывно связанный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — almost always after a verb, with *with*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more synonymous*, *most synonymous*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [synonymous with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#synonymous-with)
**Form:** -ous from *synonym*

Synonymous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having the same meaning as another word.** (синонимичный)

1. *Big* and *large* are nearly *synonymous*.
2. The two terms are *synonymous* in everyday speech.

**2. So closely connected that one makes you think of the other.** (неразрывно связанный, ставший символом)

3. His name became *synonymous* with the city itself.
4. For my grandparents, Detroit was *synonymous* with cars.

---

### synthetic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪnˈθɛtɪk/ &middot; *sin-THET-ik*
**Русский:** синтетический, искусственный; неискренний
**Type:** material; also classifying
**Position:** both — *a synthetic fabric*, *The fibers are synthetic*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more synthetic* is possible about a sound or a feeling
**Form:** -ic from *synthesis*
**Opposite:** *natural*

Synthetic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made by a chemical process instead of coming from nature.** (синтетический, искусственный)

1. The jacket is filled with a *synthetic* material.
2. *Synthetic* rubber holds up better in the cold.
3. Most of the vitamins on that shelf are *synthetic*.

**2. Not genuine — made to look or sound real.** (искусственный, поддельный)

4. The dialogue felt *synthetic*.
5. There was something *synthetic* about his enthusiasm.

---

### Syrian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪriən/ &middot; *SIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** сирийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Syrian food*, *Her husband is Syrian*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Syria*
**Contrast:** Always capitalized, and the same word names the people: *a Syrian family*, *He's Syrian*, *two Syrians*.

Of or from Syria, its people or its culture.

1. We ate at a *Syrian* restaurant on Fifth Street.
2. She studies *Syrian* history.
3. My co-worker is *Syrian*.

---

### systematic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *sis-tuh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** систематический, методичный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a systematic search*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more systematic*, *most systematic*
**Form:** -ic from *system*
**Opposite:** *haphazard*, *random*
**Contrast:** *Systematic* means done step by step, on a plan; *systemic* means built into the whole system: *a systematic search* but *systemic corruption*.

Done carefully, in an organized, step-by-step way.

1. They made a *systematic* search of the building.
2. We need a more *systematic* way to track orders.
3. The report describes *systematic* abuse over many years.

---

### systemic

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈstɛmɪk/ &middot; *si-STEM-ik*
**Русский:** системный; общий (о всём организме)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *systemic failure*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *system*
**Contrast:** Don't mix it up with *systematic*, which means methodical. *Systemic* means running through the whole system: *systemic racism*, *a systemic infection*.

Systemic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Affecting a whole organization or society, not just one part of it.** (системный)

1. The audit found *systemic* problems, not one bad manager.
2. They call it a *systemic* failure of oversight.

**2. Affecting the entire body, in medicine.** (общий, системный (о болезни))

3. The infection became *systemic*.
4. *Systemic* drugs travel through the bloodstream.
